# Proximity Prize research progress

The universal rank formula now determines the operational MCA and list cutoffs on every supplied smooth prize code. Evaluating it at large dimensions can be impractical. The table records numerical values and bounds with their verification status. Dated entries preserve intermediate results. [Current reviewer guide](full-prize-review.md) · [November 21 integration assessment](integration-assessment-2026-11-21.md)

## First-order interpolation at multiples of 4096

For any injective prime-field domain of length $n=4096b$, $b>0$, the line-MCA test at 128-bit security passes at radii **1265/4096, 2170/4096, 2783/4096 and 3197/4096** in decreasing prize-rate order whenever $p\ge2^{171}n^2$. This includes the length-$2^{30}$ code over $65\cdot2^{239}+1$. At these same radii every center and finite interleaving width have list size at most **96741451n, 138925816n, 25849285n and 25632097n**, respectively, for every prime field admitting the domain. [Line-MCA bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FineGridProductionMcaIntervals.lean) · [Interleaved list bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FineGridWideLists.lean) · [Exact arithmetic](../../../scripts/probes/probe_first_order_fine_grid.py)

## How to use the general result

The first failing count can also be computed in Lean from explicit finite input data. At 128-bit tolerance the integer budget is the field order divided by $2^{128}$, rounded down. The correctness theorem applies to all four rates and every positive width. Actual numerical evaluation can require a prohibitive number of rank computations. [Executable boundary theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/RankExecutable.lean) · [Compiled examples](../../../ArkLib/Tests/ProximityPrize/GrandChallengeReview.lean)

At error count $e$, each failure test uses $L=\lfloor εq\rfloor+1$ witnesses and at least $t=n-e$ agreeing positions. For MCA, put $s=\max(t,k+1)$. Every actual bad label has a support of exactly $s$ sites. For $1\le B<q$, an affine reparametrization puts two of the $L$ labels at zero and one. The rank test uses $\binom{q-2}{B-1}$ scalar sets and $\binom{n}{s}^L$ support patterns per set. For $B=0$ one may fix the sole label at zero. The executable Lean evaluator generates these scalar sets and fixed-size supports directly, stops at first failure and uses the single-pair list test when its field-size condition holds. The entire interval beyond $n-k-1$ errors has the radius-one MCA error. [Fixed-size support theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ExactSizeMcaSupport.lean) For lists, choose $L$ supports of exactly $t$ sites, giving $\binom{n}{t}^{L}$ patterns. A positive Vandermonde inclusion–exclusion sum detects distinct compatible codewords. It has $2^{\binom{L}{2}}$ terms per pattern. At 128-bit security and $q<2^{256}$, one base dimension and at most $\binom{L}{2}$ pairwise comparisons per pattern suffice. [MCA criterion](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SimultaneousSupportRank.lean) · [List criterion](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ListSupportRank.lean) · [Pairwise reduction](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ListSupportForcedPair.lean)

The first failing count $e>0$ gives safe radii $[0,e/n)$, which have a supremum and no largest member. Failure at zero gives an empty safe set. If every count passes, one is safe. A small prime-field program cross-checks both rank criteria against exhaustive computation on three length-three codes over fields of sizes three and five, including one width-two list. Numeric production cutoffs beyond the established windows can require further bounds or tractable certificates. [Endpoint proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/RankBoundary.lean) · [Small-field cross-check](../../../scripts/probes/probe_supplied_domain_rank_thresholds.py)

## An exact list size on every supplied evaluation domain

For an injective Reed–Solomon evaluation domain of length $n$, dimension $1\le k\le n$, and any positive interleaving width, the worst list at $n-k$ errors has exactly $\binom nk$ codewords. This is the last grid point before the minimum distance. Distinct words cannot share a set of $k$ agreements with the center. The all-subsets interpolation construction attains the bound on the supplied domain.

Write $C=\binom nk$ and let $B$ be an integer list budget below the field size. If $\lfloor C/(k+1)\rfloor\le B<C$, the first failing error count is $n-k$. If $C\le B<|F|$, the first failing count is $n-k+1$. These statements classify every real radius and specialize directly to $B=\lfloor |F|/2^{128}\rfloor$. In particular, $|F|\ge 2^{128+n}$ implies the second formula for every dimension. Below this interval the universal rank test still determines the cutoff. A short numerical evaluation may require further estimates. [Exact list size and budget windows](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ListInterpolationBoundary.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · An exact MCA threshold from Reed–Solomon shortening

This entry uses downstream source revision `a15abf509f39bf87781119462b81d9c5e08a6848`. The following is a mathematical derivation. Kernel verification will be recorded separately.

Let $n=2^{30}$, $k=n/16$ and $Q=2^{128}$. Let $p$ be prime with $n$ dividing $p-1$. Set $B=⌊p/Q⌋$, $N=2⌊B/2⌋$ and $s=N-n$. Suppose $0≤s≤⌊n/14⌋$. On a multiplicative subgroup of size $n$, the first bad Hamming error count at tolerance $1/Q$ is exactly $N/2$. The supremum of the good real radii is $N/(2n)$. The largest good integer error count is $N/2-1$.

**Good side.** Extend the evaluation domain by $s$ fresh field elements. Let $Z$ be the monic product of their linear factors. Multiply each received row by $Z$ on the old domain and set it to zero on the fresh coordinates. If a scalar has a bad witness on $S$ at $e=N/2-1$ errors, multiplying the witness polynomial by $Z$ gives a witness of degree below $k+s$ on $S$ together with all fresh coordinates. Any two polynomials jointly explaining those rows there vanish at every fresh point. Dividing them by $Z$ and cancelling its nonzero values on $S$ would jointly explain the original witness. Thus each bad scalar stays bad under a single extension of the received rows, independent of the scalar. The longer code has even length $N$, agreement at least $N/2+1$, and $8(k+s)≤N$ because $7s≤n/2$. The theorem HalfPredecessorRateEighthFullWiring.halfPredecessor_badScalar_filter_card_le_length then gives at most $N≤B$ bad scalars for every original pair.

**Bad side.** At $e=N/2$, PackingBudgetFirstJump.overlap_epsMCA_lower_bound_of_supply gives at least $2e+2=N+2>B$ bad scalars. Its hypotheses hold since $n≤2e$, $e+k+1≤n$, and $2(2e-n+2)≤p-n$. Monotonicity and constancy on each Hamming step give the threshold. The case $s=0$ recovers the known half pin. Positive $s$ gives a field window with thresholds strictly above one half. The window excludes the second repository prime with budget $2n$. The general Paley estimate and the remaining prize parameters are open here.

## 2026-11-21 · Scalar list budgets and a rate-half exact slice

The closed-ball list test has a dimension-free reduction. Write $q=|F|$ and $B=\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor$. For any linear code over $F$, any positive interleaving arity, and any one radius, the scalar and interleaved tests $\Lambda\le B$ are equivalent whenever $\binom{B+1}{2}<q$. This includes every $q\le 2^{256}$. Indeed, $B\le 2^{128}$, so for $B\ge1$ one has $B(B+1)/2<B2^{128}\le q$. For any $B+1$ distinct interleaved words in one ball, pick a distinguishing coordinate for each pair. The row weights identifying the symbols there form a proper hyperplane with $q^{m-1}$ points. Some row weighting avoids all of these hyperplanes and projects the entire list and its center into a scalar ball of the same radius. Every original agreement survives. Embedding a scalar list into one row proves the converse. This reduces the outstanding list question to scalar RS and leaves its worst-case radius to be determined.

A second Lean theorem removes the field-size restriction whenever the length or the maximum error count is at most $2^{128}$. At budget $B<q$, preserving all mismatches of $B$ designated candidates among $B+1$ uses at most $BE$ proper hyperplanes, where $E$ is the error count. If $BE\le q$, one projection avoids all of them. The scalar budget implies that the values of a listed word on its agreement set determine it, since otherwise the zero scalar ball contains the $q$ multiples of a nonzero difference row. This separates the unprotected candidate as well. Consequently the general list-decoding challenge through length $2^{128}$ reduces to determining the scalar threshold on the supplied field and domain, at all widths and every field size. [Statement and proof](interleaved-list-projection.md#a-width-independent-threshold-over-every-finite-field)

A direct construction gives an exact slice with large block length. Let $n=Ms$, $k=bs$, $1\le b<M$, and let $D$ be a cyclic subgroup of order $n$. Its $s$-power map has $M$ fibers of size $s$. Set $H(X)$ to the monic polynomial vanishing on $s-1$ points of one reserved fiber. Use the single received word $H(X)X^k$. For each $b$-subset $T$ of the other $M-1$ fiber values use the polynomial
$$f_T(X)=H(X)\left[X^k-\prod_{a\in T}(X^s-a)\right].$$
The two leading terms cancel, leaving degree at most $(s-1)+(b-1)s=k-1$. Its agreement set consists exactly of the $s-1$ reserved roots and the $bs$ points in the chosen fibers. Distinct choices have different agreement sets, hence give distinct codewords. This proves a list lower bound of $\binom{M-1}{b}$ at $k+s-1$ agreements, independently of the field characteristic.

For $M=4,b=2$, the three candidates have distance $s+1$ from the center. At radius $s$, the minimum distance $2s+1$ forces every list to have size at most one, also after interleaving. Thus for a budget of two the last good integer radius is $s$, and the supremum of the good real radii is $(s+1)/(4s)$. At $n=2^{30}$ the prime $680564733906249808969588989158290882561$ has this budget. The exact radius pair is $268435456,268435457$. The repository's first sponsor field has a different budget. Run `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_quarter_coset_lists.py` to verify the prime, subgroup order, quotient polynomials and finite tests. The existing three-coset syzygy is described in `_SW1_F1_UniformSylvesterRefuted.lean`.

## 2026-11-21 · Subfield obstruction to the workbench cancellation conjecture

The quantifier over all finite fields in `ShawFlatnessConjecture` in `PROXIMITY_PRIZE_WORKBENCH.lean` admits the following counterexample. For an odd prime $p$, take $F=\mathbb F_{p^2}$, $D=\mathbb F_p^\times$, and $\psi(x)=\exp(2\pi i\operatorname{Tr}(x)/p)$. The character is primitive as an additive character of a field. Choose $b\ne 0$ of trace zero. Then every summand in $\eta_b$ equals one, and $|D|^2\le p^2$. The proposed constant would have to satisfy $C^2\ge (p-1)/\log_2(p^2/(p-1))$ for every odd prime.

Even a dyadic subgroup which generates the whole field can exhibit linear-size sums. Put $n=2m$ with $m$ a power of two. Choose a prime $p\equiv3m+1\pmod{4m}$ of size polynomial in $m$ by Linnik's theorem. For $m\ge4$, $D=\mu_{2m}\subset\mathbb F_{p^2}$ generates the field and has the disjoint parts $A=\mu_m\subset\mathbb F_p$ and $uA$ with $u\notin\mathbb F_p$. In the basis $(1,u)$ let the character depend on the second coordinate. Averaging its dilations by nonzero prime-field scalars gives the exact mean $m-m/(p-1)$ by additive orthogonality. The maximum absolute period is at least this mean, whereas $\log_2(p^2/n)=O(\log n)$. The finite certificate in `scripts/probes/probe_shaw_generating_quadratic.py` checks a length-$2^{30}$ example and the bound $C>1442$. The prime-field cancellation problem survives. Bourgain and Glibichuk's Theorem 5 gives the relevant quantitative intersection hypothesis for extension fields. [Paper](https://www.math.ias.edu/files/avi/Bourgain_Glibichuk.pdf)

The reference PDFs in `research/` include [Kambiré](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09724), [Jeronimo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05870), [Harsha, Kumar and Saptharishi](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08610), and [Chiesa, Weissenberg, Yogev and Zheng](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15128). Jeronimo gives a direct prime-field bound polynomial in $n$ at fixed capacity slack. An unspecified polynomial exponent does not certify the literal integer budget $\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor$ for a given production code.

## 2026-11-21 · Fourth moment at the two named prize primes

I am running the exact G139 half-window injectivity test at the primes in `_PrizeShapePrimeP30.lean` and `_PrizeShapePrimeP30Second.lean`. The existing full-size G139 arithmetic artifact uses a third prime ending in 196377235457. Its result cannot be transferred between these fields. The target here is the production fourth moment. The higher even moments entering the uniform Paley bound are separate obligations. Results will be appended after the exhaustive checks finish.

### Concrete shortening prime and exact error counts

For $p=7^5 2^{144}+1=374808624551704183139790817008401011831012851713$, the Lucas witness is 3 and the order-$2^{30}$ generator is 287207082126316176040739287625456263882736992611. At the original tolerance the budget is 1,101,463,552. The predecessor is good at 550,731,775 errors. At 550,731,776 errors the packing supplies 1,101,463,554 scalars. The supremum is $16807/32768$. These numbers use the prime displayed here.

More generally, at any integer $e$ with $n≤2e$, use the extended length $N=2e+2$. Provided $e+k+1≤n$, $e+1≥1699$, $8(k+N-n)≤N$, $N≤p$, and $2(2e-n+2)≤p-n$, the same two proofs give the exact worst-case error probability $(2e+2)/p$ at radius $e/n$. This describes an interval of errors even when the field budget crosses the target outside that interval.

### Paley sharp-constant counterexamples

For the order-64 subgroup at $p=16,890,817$, one multiplicative coset of frequencies exceeds the entire proposed unit Gaussian moment budget at each depth 9 through 20, including the ceiling of the natural logarithm of the prime, 17. The depth-2 through depth-5 bounds all pass. Three further examples near the production value of the log field size divided by the log subgroup size fail the proposed unit bound already at depth two. Run `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_paley_minimal_valuation_witness.py` for the exact checks. The [disproof log](../../../docs/research/proximity-gap/DISPROOF_LOG.md) gives the derivation. The unspecified-constant bound remains open.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact list of the geometric-sum center

Let $n=Mh$, $M\ge3$, $h+1>M$, and let a field contain all $n$ distinct $n$th roots of unity. For the code on $\mu_n$ of dimension $k=(M-2)h$, set $w=1+X+\cdots+X^{(M-1)h-1}$. Its **entire** list at $h+1$ errors has exactly $M-1$ words. At most $h$ errors gives an empty list for this center.

To see this, for a candidate polynomial $P$ of degree below $k$ let $D$ be the monic vanishing polynomial of its error locations. The polynomial $D(w-P)$ vanishes on the domain, is monic, and has degree at most $Mh$. Consequently $D(w-P)=X^{Mh}-1$ and $\deg D=h+1$. The leading $h$ coefficients of $w-P$ are all one. Comparing the top $h$ coefficients in the product forces $D=X^{h+1}-X^h+aX+b$. Its $h+1$ distinct roots lie on $M$ fibres of $x\mapsto x^h$, so two lie on a common fibre with value $t$. There $D(x)=(t+a)x+b-t$. Two roots force $a=-t$, $b=t$, giving $D=(X^h-t)(X-1)$. Distinctness forces $t\ne1$. Each of the $M-1$ choices $t\in\mu_M\setminus\{1\}$ gives a distinct error set and the polynomial $P=w-(X^n-1)/D$ has degree below $k$: polynomial division, or comparison of leading coefficients in $(X^n-1)/D$, cancels the top $h$ coefficients. At every point in that error set the complementary vanisher is nonzero.

For $M=4$, the previously constructed quarter-coset center $X^{2h}(X^h-1)/(X-1)$ differs from $w$ by $(X^{2h}-1)/(X-1)$, which is a codeword. Thus the center gives exactly three candidates at the first nonunique radius even at $n=2^{30}$. A fourth candidate at the same radius would require a different code coset. The global maximum for budget three still needs proof. `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_geometric_word_exact_list.py` independently enumerates every possible agreement-set vanisher at $(p,n,k)=(13,12,4),(17,16,8),(97,16,8),(31,15,5)$ and returns counts $2,3,3,2$. The general argument above is a paper proof.

### Independent verification of the first-field ceiling below one third

The first-field rate-half construction gives at least 1,073,741,826 bad scalars at 357,913,941 errors. This improves the existing Lean ceiling by one Hamming step. Its generator, six exceptional evaluations, and all three fractional linear maps are checked by `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_rate_half_mobius_seams.py`. An independent run returned 66, 258, and 1,026 explicit witness labels at subgroup lengths 64, 256, and 1,024 in the same prime field. The production conclusion uses the coordinate-count argument in [the full proof](../../../docs/research/proximity-gap/rate-half-mobius-seams.md).

Theorem 2 of Corvaja and Zannier supplies an independent production-size coordinate count. Take the smooth projective curve $\mathbb P^1$ over the first prime field and functions $u=X^n$, $v=R(X)^n$, where $R=f_j^{-1}f_i$ is one of the relative maps. Its four nonzero entries and nonzero determinant put the zero and pole of $R$ at distinct finite nonzero points. Thus $u,v$ have disjoint divisor supports and are multiplicatively independent modulo constants. Both have degree $n$ and nonzero differential since $p\nmid n$ and $R$ has degree one. The set of zeros and poles has four elements, giving $\chi=2$. Every $x\in\mu_n$ with $R(x)\in\mu_n$ contributes at least one to the theorem’s multiplicity sum. Its upper bound is $\max((108n^2)^{1/3},12n^2/p)$. At $n=2^{30}$ the second term is below one and the first has integer part 4,993,531. Removing both endpoints for each of three unordered pairs and the 27 exceptional positions of the proof leaves at least 1,043,780,611 available positions. The construction needs 536,870,910. This checks the finite-characteristic hypotheses directly against page 1929 of [the paper](https://doi.org/10.4171/jems/409).

**The radius is optimal for witnesses covered by three decoded affine lines.** At an error count $e$, let a line have $z$ joint positions. Each of its selected bad scalars needs at least $\max(1,n-e-z)$ agreements outside those positions, and each outside position can serve at most one scalar on that line. Thus the line has at most $e+1$ selected scalars. This follows from $(n-z)/(n-e-z)=1+e/(n-e-z)$ when $z<n-e$, and from $n-z\le e$ otherwise. At $e=357913940$, three lines can supply at most $3(e+1)=1073741823$, below the overbudget requirement of 1,073,741,825. This also excludes improvements from multi-fresh agreement on those same three lines. A smaller ceiling would require witnesses outside their union. The exact first-field rate-half threshold remains open.

The common-locator degree $c=k-4$ is also maximal within the construction with three nonconstant edge-direction maps and cores of size $s=2(n-1)/3$. Write $d=k-1-c$ for the remaining edge degree and $b_i$ for the nodes belonging to precisely the two cores on edge $i$. Counting core multiplicities gives $\sum b_i\ge 3s-n-2c=2d$. The edge-difference vector vanishes at all its $b_i$ nodes after the common locator is removed. A nonconstant residual direction requires $b_i\le d-1$. Consequently $2d\le3(d-1)$ and $d\ge3$. The construction realizes $d=3$ with two nodes per edge.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact fourth moment at both named primes

On each field the ordered scan of $1\le r\le 536870912$ produced 536870912 distinct low 64-bit limbs of the least nonnegative representatives of $(g^r-1)^{2^{30}}\bmod p$. Distinct limbs certify distinct residues exactly. The scan checked the generator order and the endpoint power of each of six disjoint consecutive shards. At the positive control $(n,p,g)=(64,17318209,9133071)$ it found 29 distinct images on 32 inputs, with collisions $(4,11),(2,13),(23,30)$. The full finite evaluation was performed by C++/GMP. The general implication from half-window injectivity to the additive relation classification is in Lean. A kernel replay of the 536870912 evaluations was not performed.

It follows that the exact ordered additive energy in both fields is $E_2=3n(n-1)=3458764510599315456$. In detail, an equality $a+b=c+d$ with $a\ne c$ writes $c(a/c-1)=b(d/b-1)$. Raising to $n$ and using half-window injectivity identifies the exponents up to sign, since inversion preserves the signature. Equal ratios give $c=b$ and $a=d$. Inverse ratios give $a=-b$ and $c=-d$. The case $a=c$ gives $b=d$. The two permutations and the zero-sum family each have $n^2$ members, each pair overlaps in $n$ members, and their triple intersection is empty in odd characteristic. Character orthogonality gives the DC-subtracted fourth moment $E_2-n^4/p$ separately for each prime. The next moment needs control of sums of three subgroup elements.

Reproduce with a C++17 compiler and GMP. Use a fresh directory name for each execution. On Homebrew, add `-I"$(brew --prefix gmp)/include" -L"$(brew --prefix gmp)/lib"` to the compile command.

```sh
c++ -O3 -std=c++17 scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_phi.cpp -lgmpxx -lgmp -o /tmp/sponsor-phi
/tmp/sponsor-phi positive-control /tmp/sponsor-phi-control
/tmp/sponsor-phi first /tmp/sponsor-phi-first 6
/tmp/sponsor-phi second /tmp/sponsor-phi-second 6
```

The first prime ends in 120141565953, the second in 841924915201. ## 2026-11-21 · Uniform upper bounds for the actual periods at both prize primes

For $n=2^{30}$, every nonzero frequency satisfies $|\eta_b|<1059557319$ at the first prize prime and $|\eta_b|<1059633924$ at the second. These improve the trivial ceiling $n=1073741824$ by more than fourteen million at each prime. The bound keeps linear dependence on $n$, well above the conjectured square-root scale.

**Dyadic determinant bound.** Let $p$ be an odd prime, $H\subseteq\mathbb F_p^\times$ a subgroup of order $n$, and $d$ a power of two with $2d\mid n$. Then for every $b\ne0$,

$$
\frac{|\sum_{x\in H}\exp(2\pi i bx/p)|}{n}
\le 1-\frac{8}{d}(2p)^{-2/d}.
$$

Choose $h\in H$ of order $2d$. For each coset of $\langle h\rangle$, absorb its representative into $b$ and let $v_i$ be the representative in $\{1,\ldots,p-1\}$ of $bh^i$ for $0\le i<d$. The opposite $d$ elements give conjugate phases. It is enough to bound both signs of the mean of the $d$ real cosines.

For the positive sign take $a_i$ to be the least absolute residue of $v_i$ and set $m=p$. For the negative sign take $a_i=2v_i-p$ and set $m=2p$. In either case the signed cosine is $\cos(2\pi a_i/m)$ and $|a_i|\le m/2$. Form the $d$-by-$d$ integer matrix of multiplication by $A(X)=\sum_{i<d}a_i X^i$ modulo $X^d+1$ in the power basis. Its determinant is nonzero because $X^d+1$ is irreducible over the rationals and $A$ has degree below $d$ and is nonzero. Its columns are the signed cyclic shifts of the coefficient vector.

Modulo $p$, each successive shift is $h^{-1}$ times the preceding one, including the wraparound entry because $h^d=-1$. For the negative sign all entries are also odd, so the columns agree modulo $2$. For each column after the first, subtract a multiple of the first chosen to match its proportionality constant modulo $p$, and chosen odd when $m=2p$. The resulting $d-1$ columns are divisible by $m$. Thus $m^{d-1}\le|\det|$. Hadamard's inequality bounds $|\det|\le(\sum_i a_i^2)^{d/2}$.

The chord bound $1-\cos(2\pi u)=2\sin^2(\pi u)\ge8u^2$ for $|u|\le1/2$ now gives a deficit of at least $8m^{-2/d}/d$ in the signed mean. Taking the weaker of $m=p,2p$ and averaging the cosets proves the displayed inequality. At $d=256$ the guaranteed saving is $2^{25}/(2p)^{1/128}$. Exact integer powers put this quantity in $(14184505,14184506)$ and $(14107900,14107901)$ respectively. The probe also enumerates both sign branches for all frequencies at $p=17,97$ and $d=2,4,8$. Banks and Shparlinski give related estimates using squared least residues and the geometry of numbers. [Exact probe](../../../scripts/probes/probe_paley_dyadic_period_ceiling.py) · [Banks and Shparlinski, arXiv:1310.5669](https://arxiv.org/abs/1310.5669)

## 2026-11-21 · The order-eight prescribed-product list at the rate-half MCA predecessor

Al Kindi's Theorem 1.1 combines a prescribed-product quotient list with common polynomial roots. It gives at least $\binom{M-1}{b+1}/M$ list members at $k+2s-1$ agreements for $n=Ms$, $k=bs$, $s\ge2$ and $1\le b\le M-2$. The reserved-fiber construction above uses only monicity, and its count can be larger when the budget is small. For production-scale list ceilings the extra $s$ agreements in the published theorem matter. [Al Kindi, ePrint 2026/2245](https://eprint.iacr.org/2026/2245)

At the first repository prime, put $M=8$, $s=n/8$, $k=4s$. Consider Al Kindi's seven quotient heads for five-subsets of $\mu_8$ sharing one product. Each complement is a triple. Rotation reduces every product class to triples with exponent sum zero modulo eight. Test the following attempted MCA construction. At each coordinate the two-component polynomial heads take the form $A(x)+V(x)Q_i(x^s)$, with at most $s$ common zeros of the vector multiplier $V$. The received pair can be chosen freely. A scalar supplied by a head extends its own joint core by one fresh coordinate. If this construction supplies at least $n$ distinct scalar labels from four or more active heads, at least one of those heads has joint core size at most $2n/3$. This excludes an over-budget witness of this form at $e=(n-1)/3-1$ errors, where every active core would need at least $n-e-1=(2n+1)/3$ points. For at most three heads the direct slot count is $3(e+1)=n-1$. This restriction concerns one-fresh witnesses from one prescribed-product class. Other families and witnesses needing several fresh agreements are open.

Here is a finite certificate for the four-to-seven-head claim. At quotient node $y$, partition the active heads by their actual $Q_i(y)$ in the prime field and let $d_y$ be the number of classes. Give the heads nonnegative weights $w_i$ with total $W>0$. Give each available label weight $l\ge0$, and charge $c\ge0$ per zero of $V$. The sum of agreement weights plus label weights less root charges is bounded pointwise on this fiber by
$$U_y=\max\{\max_G \textstyle\sum_{i\in G} w_i + l\,\mathbf1_{d_y>1},\quad l d_y,\quad W-c\}.$$
Indeed the collinear heads give at most one finite new direction when the received pair equals one of their values. An unexplained received value yields no joint agreements and at most one direction per distinct value. At a root, simultaneous agreement uses up a root charge. Count a direction only at a point where the scalar equality extends a genuine vector discrepancy. Since each fiber has size $s$ and there are at most $s$ roots, a supply of at least $8s$ labels bounds the minimum joint core by $s(\sum_y U_y-8l+c)/W$. The linked integer verifier exhibits weights for every one of the 64 active subsets, in every case giving a ratio at most $16/3$. It obtains the actual equality classes by modular arithmetic. The proof uses the certificate inequalities directly. [Verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_rate_half_eight_fiber_head_bound.py)

## 2026-11-21 · Two-line packing gives a rate-sixteenth MCA floor above 60%

**Status.** This is a written proof for two-row MCA in the repository's witness-set definition. The numerical comparisons use exact rationals. Kernel verification of the generalized incidence argument is pending.

Let $n$ be the length of an injective Reed–Solomon evaluation domain in a finite field, $1\le k\le n/16$ the dimension, $d=k-1$, $e$ the integer error allowance and $t=n-e$ the agreement threshold. For $n-k\le2e\le n-2$, the maximum number of bad scalars over all received row pairs is **exactly $2e+2$**. The upper bound extends to $n-k\le2e$ and $t\ge2n/5$ for $n\ge60{,}000$. At $n=2^{30}$ it holds through $E=647{,}626{,}307$ errors, where $t\ge396851n/10^6$.

**Incidence facts.** Suppose there are $2e+3$ bad scalars. For each, select a witnessing polynomial and its full agreement set. On a line of selected points the polynomial has the form $A+\gamma B$. The line's core consists of the $z$ coordinates where the received rows equal $(A,B)$. Each of its $L$ points has at least $\max(1,t-z)$ fresh agreements, disjoint across the line. The extra 1 follows from badness on the witnessing subset. Hence $z+L\max(1,t-z)\le n$ and $L\le e+1$. A point off the line agrees on at most $d$ core positions. Distinct determined cores intersect in at most $d$ positions, and a noncollinear triple has at most $d$ common agreements, by polynomial root counting. A collinear triple has exactly its line's core as common agreement set. [Half-predecessor incidence assembly](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_HalfPredecessorIncidenceAssembly.lean) · [Core intersection lemma](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_HalfPredecessorRateEighthFullWiring.lean)

For a subfamily of $M$ points let $W$ be the average common agreement count of an unordered triple. If the largest line has $L_{\max}$ points, every pair extends to at most $L_{\max}-2$ collinear triples. The collinear fraction is thus at most $(L_{\max}-2)/(M-2)$. Coordinate multiplicities have mean at least $Mt/n$. For $f(x)=x(x-1)(x-2)$, $v\ge2$ and $x\ge0$ one has $f(x)-f(v)-f'(v)(x-v)=(x-v)^2(x+2v-3)\ge0$ and $f'(v)>0$. Summing the tangent bound and double counting gives $W\ge nf(v)/f(M)$ for $2\le v\le Mt/n$.

**Below half.** Take $M=2e+3$ and $2e\le n-2$. If $2z>2n-3t+4d$, every triple outside the line has at least $3(t-d)-2(n-z)>d$ common fresh agreements. All outside points therefore lie on a single secant, and two lines cover at most $2e+2$ points. Hence $z\le n/4+2d\le3n/8-2$. Five on-line points would require $5t-4z>n$ coordinates. Consequently $L_{\max}\le4$ and $W\le d+n/(M-2)<n/16+1/15$, using $M-2>15n/16$. At $v=M/2$ the lower bound gives $W\ge n(M-4)/(8(M-1))>n/8-2/5$. This contradicts the upper bound for every $n\ge16$.

For equality, take two cores $A,B$ of size $t-1$ covering the domain, with intersection $I$ of size $n-2e-2$. Let $Z$ be the monic polynomial with simple roots $I$. Assign rows $(0,0)$ on $A$ and $(XZ,Z)$ on $B$. They agree on $I$, and the displayed polynomials have degree below $k$. For each $x\in A\mathbin{\triangle}B$, scalar $-x$ gives a witness on the opposite core together with $x$. The $t-1\ge k$ core positions force any explaining pair to equal the core pair, which fails at $x$ since $Z(x)\ne0$. This supplies $2e+2$ distinct scalars.

**Beyond half.** Fix $s>0$ with $2s<3t-n-3d$, and call a determined line large if $z\ge t-s$. Given two large lines, an outside point agrees at most $2d$ times inside their union, and at most $n-2(t-s)+d$ times outside it. Their sum is below $t$. Two lines then cover all points, contradicting their occupancy bounds. Otherwise remove the unique large line if one exists, leaving $M\ge e+2$ points. Each line determined by the remaining points has $t-z>s$ and satisfies $L-1\le e/(t-z)<e/s$. Its collinear triple fraction is therefore globally below $1/s$, since $M-2\ge e$. Collinear triples weigh at most $t$, and the others at most $d$. For $a\le t/n$ with $aM\ge2$, the resulting bounds are
$$
na^3-3a^2n/M\ \le\ nf(aM)/M^3\ \le\ W\ \le\ d+t/s .
$$
The two-core step is the repository's [quantitative covering argument](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_HalfPredecessorRateQuarterComplementaryCores.lean).

Here $e\ge15n/32$ and $t\le17n/32$. Taking $a=2/5$, $s=n/160$ gives $2s=n/80<3t-n-3d$ because $d\le n/16-1$. The upper bound is at most $n/16+84$ and the lower exceeds $(2/5)^3n-2$, which contradict each other for $n\ge60{,}000$. At $n=2^{30}$ use $a=396851/10^6$, $s=n/660$. The exact inequalities $660(3a-1-3/16)>2$ and $n(a^3-1/16)>373$ apply. Then $W<n/16+350$ and $W>na^3-2$, an impossibility. More generally, for every fixed $a>(1/16)^{1/3}$ in this band one can take $s$ proportional to $n$ with $2s/n<3a-1-3/16$, giving the cap for all sufficiently large lengths.

**Production consequences.** Let $n=2^{30}$, $k=n/16$, $Q=2^{128}$ and $B=\lfloor p/Q\rfloor$. The second repository prime has $B=2n$. Goodness at $E=647{,}626{,}307$ gives $\delta^*\ge647626308/1073741824$ by Hamming-step constancy. For any prime $p\equiv1\bmod n$ with $n\le B\le1{,}295{,}252{,}617=2E+3$, the first bad error count on the order-$n$ multiplicative domain equals $e_*=\lfloor B/2\rfloor$. At $e_*-1$ there are at most $2e_*\le B$ bad scalars. At $e_*$ the overlap construction supplies $2e_*+2>B$; its hypotheses $n\le2e_*$, $e_*+k+1\le n$ and $2(2e_*-n+2)\le p-n$ hold throughout this window. Thus the supremum is $e_*/n$. The second-prime floor has no matching upper endpoint from this proof. The full list-decoding and prime-field Paley conjectures remain open. [Overlap packing lower bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_PackingBudgetFirstJump.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · Three-list bound for shifted low-degree centers at rate one half

Let $n=4h$ be a power of two with $h$ even, let $p\equiv1\pmod n$ be prime, and evaluate polynomials of degree below $2h$ on $\mu_n\subset\mathbb F_p$. Suppose $p^{n/8-1}>(2h+2)^{n/2}$. If a received word is represented on the domain by $w(x)=A(x)/x^t$ with $0\le t\le h$ and $\deg A\le3h-1$, its list at $h+1$ errors has at most **three** words. This includes every polynomial center of degree at most $3h-1$. The preceding geometric-sum example attains three. At $n=2^{30}$ the numeric hypothesis holds for every $p\ge 2^{128}$, since $2h+2<2^{30}$ and $128(n/8-1)>30n/2$.

**Error moments.** Cancel common powers of $X$ from the representation first, so $A(0)\ne0$ if $t>0$. A center equal to a codeword has only itself at this radius for $h>0$. For each other candidate $P$, the nonzero residual $A-X^tP$ has at most $3h-1$ roots, while agreement supplies at least $3h-1$. Hence it has exactly that degree and exactly those roots, and the error set $E_P$ has size $h+1$. Its monic vanisher $L_P$ satisfies $L_P(A-X^tP)=c_P(X^n-1)$ with $c_P\ne0$. For $t<h$, the leading $h-t$ coefficients of $A-X^tP$ are independent of $P$, so its leading coefficient $c_P$ is fixed and polynomial division shows the top $h-t$ coefficients of $L_P$ are fixed. For $t>0$, the identity modulo $X^t$ gives $L_P/c_P\equiv -1/A\pmod{X^t}$. In particular the first $t$ coefficients of $L_P/L_P(0)$ are fixed. Newton's identities therefore make $\sum_{x\in E_P}x^r$ independent of $P$ for every nonzero integer $-t<r<h-t$, using reciprocal roots for $r<0$.

**Lifting moment equalities.** For any two candidates write $f_j=1_{E_P}(g^j)-1_{E_{P'}}(g^j)$ with $g$ a generator of $\mu_n$, and $F(T)=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1} f_jT^j\in\mathbb Z[T]$. The moment equalities imply $F(g^r)=0$ modulo $p$ for every odd integer in that interval. These are at least $n/8-1$ distinct primitive $n$th roots. For a complex primitive $n$th root $\zeta$, the integer norm of $F(\zeta)$ is the determinant of multiplication by $F$ on $\mathbb Z[T]/(\Phi_n)$. Modulo $p$, evaluation at the distinct primitive roots diagonalizes this matrix. Its rank deficiency therefore forces the integer determinant to be divisible by $p^{n/8-1}$ (Smith normal form). Every complex conjugate has magnitude at most $\sum |f_j|\le 2h+2$, and there are $n/2$ conjugates. The numeric hypothesis forces norm zero. Since $\Phi_n=T^{n/2}+1$, reduction of $F$ gives $f_j=f_{j+n/2}$ for every $0\le j<n/2$.

**Excluding a fourth word.** This equality says the indicators of every two error sets have the same imbalance on each opposite pair $\{x,-x\}$. Since each error set has odd size $h+1$, some pair has nonzero imbalance in the first one. The same point of that pair belongs to every error set. For distinct candidates the minimum distance $2h+1$ gives $|E_P\cap E_{P'}|\le 2(h+1)-(2h+1)=1$. Four error sets would thus leave four disjoint $h$-sets after deletion of their shared point, in a space of size $4h-1$. This proves the cap. `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_geometric_word_exact_list.py` checks all $\binom{16}{5}$ supports at each $t=0,1,2,3,4$ over $p=2013265921$, and verifies the production exponent comparison. The proof applies to the stated class of centers. A bound for arbitrary centers or a four-word witness is still required to pin the global budget-three threshold.

**General center test.** For any received word, set $M=X^n-1$ and use its degree-below-$n$ interpolant. A nonzero $Q$ of degree $\le h$ exists with $A=Qw\bmod M$ of degree $<3h$, by $h$ homogeneous linear constraints on $h+1$ coefficients. For every candidate $i$ with error locator $L_i$, $c_i=L_i(A-QP_i)/M$ is a constant. At most one vanishes. For distinct candidates, $A_{ij}=(P_i-P_j)L_iL_j/M$ is a nonzero polynomial of degree at most one. Both divisions are exact by evaluation on all of $\mu_n$. Thus $c_iL_j-c_jL_i=-Q A_{ij}$. Picking $j$ with $c_j\ne0$ shows all locators lie in $\operatorname{span}\{L_j,Q,XQ\}$. A general $Q$ can have exponents throughout $[0,h]$; the preceding moment argument does not impose its dense-window conclusion on arbitrary $Q$.

A stronger norm estimate covers **every denominator of degree at most $h/2$** at $n=2^{30}$, $p\ge2^{128}$. Suppose $w=A/Q$ with $\deg A\le3h-1$, $\deg Q\le h/2$, and $Q$ nonzero everywhere on $\mu_n$. The same factorization of $L_P(A-QP)$ supplies fixed leading locator coefficients, hence equal positive power sums for $1\le r<h-\deg Q$. This gives at least $h/4$ odd frequencies. Complex Fourier Parseval gives $\sum_{j\text{ odd}}|F(\zeta^j)|^2\le n\sum_u f_u^2\le 2n(h+1)$. The primitive embeddings number $n/2$, so AM–GM gives $|\mathrm{Norm}\,F(\zeta)|\le [4(h+1)]^{n/4}$. At $h=2^{28}$, $[4(h+1)]^4<2^{128}\le p$, hence $p^{h/4}>[4(h+1)]^{n/4}$. The same contradiction excludes four close codewords. The calculation uses only Parseval for a signed indicator vector. Sharpness for dense denominators above this degree remains open.

**Field-uniform consequence of the dyadic determinant bound.** For dyadic $n\ge4\log_2(2p)$, every nontrivial period satisfies $|\eta_b|/n\le1-2/\log_2(2p)$. Choose $d$ as the smallest power of two at least $\log_2(2p)$, so $2d\mid n$ and $x=\log_2(2p)/d\in[1/2,1]$. Concavity of $\log_2$ between 1 and 2 yields $\log_2(2x)\ge2x-1$, equivalently $8x2^{-2x}\ge2$. Substitution in the proved deficit $8(2p)^{-2/d}/d$ gives the stated constant.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact list-budget-three boundary for dimension two

For $n=4h\ge4$ distinct evaluation points and dimension $k=2$, the largest possible minimum agreement among four distinct codewords with a common center is exactly $h+1$. This also holds at any positive interleaving width. To bound the agreement, count the number $m_x$ of the four words matching the center at each point. Each of the six pairs of distinct words agrees at most once, since some row difference is a nonzero affine polynomial. The inequality $m_x\le1+\binom{m_x}{2}$ therefore gives $\sum m_x\le n+6$, so at least one of the four has agreement at most $h+1$.

For equality, choose four distinct evaluation points $a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3$, with cyclic indices. Use the four affine polynomials $P_i(X)=(a_i+a_{i+1})X-a_i a_{i+1}$. These secants are distinct because $X^2-P_i$ has exactly the root pair $\{a_i,a_{i+1}\}$. Give the center value $a_j^2$ at each of the four points. Partition the remaining $4(h-1)$ points into four sets of $h-1$, giving the center value $P_i(x)$ on set $i$. Every polynomial has at least $h+1$ agreements. Repeating the same word in each row gives the interleaved lower bound.

Thus at budget three the last good integer radius is $3h-2$ and the first failure is $3h-1$. Sponsor-rate instances include $(n,k)=(16,2)$ and $(32,2)$ at rates $1/8$ and $1/16$. In the prime field $p=(3\cdot2^{48}+69)2^{80}+1=1020847100762898806271677231708359360513$, the budget is $\lfloor p/2^{128}\rfloor=3$ and both smooth domains exist. The Proth witness is $5^{(p-1)/2}\equiv-1\pmod p$, with the odd cofactor below $2^{80}$. These two last-good/first-bad pairs are $(10,11)$ and $(22,23)$. This dimension-two construction does not settle the same budget at production length.

The scalar dimension-two equivalence `ProximityGap.FourSecants.fourAt_iff` also passed the same focused check in [FourSecantListBoundary.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourSecantListBoundary.lean) with those three axioms. The argument for arbitrary positive interleaving width is given above.

**Small-error extension of the two-line count.** The upper bound $2e+2$ in fact holds for every $0\le 2e\le n$ at $1\le k\le n/16$. If $3e<n-d$, any three selected agreement sets share more than $d$ coordinates. All points lie on one line of occupancy at most $e+1$. Otherwise $e\ge(n-d)/3>5n/16$. With $M=2e+3$ we have $M-2>5n/8$. The same large-core argument gives $L_{\max}\le4$ and $z\le n/4+2d$. The excess of each collinear triple over $d$ is at most $n/4+d<5n/16$. Consequently $W<d+1\le n/16$, whereas $W\ge n(M-4)/(8(M-1))>n/8-3/5>n/16$ for $n\ge16$. The lower construction begins at $2e=n-k$ when $n-k$ is even. For the production length this extends the exact budget window to $1{,}006{,}632{,}960\le B\le1{,}295{,}252{,}617$. Below $B=n$ the intersection-locator construction supplies the bad step. This is a written extension of the incidence proof above.

## 2026-11-21 · General rate range of the two-line argument

Here is a consequence of the two-line proof above for sequences of growing RS codes on arbitrary injective domains. Suppose the rate tends to $ρ>0$, the agreement fraction tends to $α<1$, and $α^3>ρ$ and $3α>1+3ρ$. For sufficiently large lengths the maximum number of bad scalars is at most $2e+2$, where $e=n-t$ and $t$ is the required number of agreements.

Choose a fixed integer $L$ with $2/L<3α-1-3ρ$. Any subfamily of at least $e+2$ selected scalars eventually contains $L$ points on a line. Otherwise, a uniform triple of distinct selected points is collinear with probability at most $(L-2)/(e)$ by uniqueness of the line through a pair. Its expected number of common agreements is then at most $k-1+n(L-2)/e$. The coordinatewise third-moment lower bound gives at least $(α^3-o(1))n$, a contradiction. Each of two such lines has at least $t-n/L$ common row agreements. Their cores meet in at most $k-1$ positions, and an outside point agrees on at most $k-1$ sites of each core. It therefore has at most $n-2t+2n/L+3(k-1)<t$ agreements. In a purported family of $2e+3$ points, first find one line and then find a second among its at least $e+2$ outsiders. Since each line has at most $e+1$ bad scalars, some point lies outside both. This proves the upper bound.

When $2e≥n-k$, $e+k+1≤n$, $k≥2$, and the field has at least $n+2\max(0,2e+2-n)$ elements, explicit received rows attain $2e+2$. If $2e+2≤n$, use the two overlapping cores already described. If $2e+2>n$, choose two disjoint cores of size $n-e-1$, prescribing row values $(0,0)$ and $(x,1)$ respectively at evaluation point $x$. For each of the $c=2e+2-n$ leftover points, choose two new distinct scalars $a,b$, with all $2c$ choices distinct and outside the negated domain. Solve $u+av=0$ and $u+bv=x+b$ for its two received entries. Now both decoded lines acquire each other's core positions with scalars $-x$ and the leftover positions with their assigned scalars. Core size at least $k$ forces the unique row polynomials on each core and makes its extra agreement nonjoint. This lower bound uses arbitrary injective evaluation points. For rate $1/8$, the asymptotic exact range includes every fixed error fraction strictly between $7/16$ and $1/2$ in fields of the displayed size. This paragraph is a mathematical proof; the production Lean modules have more specific finite hypotheses.

### Four rotation-related heads on 32 fibres cannot lower the rate-half ceiling

This test concerns four scalar polynomials of the form $f(s^jX^{n/32})$ with $j=0,1,2,3$, $s$ of order four, and $\deg f\le15$, lifted to two rows with the common direction $(X,1)$. At the predecessor a one-fresh construction requires four cores of size $z\ge(2n+1)/3$. Write $c$ for the number of coordinates in all four cores, $h$ for the number in none and $t_3$ for the number in exactly three. The common direction allows at most one scalar per nondead owned coordinate and four per hole. More than $n$ scalars requires $3h>c$. Counting core memberships then gives $t_3\ge4z-2n+2h-2c>4z-2n-4c/3$.

Write $f(Y)=\sum_{i=0}^3Y^iA_i(Y^4)$, with $\deg A_i\le3$. The 32 base coordinates form eight four-point orbits. On each orbit with a proper triple, Fourier inversion gives $A_1(t)=r A_2(t)=r^2 A_3(t)$, where $r$ is the unique exceptional point and $r^4=t$. On the $q$ orbits on which all four heads coincide, all three nonconstant Fourier coefficients vanish. Distinct heads force $q\le3$. Since each orbit covers $n/8$ positions, the count above requires at least $6,5,3,2$ proper-triple orbits for $q=0,1,2,3$ respectively.

For $q=0$ take six exceptional representatives $r_i$ and let $U(T)=\prod_i(T-r_i^4)$. Interpolation of the three cubics on six points gives six homogeneous equations on the nonzero weights $A_3(r_i^4)/U'(r_i^4)$. Their exponents in $r_i$ are $0,1,2,4,5,6$. The alternant is singular exactly when $e_3(r_1,\ldots,r_6)=0$, since the degree-six monic root polynomial has no cubic coefficient precisely in that case. For $q>0$, cancel the common factor of degree $q$ from all three $A_i$. The required proper triples outnumber the degree $2(3-q)$ of $A_1A_3-A_2^2$, so this polynomial vanishes identically. In lowest terms write $A_1/A_2=A/B$. The identity implies $(A_1,A_2,A_3)=V(A^2,AB,B^2)$ over the polynomial ring. For $q\ge2$ this makes the ratio constant, allowing at most one exceptional representative. For $q=1$ the ratio has numerator and denominator of degree at most one. Five exceptional representatives would be roots of the same nonzero polynomial $YB(Y^4)-A(Y^4)$, requiring both $e_2=e_3=0$ by its missing quadratic and cubic coefficients.

At the first production prime the six-point and five-point conditions both fail for every choice of distinct base orbits. Simultaneous rotation removes four equivalent choices and preserves the vanishing conditions. The exact probe checks $\binom86 4^5=28672$ and $\binom85 4^4=14336$ configurations. The result covers the stated pure rotational lift. A different four-head construction remains possible. [Probe](../../../scripts/probes/probe_rate_half_four_rotations.py)

## 2026-11-21 · Punctured coset complements at the unique-decoding boundary

Four distinct rate-half polynomial heads cannot each agree with a shared scalar word outside a different quarter-coset and a common set of at most $n/4$ erased coordinates. This holds at both named primes. It rules out moving the four supports slightly away from a partition into quarter-cosets in this precise sense. A family of unrestricted error supports need not fit this pattern.

More generally, let $D$ have $n$ evaluation points, let $H$ be a nonconstant classifier polynomial of degree $m$, and let the labels $a_i$ include all its values on $D$. Let $S$ be the retained coordinates. Suppose every polynomial $P_i$ has degree below $k$ and agrees with the same word $w$ on $S$ away from its own fiber $H(x)=a_i$. If $k+m\le |S|$ and $k+2m\le n$, all heads are identical. To prove this, write $Q_i=(H-a_i)P_i$. At every retained point, $Q_i(x)=(H(x)-a_i)w(x)$, affine in the label. For any three indices the polynomial $(a_j-a_l)Q_i+(a_l-a_i)Q_j+(a_i-a_j)Q_l$ vanishes on $S$ and has degree below $k+m$, so it vanishes identically. At any original coordinate, take $a_l=H(x)$. The identity gives $(H-a_i)(H-a_j)(P_i-P_j)=0$ there. This polynomial has degree below $k+2m$, so it vanishes identically on degree grounds. Cancellation in the polynomial ring gives $P_i=P_j$.

For $n=4m$, $k=2m$, and $H=X^m$ on the cyclic subgroup, the uniform erasure limit $m$ is sharp. With $m+1$ erasures all outside the first fiber, just $k-1$ coordinates remain outside that fiber and the erased set. Set its head equal to the monic locator of those $k-1$ points and all other heads and the received word to zero. Distinct heads are then possible. This last example exhibits two different polynomials among the four heads.

### A volume obstruction to simultaneous approximation at highly composite orders

This concerns a possible counterexample to uniform **prime-field** character-sum bounds at non-dyadic orders. Let $n=\prod_{\ell\le x}\ell$ be a primorial, $d=\varphi(n)$, and let $V\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be the primitive Fourier subspace of the cyclic group of order $n$. Under the Chinese remainder isomorphism, arrays in $V$ have zero sum on every coordinate fiber in each prime direction. Values on the box with one entry deleted from each axis determine all other entries by the zero-sum conditions. Consequently $L=V\cap\mathbb Z^n$ has the integral tensor-product basis $\bigotimes_\ell(e_{i_\ell}-e_{\ell-1})$. Its Gram matrix is the tensor product of $I+J$ matrices. The prime-$\ell$ factor has determinant $\ell$, so
$$\log \operatorname{covol}(L)=\frac d2\sum_{\ell\le x}\frac{\log\ell}{\ell-1}.$$

For $p\equiv1\pmod n$ and an order-$n$ element $g$, each residue vector $(b g^i/p\bmod 1)_i$ lies in $V/L$. Indeed every fiber sum vanishes modulo one by the geometric-series identity in $\mathbb F_p$. Lift the values on the deleted-entry box to real representatives, then complete by zero sums. A nonzero $b$ with a lift of norm $\le c\sqrt n$ would give real character sum at least $(1-2\pi^2c^2)n$. But for every **fixed** $c>0$ the Haar measure of the image of a ball of that radius in $V/L$ is at most its Euclidean volume divided by $\operatorname{covol}(L)$. Stirling, $d/n\sim e^{-\gamma}/\log x$, and $\sum_{\ell\le x}\log\ell/(\ell-1)\sim\log x$ give an upper bound $\exp[-(e^{-\gamma}/2+o(1))n]$ on that measure. Pigeonholing translates of this ball cannot force an aligned frequency in the regime $\log p=o(n)$ needed for this linear-size counterexample. This distinguishes the real dimension $d=o(n)$ from the volume cost of controlling the periods. It leaves open alignments whose small coordinate representatives wrap across multiple translates of $V$. Dyadic prize orders have $d=n/2$ and fall outside this primorial calculation.

## 2026-11-21 · The named order-sixteen full-window obstruction is empty

The previously assumed predicate `V16PrimEmptyCharP p` now has a Lean proof for **every prime** $p$. More generally, for $Y$ a set of $(2m)$th roots in a field and $m$ nonzero in that field, if $y\in Y$ but $-y\notin Y$, some odd power sum with exponent $1,3,\ldots,2m-1$ is nonzero. Indeed,
$$
\sum_{r=0}^{m-1} y^{-2r}\sum_{x\in Y}x^{2r+1}
=\sum_{x\in Y}x\sum_{r=0}^{m-1}((x/y)^2)^r
=m y.
$$
Only $x=y$ contributes. Every other ratio squared is a nontrivial $m$th root. At $m=8$ this discharges the exact hypothesis of `q1_d16_prize_clean`. In characteristic two a nonempty antipodal-free set is impossible. The focused Lean 4.35 build checked the general theorem, the every-prime specialization and the original gate without its hypothesis. Their only axioms are `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. The prime-field Paley problem requires control with fewer available moments. [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_wfLB2_Q1Direct_d16.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · Sharper prime-field upper bounds from all dyadic harmonics

At the two production primes, respectively, every nonzero frequency satisfies
$|\eta_b|<1055664013$ and $|\eta_b|<1055761643$. This improves the preceding determinant bounds by 3,893,306 and 3,872,281. The estimate still has linear dependence on subgroup size. The proof below is mathematical. The exact finite bounds were checked with `python3 -O scripts/probes/probe_paley_harmonic_determinant.py`.

Write $f(t)=1-\cos(2\pi t)$ and $\phi(t)=\operatorname{dist}(t,\mathbb Z)^2$. Set $h_j=2^{-j-1}$ and $a_j=2f(h_j)^2$. For all real $t$,
$$f(t)\ \ge\ \sum_{j\ge0} a_j\phi(2^jt).$$
The coefficients begin with $8,2,3-2\sqrt2$. The weights use the squared-distance certificate at many nonzero frequencies of the same subgroup.

**Proof of the harmonic inequality.** The identity $a_j=4f(h_j)-f(2h_j)$ gives $S_j:=\sum_{i\le j}4^ia_i=f(h_j)/h_j^2$. By symmetry it suffices to work on $[0,1/2]$. On the grid of step $h_0$, the term $a_0\phi(t)$ agrees with $f$ at both nodes. Assume the inequality on the grid of step $2h_j$. The previous sum $P_{j-1}=\sum_{i<j}a_i\phi(2^i t)$ is a quadratic with leading coefficient $S_{j-1}$ on each old cell. At its midpoint $z$ the new summand equals $a_j/4$. The new residual, minus the mean of the old endpoint residuals, equals
$$S_{j-1}h_j^2-a_j/4-\cos(2\pi z)f(h_j).$$
This expression vanishes at $z=h_j$ because the new sum agrees with $f$ there and at the two endpoints of the first cell. It is nonnegative at every other midpoint by monotonicity of cosine on $[0,\pi]$. Old nodes keep their values. Induction establishes the inequality on all dyadic grids. Each finite partial sum is below $f$ on a dense set since later terms are nonnegative. Continuity and then monotone passage to the series prove the claim.

**Optimality for nonnegative combinations of harmonic squared distances.** Suppose $c_m\ge0$ and $f(t)\ge\sum_{m\ge1}c_m\phi(mt)$ for all $t$. For $s=v_2(m)\le j$ the odd numerator of $m h_j$ gives $\phi(m h_j)\ge4^s h_j^2$. Therefore $T_j:=\sum_{s\le j}4^s\sum_{v_2(m)=s} c_m\le S_j$ for each $j$. For any $J$, multiply these inequalities by $4^{-j}-4^{-j-1}$ for $j<J$ and $4^{-J}$ for $j=J$, then add. This bounds $\sum_{v_2(m)\le J}c_m$ by $\sum_{j\le J}a_j$. The limit proves $\sum_m c_m\le\sum_j a_j$. The same argument bounds the objective with odd frequencies weighted by $(2p)^{-2/d}$ and even frequencies by $p^{-2/d}$, since $(2p)^{-2/d}\ge p^{-2/d}/4$ for $d\ge1$. The displayed dyadic series attains both objectives.

**Application.** The dimension-$d$ determinant argument above gives mean squared phase distances at least $p^{-2/d}/d$ on an ordinary coset, or $(2p)^{-2/d}/d$ after translation by half a turn. Every even dilation removes the half turn. Every dyadic multiplier stays nonzero modulo the odd prime $p$. For the two possible signs of the real period this yields the common ceiling
$$\frac{|\eta_b|}{n}\le1-\frac{8(2p)^{-2/d}+p^{-2/d}\sum_{j\ge1}a_j}{d}.$$
At $d=256$, terms $j=0,\ldots,8$ already save strictly more than $18077811$ and $17980181$, respectively. The probe encloses the cosine half angles and inverse 128th roots by rational arithmetic. The optimality concerns this class of harmonic test inequalities. The square-root-scale upper bound remains open.

For the earlier field-uniform choice $n\ge4\log_2(2p)$, the same proof upgrades its deficit $2/\log_2(2p)$ to $\bigl(\sum_{j\ge0}a_j\bigr)/(4\log_2(2p))$. Indeed $p^{-2/d}\ge(2p)^{-2/d}$, so the new deficit is at least $\sum a_j/8$ times the old one.

## 2026-11-21 · A three-head ceiling at the second production prime

For the second repository prime, let $n=2^{30}=3r+1$, $r=357913941$, with the order-$n$ domain $H$. For every dimension $2≤k≤r$, the MCA error at $e=2r=715827882$ is at least $(2n+1)/p$. The security budget on this field is exactly $2n$. In particular the upper endpoint for the rate-sixteenth threshold improves from $119/128$ to $715827882/1073741824$. Together with the two-line lower bound already recorded above, the width of the new bracket is $68201574/1073741824$. This entry records the mathematical proof. Full kernel verification is being tracked separately.

Partition $H\setminus\{1\}$ into three sets $C_0,C_1,C_2$ of size $r$. Put the two received symbols at $x\in C_j$ equal to $(j^2x,j)$, and at $1$ put $(8,3)$. For $i\ne j$, the head pair $(i^2X,i)$ explains its entire core $C_i$. At $x\in C_j$ the fold also agrees with this head at scalar $-(i+j)x$. At $x=1$ the corresponding scalar is $(i^2-8)/(3-i)$. Each witness has $r+1=n-e$ agreements. A joint explanation on it must equal the head on its core, by the root bound on at least $k$ evaluations, and its second row disagrees at the extra site. Each listed scalar is therefore bad.

The ordinary labels occupy the three cosets with representatives $-1,-2,-3$. In each coset the unordered head pair is fixed by its sum, and the site fixes which of the two heads was the received one. Every label listed in that coset is consequently distinct. Raising the three representatives and three exceptional labels to the $n$-th power modulo the second prime gives six distinct residues. Thus the three exceptional labels are distinct and outside the ordinary cosets. The construction supplies $6r+3=2147483649$ distinct bad scalars. The same construction applies to rates one eighth and one quarter on this field.

## 2026-11-21 · The one-line regime before the rate-sixteenth packing jump

Let $n\ge36$, $1\le k\le n/16$, $d=k-1$, $t=n-e$, and $S=n-2e-d$. For any injective Reed–Solomon evaluation domain over a finite field, **the exact worst-case number of bad scalars is $e+1$ whenever $S\ge3$**. This counts distinct bad scalars; the selected polynomials need not depend affinely on the scalar throughout the unique-decoding ball.

Suppose a family has $M=e+2$ points. If $3e<n-d$, every triple shares more than $d$ agreements, so all the points lie on one line, which holds at most $e+1$. Otherwise $e>5n/16$. A core of size $z$ with $2z>2n-3t+4d$ forces all points outside its line to be collinear, by the fresh-coordinate triple argument above. If no such core exists, then $z\le n/4+2d$, and five on-line points cannot fit because $5t-4z>n$. The proportion of collinear triples is at most $2/(M-2)=2/e$, and their excess over $d$ is at most $n/4+d<5n/16$. Thus the mean triple weight $W$ is less than $d+2\le n/16+1$. At average incidence at least $M/2$, the same third-moment lower bound gives $W\ge n(M-4)/(8(M-1))>n/8-6/5$. These bounds contradict one another for $n\ge36$; a two-line cover is forced.

Every line has an outside point, since $M>e+1$. Its outside point has at most $d$ core agreements and at most $n-z$ others. Therefore $z\le e+d$ and $t-z\ge S$. The line packing $L(t-z)+z\le n$ gives $(L-1)S\le e$. Under $S\ge3$ the two lines together hold at most $2+2\lfloor e/3\rfloor\le e+1$ for $e\ge1$. For $e=0$ all points are already on the first line. To attain the bound, prescribe $(0,0)$ on a core of $t-1\ge k$ points and $(-x,1)$ at every remaining evaluation point $x$. Each remaining point gives its own bad scalar $\gamma=x$ with the core plus that point as a witness.

At $n=2^{30}$ and $k=2^{26}$ the slack $S$ is odd. The exact count is $e+1$ through $e=503316479$, then jumps to $2e+2$ at $e_0=503316480$. Combining the counts gives the following first bad error, hence its quotient by $n$ as the supremum, on the smooth domain for security budget $B=\lfloor p/2^{128}\rfloor$: it is $B$ for $0\le B<e_0$, $e_0$ for $e_0\le B\le2e_0+1$, and $\lfloor B/2\rfloor$ for $2e_0+2\le B\le1295252617$. Each interval uses an explicit bad witness at the stated step and monotonicity below it.

The condition $S\ge3$ matters for general lengths. In $\mathbb F_{67}$ take $n=65$, $k=4$, $e=30$, $D=\{0,\pm1,\ldots,\pm32\}$. Prescribe $(0,0)$ on $\{0,\pm1,\ldots,\pm16\}$ and $(X^3,X)$ on $\{0,\pm17,\ldots,\pm32\}$. At $\gamma=-j^2$, the opposite core of 33 positions and the pair $\pm j$ supply 35 agreements, with no joint explanation. All 32 scalars are distinct. Here $S=2$ and the count exceeds $e+1=31$. The same two-line upper bound gives $2+2\lfloor30/2\rfloor=32$, so the example is sharp.

For any fixed rate bound $\rho<\sqrt5-2$, the argument gives the same one-line result at all sufficiently large $n$ with $k\le\rho n$ and $S\ge3$. To see the uniform margin, put $r=d/n$ and $\alpha=t/n\ge(1+r)/2$. The function $((1+r)/2)^3-r$ is positive on the compact interval $[0,\rho]$, since $(1+r)^3-8r=(r-1)(r^2+4r-1)$. If $3e\ge n-d$, then $M$ is proportional to $n$. In the absence of a large core, $t-z\ge 5t/2-n-2d\ge n(1-3r)/4$, so each line holds only a bounded number of points. The mean triple weight is at most $d+O_\rho(1)$, whereas its incidence lower bound is at least $\alpha^3 n-O_\rho(1)$. This forces two lines, and the preceding occupancy argument applies. The proof at higher fixed rates stops at the cubic comparison.

### Four rate-half list candidates at seven twenty-fourths of the production length

At the budget-three prime above, write $n=8s$, $k=4s$, and take a generator of $\mu_n$. Four distinct scalar codewords can have at most $3s-\lfloor2(s-1)/3\rfloor$ errors each, relative to the same received word. At $n=2^{30}$ this gives a first-bad upper bound of **313,174,700** for budget three. The construction also works over any field with an element of order $8s$.

For the base octagon let $u$ be primitive of order eight and label its points $u^j$ by $j$. The center has value $1$ at exponent $0$, value $u^2(1+u)/(1+u^2)$ at exponent $1$, and is zero elsewhere. The zero polynomial has errors $T=\{0,1\}$. The other three cubics are the following products, each divided by its value at $X=1$:
$$
(X+1)(X+u)(X+u^2),\qquad
(X+1)(X^2+u^2),\qquad
(X^2+1)(X+u^3).
$$
They all agree with the center at $1$ and $u$. For the third cubic the equality at $u$ is $u(1+u^2)^3=2u^2(1+u)(1+u^3)$, where both sides reduce to $2u^3-2u$ by $u^4=-1$. Their respective error sets are $\{2,3,7\}$, $\{2,5,6\}$ and $\{3,4,5\}$. Substitute $X^s$ in the heads and compose the center with the same map. Each octagon point now lifts to a fiber of size $s$. Choose $s-1$ points split as evenly as possible across the three fibers over exponents $2,3,5$ and multiply all heads and the center by their common vanisher. Degrees are at most $3s+s-1=k-1$. Each nonzero head has errors on two of the three selected fibers, so its error count decreases by at least $(s-1)-\lceil(s-1)/3\rceil=\lfloor2(s-1)/3\rfloor$. The zero head retains $2s$ errors. The distinct cubics remain distinct codewords by degree and injectivity of evaluation.

**Exact small test for a nearest candidate.** At $n=4h$, $k=2h$, suppose a candidate is at distance $h$. Subtract it from the center and let $T$ be the center's support. Each other candidate within distance $h+1$ has exactly $h+1$ errors on a set $E$ disjoint from $T$, by minimum distance $2h+1$. It is a scalar multiple of $\prod_{x\notin T\cup E}(X-x)$, of degree $2h-1$. Its restriction to $T$ is proportional to the vector with entries $(X^n-1)'(x)/(L_T'(x)L_E(x))$, with $L$ denoting a monic vanisher. Thus multiple candidates coexist precisely when their projective locator evaluations $(L_E(x))_{x\in T}$ agree. Over the budget-three prime on $\mu_{16}$, all 72 dihedral classes of four-point $T$ yield maximum class size one (64 classes) or two (8 classes). This excludes a list of four at five errors with a candidate within four. The same exhaustive check on $\mu_8$ yields a class of at least three and directly verifies the octagon witness used in the lift. The exact low-rate bound of $e+1$ bad scalars at slack $S=n-2e-(k-1)\ge3$ fails for unrestricted rates and lengths. In $\mathbb F_{31}$, let $D=\{0\}\cup\mu_{15}$, $n=16$, $k=8$, and $e=3$. Use received rows $X^{13},X^{10}$. For every $\gamma\in\mu_5$, the quotient
$$
(X^{16}-X)/(X^3-\gamma)=X^{13}+\gamma X^{10}+\gamma^2 X^7+\gamma^3 X^4+\gamma^4 X
$$
vanishes at exactly the 13 points of $D$ outside the three-point fiber $X^3=\gamma$. Negating its last three terms gives the folded codeword on those 13 positions. The second row has degree 10, so it agrees with any degree-below-8 polynomial on at most 10 positions in the entire domain. All five scalars are MCA-bad, with $e+1=4$ and $S=3$.

A dyadic subgroup also admits a finite obstruction. In $\mathbb F_{97}$, take $D=\mu_{32}$, $k=22$, and $e=4$. Here $(X^{32}-1)/(X^4-\gamma)$ begins with $X^{28}+\gamma X^{24}$, and its remaining terms have degree at most 20. Each $\gamma\in\mu_8$ gives a folded agreement on 28 positions. No degree-below-22 polynomial can match $X^{24}$ on 28 positions. This gives eight bad scalars at $S=3$ and $e+1=5$. Both examples have fixed finite length. ## 2026-11-21 · A four-head and five-head obstruction at the second prime

At the second production prime let $n=2^{30}=3r+1$, $k=2^{26}$ and $d=k-1$. At every integer error count $e\le 2r-1$, a collection of bad scalars covered by affine decoded heads whose joint agreement cores all have size greater than $(n+10d)/5$ has at most $2n$ elements. In particular every construction with a core of $n-e-1$ and one fresh agreement for each assigned label obeys the budget up to that step. The existing three-head construction first exceeds it at $e=2r$. A head covers a scalar $\gamma$ here if $A_i+\gamma B_i$ is the folded polynomial on a bad witness for that scalar. The statement also permits multiple fresh coordinates whenever the chosen heads meet the core cutoff.

Write each distinct head as a pair of degree-at-most-$d$ polynomials $(A_i,B_i)$, and its core as the coordinates at which the received pair equals its evaluated pair. Two different heads have at most $d$ common core sites by the polynomial root bound. For five heads their summed core sizes are therefore at most $n+10d$, since at each site $m\le1+\binom m2$ for the number $m$ of matching heads. There can be at most four heads above the cutoff.

For four heads that are not all on one affine line over the rational function field $F(X)$, at most one of their four triple determinants vanishes identically. Each nonzero determinant has degree at most $2d$. If $h\ge2$ members of a triple agree with the received pair at a site $x$, subtract one of their rows from the others in the determinant with rows $(1,A_i,B_i)$. This shows that the order of vanishing there is at least $h-1$. For an identically zero determinant, charge the three pairwise core intersections of that triple instead. At each site this charges $\binom h2\ge h-1$ for $h\ge2$, and it costs at most $3d$ in total. Summed over the four triples, the total available charge is at most $9d$. A site matching $m=2,3,4$ of the four heads receives at least $2,5,8$ units, respectively. Thus $2(\sum_i |C_i|-n)\le9d$. At the stated production dimension $4(n+10d)/5>n+9d/2$, so four heads above the cutoff must all lie on one affine line over $F(X)$.

Identically vanishing triple determinants also vanish after evaluation at each domain site. On the union $U$ of the four cores, the received pair coincides with one evaluated head. The other distinct evaluated pairs give at most one finite scalar where a genuine mismatch can give a fresh agreement, by collinearity in $F^2$. Outside $U$ there are at most four such scalars per site. Assign one fresh agreement to each bad scalar. The number of distinct labels is at most $4n-3|U|$, with $|U|\ge\sum_i|C_i|-6d>4(n+10d)/5-6d>2n/3$. This is below $2n$. For up to three heads the usual line occupancy gives at most $3(e+1)\le6r=2n-2$. The numerical comparisons use the stated production dimension.

Consequently a budget violation at $e\le2r-1$ would require a bad scalar outside the union of *all* decoded heads with core size above this cutoff. For a witnessing head assigned to such a scalar, at least $n-e-\lfloor(n+10d)/5\rfloor$ distinct fresh coordinates would be needed. The argument supplies no bound on the total contribution of these smaller cores.

The same equivalence and supremum hold for every positive interleaving width by the checked affine-line interleaving theorem. The three new operational statements pass the pinned kernel and standard-axiom audit. **Sharpness in the octagon lift with a fixed base word.** Suppose four base cubics each have at most three errors relative to a fixed word on the octagon. At most one has two errors because the minimum distance is five. If one has two, the other three have three each, all disjoint from those two. These three error sets have no point in common, since three size-three sets on the remaining six positions with pairwise intersections at most one would need at least seven positions if a point belonged to all three. Multiplying by a vanisher with $s-1$ roots can therefore remove at most $2(s-1)$ errors in total across the three lifted heads. If all four have three errors, at most one base coordinate belongs to three error sets, since any two triples of indices share a pair. A point common to all four would require nine distinct positions. Give the head absent from a triple weight two and the other three weight one. At every point the total weight of the error heads is at most three. If there is no triple, use unit weights and a cap of two. The weighted average reduction per head is at most $3(s-1)/5$ in the triple case and $(s-1)/2$ otherwise. A base head with at least four errors still has at least $3s+1$ after this operation. Thus the displayed $3s-\lfloor2(s-1)/3\rfloor$ is optimal among these octagon lifts using a fixed base word and at most $s-1$ common roots.

### Four heads at the unique-radius successor force a cross-ratio of degree one or two

On any injective evaluation domain of size $n=4h$, let four distinct polynomials of degree below $k=2h$ each agree with a common word in at least $3h-1$ coordinates. Their rational cross-ratio
$$
R=\frac{(P_0-P_1)(P_2-P_3)}{(P_0-P_2)(P_1-P_3)}
$$
has degree one or two. Indeed, at a point with $m$ matching heads every complementary product has a zero of multiplicity at least $\max(0,m-2)$, so the polynomial gcd of numerator and denominator has degree at least $\sum_x m_x-2n\ge n-4$. The two raw products have degree at most $2(k-1)=n-2$. Cancelling their gcd proves the upper bound.

If $R$ is a constant, it is neither zero nor one since all four polynomials are distinct and the difference of the two displayed products is, up to sign, the third complementary product. At any evaluation point, a coincidence of two polynomial values forces a coincidence of at least three, by substituting in the cross-multiplication identity. Let $C,T,U$ count points where all four coincide, exactly three coincide, or all four differ. The six pair differences give $6C+3T\le 6(k-1)=3n-6$, whence $U\ge C+2$. The four candidates require at least $3n-4$ total agreements, while the three coordinate types give at most $4C+3T+U=3n+C-2U$. Hence $C=0$, $U=2$ and $T=n-2$. Every pair of heads then coincides in exactly $2h-1$ coordinates, by equality in the root bound. Writing $t_i$ for the number of triple points missing head $i$, the six pairwise sums of distinct $t_i$ all equal $2h-1$. This forces the four $t_i$ to agree, contradicting $T=n-2\equiv2\pmod 4$. This excludes every constant-cross-ratio family at the boundary without restrictions on the interpolant or the error supports.

## 2026-11-21 · Sixth-moment orbit classification and exact Wick budget

Let $H$ be a multiplicative subgroup of dyadic size $n$ in an odd prime field, closed under negation. Assume that every equality of pair sums in $H$ either permutes the pair or has both sums zero. Also assume that $3a=b+c+d$ forces $a=b=c=d$, and that $2(a+b)=c+d$ forces $a+b=0$, for entries in $H$.

Any zero-sum sextuple containing an opposite pair splits into three opposite pairs by the pair-sum hypothesis. In a non-pairable sextuple a triple repetition contradicts the $3a$ hypothesis applied to the negatives of the other three entries. Two distinct repetitions contradict the $2(a+b)$ hypothesis. Thus its unordered multiset either has five antipodal classes and one double, with 360 orderings, or six antipodal classes with 720 orderings. Dyadic scaling acts freely on each such multiset. Indeed, any nonidentity dyadic-order multiplier has a power equal to $-1$, which cannot preserve the nonempty antipode-free support.

Write $A_j$ for the number of scaling orbits of non-pairable unordered zero sums with $j$ antipodal classes. Put $k=n/2$. Pairable sextuples contribute $20k+180k(k-1)+720\binom{k}{3}$ orderings, according as their three pairs occupy one, two or three antipodal classes. Since $H=-H$, negating the last three entries identifies the six-term zero sums with the ordered sextuples counted by the third additive energy. Under the displayed hypotheses this gives
$$
E_3=15n^3-45n^2+40n+360n(A_5+2A_6).
$$
At $n=2^{30}$ the pairable contribution is 18,569,100,537,399,236,459,128,422,400. For each of the two named primes the DC-subtracted sixth-moment bound $pE_3-n^6\le 15pn^3$ is equivalent to $A_5+2A_6\le134217727$.

## 2026-11-21 · Prior bounds and the last uniquely decodable error

In BCHKS25 Theorem 1.3, the numerator in the relative minimum distance is $D=n-d$, where $d=k-1$ is the **maximum polynomial degree** and $k$ is our dimension. Their own discussion after Theorem 1.5 distinguishes this convention from the dimension rate. For $D/3\le e\le D/2-1$ and $S=D-2e$, the number of close scalars needed to invoke Theorem 1.3 is $A=n/e+n/S$. If $A\le e+2$, taking at least $e+2$ close scalars forces joint distance in coordinates at most $e(1+1/(e+1))<e+1$. Integrality gives at most $e$ joint errors. ABF26 Lemma 4.6 identifies the worst-case CA and witness-set MCA errors in this range. For $e\le D/3$, its Theorem 4.8 gives the same upper bound directly. The elementary matching singleton construction is explicit in BCHKS25 Remark 2.5, with $a=e+1$ groups of size one. [BCHKS25, Theorem 1.3 and Remark 2.5](https://www.math.toronto.edu/swastik/rs-proximity-gaps-2025.pdf) · [ABF26, §4.1–4.2](https://eprint.iacr.org/2026/680)

At $n=2^{30}$, the following are exact integer ceilings of $A$ at $S=3$. For $S=5$ the corresponding ceilings are 214,748,369, 214,748,368, 214,748,368 and 214,748,367, respectively, each below $e+2$. For $S\ge7$ in the theorem's range, $n/e\le6$ and $n/S\le n/7$, whereas $e\ge n/6$. This covers all intervening odd slacks. The theorem requires $S\ge2$, excluding $S=1$ at every rate.

| Rate | $e$ at $S=3$ | $\lceil A\rceil$ | $e+2$ | Theorem 1.3 gives the sharp cap |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| $1/2$ | 268,435,455 | 357,913,946 | 268,435,457 | No |
| $1/4$ | 402,653,183 | 357,913,945 | 402,653,185 | Yes |
| $1/8$ | 469,762,047 | 357,913,944 | 469,762,049 | Yes |
| $1/16$ | 503,316,479 | 357,913,944 | 503,316,481 | Yes |

Thus the independent Lean incidence proof of the rate-sixteenth $e+1$ branch re-establishes a consequence of BCHKS25 on the stated production parameters. The combined result determines the actual jump to $2e+2$ at $S=1$, the last integer radius strictly below half the minimum distance, the exact count beyond it on the proved interval, and the resulting first-bad security-budget curve. The single-line lower construction has a published antecedent. ABF26 Definition 4.3 uses the same witness set for folded agreement and failure of joint agreement as the Lean event. Its Theorem 4.7, attributed to Jo, extends the MCA result to every positive interleaving width. The interleaved list-size challenge is separate. ## 2026-11-21 · Two exact smooth BLS12-381 list thresholds, kernel checked

On the subgroup of order 256 of the BLS12-381 scalar field, the last safe and first unsafe error counts are **222 and 223 at dimension 32**, and **240 and 241 at dimension 16**. These determine the rate-one-eighth and rate-one-sixteenth entries of the official smooth-domain challenge for every positive interleaving width. At real closed-ball radius $\delta$, the original unrounded list inequality holds exactly for $\delta<223/256$ and $\delta<241/256$, respectively.

At dimension 32 the 34-agreement upper bound is $\binom{256}{32}/\binom{34}{32}=103822805756204557481443916015906283000$, below the integer security budget $154095187621958656428822154526901524485$. At 33 agreements grouping subsets by product puts at least $1544364235623542792536478250736605959625$ words into one ball. At dimension 16, $\binom{256}{16}=10078751602022313874633200$ suffices for all lists at 16 agreements. A minimum-weight nonzero word generates a full field's worth of distinct codewords at 15 agreements. ## 2026-11-21 · Four exact grand list-decoding thresholds on one smooth domain

Take the 243-bit field $K=\mathbb F_{641^{26}}$ and the evaluation domain $H=\mu_{128}(K)$. The prime-subfield element $21$ has order 128, so this smooth domain has exactly 128 coordinates. At security $2^{-128}$ the integer list budget is $B=\lfloor 641^{26}/2^{128}\rfloor=27955632488535336577577692993784094$. For **every positive interleaving width and every real radius**, the worst closed-ball list size is at most $2^{-128}|K|$ exactly when the radius lies strictly below the corresponding first bad radius.

| Prize rate | Dimension | Last good error count | First bad radius |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| $1/2$ | 64 | 62 | $63/128$ |
| $1/4$ | 32 | 96 | $97/128$ |
| $1/8$ | 16 | 112 | $113/128$ |
| $1/16$ | 8 | 120 | $121/128$ |

At half rate, any candidate agreeing in 66 positions consumes at least $\binom{66}{64}$ distinct 64-subsets, giving the upper bound $\lfloor\binom{128}{64}/\binom{66}{64}\rfloor<B$. At 65 agreements the monic locator construction partitions the 65-subsets by their product in $H$. One product class gives at least $\binom{128}{65}/128>B$ distinct codewords at the same center. Thus $63/128$ lies strictly between the Johnson radius and capacity. For each smaller dimension $k$, agreement on at least $k$ positions gives the upper bound $\binom{128}{k}\le B$. At $k-1$ agreements the zero-centered ball contains the distinct scalar multiples of a minimum-weight word, so it exceeds budget. Their three boundaries lie one grid step above capacity.
