## 2026-11-21 · A uniform floor for four heads on any even power quotient

Suppose $n=4h$, $x\mapsto x^q$ has $M=n/q$ equal fibers with $M$ even, and four distinct rate-half heads have the form $B+Hf_i(X^q)$. Four such scalar codewords cannot all lie within $e$ errors of the same word unless $4e\ge n+q+2$. The same bound applies to four pairs $W_i=(B_0,B_1)+(A_0,A_1)Hf_i(X^q)$ with a coprime positive-degree direction, each with a joint core of size at least $n-e-1$, giving more than $n$ distinct fresh mixed-agreement labels. This covers arbitrary quotient polynomials, a common locator and arbitrary receiving rows in this construction. At the first prime the sixteen-fiber family requires at least 285212673 errors and the eight-fiber family requires at least 301989889. Every such quotient with $q\ge3$ is excluded at $h+1$ errors. These are restrictions on witness families.

The two opposite-pair difference products $U=(f_0-f_1)(f_2-f_3)$ and $V=(f_0-f_2)(f_1-f_3)$ both vanish once at triple coincidences and twice at fourfold coincidences. If they are not proportional, cancellation of their leading term saves a quotient degree, worth $q$ coordinates. If they are proportional, every repeated pair must belong to a triple. Counting the fourfold, triple and all-distinct inputs then gives a stronger inequality. Lean 4.35.0 verified the pencil degree lemma, the triple and fourfold divisibilities, the proportionality implication, and the incidence arithmetic in both branches. All eight axiom reports contain only standard axioms. The application to the joint cores and the quotient fibers is written out in the [full proof](../../../docs/research/proximity-gap/four-head-quotient-floor.md). [Lean lemmas](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourHeadCrossRatio.lean)

### Four exact list boundaries on the same 32-point smooth domain

On the 32nd roots of unity in $\mathbb F_{673^{16}}$, the integer budget is 5,204,770 at tolerance $2^{-128}$. The first failing error counts are 15, 24, 29, and 31 for dimensions 16, 8, 4, and 2, respectively. The last passing counts are 14, 23, 28, and 30. Each real-radius cutoff is the first failing count divided by 32 and the cutoff fails for every positive interleaving width. The half-rate cutoff is strictly below capacity, the quarter-rate cutoff is exactly capacity, and the two lower-rate cutoffs are one grid step beyond capacity.

At 17 half-rate agreements, the subgroup products of 17-subsets supply a list of at least 17,678,835 words. With 18 agreements the packing bound gives at most 3,928,630. At eight quarter-rate agreements, choose eight of the 31 nonreserved evaluation points, using the singleton-fiber locator theorem, to obtain 7,888,725 distinct words. With nine agreements the bound is 1,168,700. At the two lower rates, multiples of a minimum-weight word already supply a field-sized list at the first bad radius. The adjacent packing bounds are 35,960 and 496.

Lean 4.35.0 compiled the [domain, four threshold equivalences and canonical all-rate prize theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourRateList673.lean) without warnings. Its axiom reports contain exactly `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

**Degree-one collision patterns.** The octagon example above has total error count $11$, so the same gcd proof gives cross-ratio degree at most $2(4-1)-(2\cdot8-11)=1$. The nonconstancy theorem makes this an example of degree exactly one. For a degree-one $R$, two distinct pairs cannot both coincide at one point: the corresponding complementary product would have a double zero while the other product is nonzero, impossible for a fractional-linear map. Each coordinate with exactly one coinciding pair lies in a different one of the three fibers of $R$ over $0,1,\infty$. Write $V$ for the number of such coordinates and $C,T,U$ for the counts above. Then $V\le3$, $V+2U\le C+4$ by agreement counting, and $2V+3U\ge3C+6$ by counting roots of all six differences. These imply $(C,V,U)\in\{(0,0,2),(0,2,1),(0,3,0),(1,3,1)\}$. The first is excluded by the parity proof above. The three other possibilities have the following additional restrictions, up to head relabeling.

* At $(0,2,1)$ the two coinciding pairs must share a head, since complementary pairs map to the same special value of $R$. Call the pairs $01$ and $02$. At every coordinate the center must match a largest class, by equality in the agreement sum. Let $r$ be the head matched at the all-distinct point; then $t_i=h-2+(2,1,1,0)_i+1_{i=r}$. If $r=0$ or $1$, the pair $23$ has at least $2h$ coincidences; if $r=2$, the same holds for pair $13$. Thus $r=3$.
* At $(0,3,0)$ and $(1,3,1)$ the total pair-root count saturates $6(2h-1)$. There is one exceptional pair from each complementary-pair partition, hence these three edges form either a triangle or a star. Every pair $ij$ satisfies $C+t_k+t_l+1_{ij\text{ an exceptional pair}}=2h-1$ for its complement $kl$. At $(0,3,0)$ these equalities force a star, since a triangle would make three equal counts $t_i$ with pairwise sum $2h-1$. The central head is missing from $h$ triples and each leaf from $h-1$. The three leaves can reach $3h-1$ agreements only if all star pairs are matched, since at most one agreement below the aggregate maximum is allowed; changing a matched pair to a singleton loses the corresponding leaf. The central head is thus at distance $h$. At $(1,3,1)$ an odd pair sum instead excludes a star. All points must realize maximal agreement. Each triangle vertex is missing from $h-1$ triples and the remaining head from $h-2$, forcing that remaining head to be matched at the all-distinct point.

### Exact unrestricted rate-half threshold at length sixteen and budget three

In the 130-bit budget-three prime above, the last safe integer radius on $\mu_{16}$ at dimension eight is **5** and the first failure is **6**. The no-four assertion at five errors uses an exhaustive finite-field calculation. The four-point-support check above handles any word at distance four from a candidate, and a candidate at distance at most three excludes even a second candidate by minimum distance nine. It remains to check four words all at distance five. Subtract the first candidate so the received word has a support $T$ of size five. Write $M=X^{16}-1$. For every other error support $E$ of size five, $|T\cap E|\le1$ by minimum distance. The corresponding polynomial must vanish on the complement of $T\cup E$.

Scale each received value at $x\in T$ by the nonzero factor $L_T'(x)/M'(x)$. When $E\cap T$ is empty, a polynomial vanishing outside $T\cup E$ is $M/(L_T L_E)$ times an affine polynomial. Thus the five scaled values lie on the projective line spanned by $(1/L_E(x))_{x\in T}$ and $(x/L_E(x))_{x\in T}$. When $E\cap T=\{t\}$, only a constant multiple of $M/(L_T L_{E\setminus\{t\}})$ is possible. The value at $t$ is unconstrained; the line is spanned by the unit vector at $t$ and the vector zero there and $1/L_{E\setminus\{t\}}(x)$ elsewhere. Four candidates therefore require three such lines sharing a point with all coordinates nonzero and with all three pairwise intersections of the index sets $E$ of size at most one. Testing the necessary condition suffices for exclusion.

Rotation of the domain and inversion preserve all support configurations, the latter by transforming $P(X)$ to $X^7P(1/X)$ and the received values by the same nonzero coordinate factors. There are 147 dihedral orbits of $T$. The exact program first rejects pairs of lines by the determinant on four projected coordinates; a nonzero determinant proves the full lines disjoint. It then intersects the two full lines by row reduction, keeps only points with five nonzero coordinates, and checks incidences on each fixed first line, including coincident lines. All 147 representatives exclude a compatible triple. An independent GMP implementation counted 3,218 compatible isolated line-pair intersections overall. [All five-point supports](../../../scripts/probes/probe_rate_half_sixteen_lines.py) · [All four-point supports](../../../scripts/probes/probe_rate_half_nearest_four.py)

For failure at radius six take four affine polynomials $aX+a^{-1}$ for $a=g^j$, $j\in\{0,1,3,7\}$. Their six pairwise intersection coordinates $(ab)^{-1}$ are distinct elements of $\mu_{16}$. Multiply by a common degree-six polynomial vanishing at six other coordinates. Let the center equal the four heads at the common roots and the coincident pair at each of the six pairwise intersections. Assign one of the remaining four coordinates to each head. Each head has ten agreements and degree at most seven. The proved finite-budget projection to one row gives the same safe radius at every positive interleaving width; taking identical rows lifts the failure.

## 2026-11-21 · Exhaustive restricted sixth-moment comparisons

At each of the two named sponsor primes, a complete GMP run found zero intersections between the nonzero difference signatures $S=\{(x-1)^n:x\in H,\ x\ne1\}$ and either $2^n S$ or $\{(3-x)^n:x\in H,\ x\ne1\}$. Here $H=\mu_n$ and $n=2^{30}$. It also verified $3^n\ne1$. The run examined 536,870,912 exponents in each inversion-symmetric family and 1,073,741,823 in the directed family. At each prime, no exact base low limb appeared among the retained possible matches. Both executions finished successfully.

Dividing $a+b$ by $-b\in H$ shows $(a+b)^n\in S$ whenever $a+b\ne0$. Thus $2(a+b)=c+d$ forces $a+b=0$. In a solution of $3a=b+c+d$, a choice $b\ne a$ would give $((3-b/a)^n)=(c+d)^n$; this lies outside $S$ on the left and inside $S$ on the right. The possible zero on the right would put $3=b/a$ in $H$. Hence $a=b$, and permuting $b,c,d$ gives equality of all four. Together with the fourth-moment scan these conclusions supply the repeated-entry exclusions in [the six-term classification](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SixthMomentAntipodalShape.lean).

Lean 4.35 checked [the finite-window implication](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SixthMomentSignatureBridge.lean). The theorem uses exactly the two scan ranges, proves that inversion covers all remaining nonzero differences, and converts both comparisons and the Sidon law into the hypotheses of the six-term classification. All four axiom reports contain only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. Consequently both named primes now have precisely the residual $A_5+2A_6\le134217727$ for the centered sixth-moment Wick bound. The reductions are proved in Lean; the two fields' exhaustive comparisons are computational evidence from GMP, with no kernel replay of the billions of modular powers.

The [C++ verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_sextic.cpp) uses a concurrent bit filter, sorts all retained low limbs, and compares exact field values if any base limb survives. Equal field values necessarily share all the filter bits and their low limb. Each base shard verifies its endpoint against independent modular exponentiation. An exhaustive control at $p=97,n=16,g=8$ produced 10 doubled-signature pairs and 24 directed triple-signature pairs, matching direct enumeration. Reproduce with `c++ -O3 -std=c++17 scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_sextic.cpp -lgmpxx -lgmp -o /tmp/sponsor-sextic` (add GMP's include and library paths as needed), followed by `/tmp/sponsor-sextic sextic-control 2`, `/tmp/sponsor-sextic first 8` and `/tmp/sponsor-sextic second 8`. Numerical field comparisons run in GMP.

This prevents a larger dyadic lift from changing the quotient table while preserving every lifted triple at degree below half the new length. ### Exact worst-center list sizes at and beyond capacity

For arbitrary distinct RS evaluation points, every finite alphabet and every positive interleaving width, the complete list-size profile at agreement counts from zero through the dimension has an explicit formula. At positive agreement count t and dimension k<n, the maximizers alternate with k−t. Even parity gives precisely the centers with maximum agreement k, including the received word whose first row is X^k. Odd parity gives precisely the codeword centers. At t=0 and at full dimension the formula handles ties. In particular the list at t=k has exactly choose(n,k) words in the worst case. At t=k−1 and k≥2 it has exactly 1+(q^m−1)choose(n,k−1). The proof counts interpolation subsets and sharpens the alternating binomial bound by comparing a ratio of first differences. Agreement counts above k remain open in general.

The [proof and full formula](../../../docs/research/proximity-gap/exact-list-profile-from-capacity.md) include the equality conditions. An [exhaustive exact oracle](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mds_capacity_list_profile.py) checked 26 codes, 37,529 coset representatives and 2,345,344 word comparisons across scalar, double, and triple interleavings. The same oracle independently verified Bonneau's coset-weight formula for every applicable weight shell. This general profile currently has a written proof. The [MDS coset weight reference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.12722) is available locally.

## 2026-11-21 · A weight-twelve squarefree relation at each named prime

For each named generator $g$ of order $2^{30}$, let $W$ consist of its first $2^{29}$ consecutive powers starting at $1$. The set contains exactly one representative from each antipodal pair. It has 33,257,280,773,621,061,829,797,459,504,797,335,680,374,626,320,384 six-subsets. At both primes this exceeds the number of residues. Two different six-subsets have the same sum. Cancel their common entries and negate the second residual subset. The resulting nonempty set lies in the actual multiplicative subgroup, has at most twelve elements, contains no opposite pair, and sums to zero. This sharpens the weight-220 obstruction in G139; the earlier BGK birthday crossover uses unrestricted six-subsets of the full subgroup.

For $N=\binom{2^{29}}6=qp+r$ with $0\le r<p$, let $L_y$ count the six-subsets of $W$ with sum $y$. The number of unordered distinct equal-sum pairs is $C=\sum_y\binom{L_y}2\ge p\binom q2+qr$. The inequality follows from $qL_y\le\binom{L_y}2+\binom{q+1}2$, with equality when $L_y=q$ or $q+1$. The first prime has $q=91$ and $C\ge1496951086932726722611412637498967365981178400075686$. The second has $q=45$ and $C\ge740250537494205522170478776785203642930465897184245$. The pinned Lean kernel checked both literal prime-field witnesses and the actual collision counts. [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProductionHalfSubsetRelations.lean)

For an unordered reduced pair of residual size $d$ on each side, exactly $\binom{|W|-2d}{6-d}$ original six-subset pairs give that reduction, by adjoining a common intersection outside both residual sets. Distinct singleton sets cannot have the same sum, so $2\le d\le6$ and the largest expansion count is $\binom{2^{29}-4}4=3461531097175924400622048913653795$. Thus there are at least 432453456262174845 distinct unordered reduced relations at the first prime and 213850610239537011 at the second. The weak Sidon property established by the separate fourth-moment scan also excludes $d=2$. With that scan the maximal expansion count falls to $\binom{2^{29}-6}3=25790416476305785593266120$, giving respective lower bounds of 58042920257143118257788465 and 28702542984301541995609681. Lean verified the fiber multiplicity upper bounds and all four lower bounds for the actual finite set of reduced unordered pairs, with its two sides identified under reversal. It also verified the passage from the full-subgroup weak Sidon hypothesis to uniqueness of two-subset sums on the half. The two larger numerical bounds use the external fourth-moment scan for that hypothesis. All thirteen reported axiom sets contain only the three standard axioms. A centered moment upper bound remains open.

The upper bounds for the general profile specialize the Bonferroni inequalities of [Adler, Karp and Ross, Theorems 2–3](https://arxiv.org/html/2505.12243#S3), who trace the sharpened coefficient to earlier work. The RS interpolation moments give the intersection sums and the two explicit center classes attain the bounds. The proof note records this connection and links the local paper.

**Unbounded prime-field corollary at fixed security.** For every sufficiently large power of two n, some prime p≡1 mod n gives all four exact first bad list counts n/2−1, 3n/4+1, 7n/8+1 and 15n/16+1. With k=n/2, the ratio between the prescribed-product list lower bound and the preceding packing upper bound is (k+2)/4. Siegel–Walfisz supplies a prime inside this exponentially located interval; the three lower-rate binomial coefficients are exponentially smaller. This written corollary uses fields growing beyond 256 bits. **Common quotient lifts at the exact rate-half boundary.** On any injective domain of length $4h$, the cross-ratio degree result excludes every family $P_i(X)=D(X)+J(X)f_i(H(X))$ of four distinct polynomial heads when $\deg H\ge3$. The common translation and scaling cancel identically from the cross-ratio. Write the base ratio in lowest terms $U/V$. Bézout's identity shows $U(H),V(H)$ remain coprime. If a nonconstant quotient $A/B$ with $\max(\deg A,\deg B)\le2$ equaled this ratio, then $U(H)\mid A$ and $V(H)\mid B$. Both base polynomials would have degree zero since composition multiplies degrees. In particular all power-map lifts from at most $n/3$ fibers are excluded at $h+1$ errors even with unrestricted common vanishers and arbitrary centers. The same rational cross-ratio is preserved by any common fractional-linear change of the four values over the function field. The obstruction applies specifically at the boundary; the octagon lift above succeeds at a larger radius.

The complete endpoint theorem, including the exclusion of constant cross-ratio, passed the focused Lean gate in [CrossRatioEndpoint.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CrossRatioEndpoint.lean). The axiom report for `ProximityGap.FourHeadCrossRatio.degree_one_or_two` contains only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. The independent pure-Python full length-sixteen sweep above finished with 147 support orbits, 3,218 isolated full-support intersections and 29 coincident line pairs. Every compatible triple was excluded.

## 2026-11-21 · The repeated-entry exclusions do not imply the unit sixth-moment bound

There are prime-field subgroups with the same three restricted-relation properties as the two production subgroups, but with a centered sixth moment strictly greater than $15n^3$. The discrepancy is the positive integer $pE_3-n^6-15pn^3$. Here $A_5,A_6$ count the two residual types up to subgroup scaling.

| $n$ | $p$ | generator | $E_3$ | $A_5$ | $A_6$ | discrepancy |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 64 | 173249 | 121218 | 4372480 | 1 | 13 | 7565522944 |
| 128 | 766721 | 656707 | 37222400 | 13 | 64 | 22192060416 |

The [exact enumerator](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sixth_moment_sparse_counterexample.py) tests primality, group order, weak Sidon, the two further difference-signature exclusions, and the absence of $3$ in the subgroup. It counts all ordered triples by their sum and squares those counts. As a second calculation, let $m_u$ count ordered pairs $(x,y)\in H^2$ with $(1+x+y)^n=u$. The triple $(a,b,c)$ corresponds bijectively to $(a,b/a,c/a)$. For each normalized pair of nonzero signature $u$, varying $a$ supplies every member of the coset exactly once. Thus $E_3=(nm_0)^2+n\sum_{u\ne0}m_u^2$. At the first example $m_0=0$ and the sum of squares is 68320. A third independent calculation counts antipode-free solutions anchored at 1 in each residual sector. All three give the same energy.

The two sectors can also occur separately. At $(n,p,g)=(32,11617,3115)$ there are four six-class orbits and zero five-class orbits. At $(32,13217,4477)$ there are two five-class orbits and zero six-class orbits. The examples refute an implication from the three tests alone. Neither example decides the centered sixth-moment inequality at the named primes.

**Exact histogram for the sixth-moment table.** [ExactHistogramSquares.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ExactHistogramSquares.lean) proves that the binary bucket computation equals $\sum_{x\in\mathrm{toFinset}(L)}\mathrm{count}_L(x)^2$ whenever each key in $L$ is below $2^d$. The proof splits the sum into its even and odd keys and accounts for the multiplicities after division by two. The normal compiled module was retained, the focused gate passed, and the semantic theorem uses only the three standard axioms. A separate importing Lean client evaluated the exact 4,096 signatures of $1+x+y$ for the order-64 subgroup modulo 173249 at depth 18. Lean verified the squared-count total **68,320** with `decide +kernel`; the report for this numeric equality lists only `propext` and `Quot.sound`.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact third additive energy from a normalized pair histogram

For a finite field and $n>0$, let $H$ consist of all solutions of $x^n=1$. For $u$ in the field, let $m_u$ count the ordered pairs $(x,y)\in H^2$ satisfying $(1+x+y)^n=u$. The ordered energy of six subgroup elements satisfies
$$
 E_3(H)=(|H|m_0)^2+|H|\sum_{u\ne0}m_u^2.
$$
Indeed every triple is uniquely $(a,ax,ay)$ with $a,x,y\in H$. For a fixed nonzero target $c$, exactly one choice of $a$ works for each pair with $(1+x+y)^n=c^n$, since $a=c/(1+x+y)$ belongs to $H$ precisely in that case. For $c=0$ every scaling works for each pair in the zero bin. This also gives the pointwise triple-count formula. If the field contains a primitive $n$th root, then $|H|=n$.

The [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/NormalizedTripleEnergy.lean) connects the literal filter cardinalities to both repository energy definitions, `SubgroupGaussSumSixthMoment.addEnergy3` and `SubgroupGaussSumMoment.rEnergy H 3`. All five theorem axiom reports contain only the three standard axioms. A concrete histogram requires $|H|^2$ pair evaluations, which makes the identity practical for the order-64 counterexample under separate verification. The identity alone supplies no upper bound on the production histogram.

## 2026-11-21 · Independent audit of the restricted order-64 classification

Consider all primes $p\equiv1\pmod{64}$ for which the order-64 subgroup satisfies the three repeated-entry exclusions. A non-pairable zero sextuple has five or six antipodal classes. Rotate the repeated entry to exponent zero in the five-class case, or any entry in the six-class case. Divide out the common power of two from the exponent differences to get an exact conductor $m$. At least five classes require $m=16,32,64$. Some difference from zero is now odd. Multiplying the exponents by its inverse modulo $m$ sends it to one and preserves the absolute cyclotomic norm by Galois conjugacy.

An independent enumeration chose the remaining antipodal classes and their signs. It checked 490,288 normalized words. To certify each norm by a different algorithm, evaluate the word at all primitive conjugates in the auxiliary prime field $Q=562949953423489$, using the root of order 64 given by 381265028180380, and multiply. Trial division proves $Q$ prime. The integer norm is positive because the complex embeddings occur in conjugate pairs and the polynomial has nonzero degree-below-$m/2$ reduction modulo $X^{m/2}+1$. Each row of its negacyclic multiplication matrix has squared norm 6 or 8. Hadamard therefore bounds the norm by $8^{16}=2^{48}<Q$. The residue reconstructs the exact integer norm uniquely.

All 40,932 distinct norms matched the norm-descent results, with maximum 112397235132034. Multiplication and exact trial division audited all 23,991 distinct prime factors. Exactly 23,469 satisfy $p\equiv1\pmod{64}$. A separate direct additive test using the full pair sums $H+H$, the translates $3-b$, and the values $2(1+b)$ gave 22,432 candidates satisfying all exclusions. Their energy scan agreed on all 32 failing primes and their energies. Direct enumeration of all $64^3$ ordered triple sums independently confirmed the energy at each of these 32 fields. For split primes outside the candidate list the norm argument excludes every non-pairable sextuple under the three hypotheses. This is an exhaustive finite computation with an independent audit. The universal classification has no Lean certificate.

## 2026-11-21 · Complete prime-field classification of the unit sixth moment at order 64

For every prime $p\equiv1\pmod{64}$, take $H=\mu_{64}(\mathbb F_p)$ and let $E_3$ count ordered equalities of triple sums in $H$. The inequality $E_3-64^6/p\le 15\cdot64^3$ fails at precisely **182 primes**. The largest exception is **926510094425921**, and its energy is **3990400**. Exactly 32 exceptions pass all three of the weak Sidon, doubled-difference and directed triple-signature tests. Their largest is **81818497**, where $A_5=4$, $A_6=2$ and the exact orbit budget is seven. This last field satisfies $64^5<40p$, the same budget-simplifying condition used at the two named production primes.

**Why finitely many candidates cover all primes.** Negation in $H$ turns an equality of two triple sums into a zero sum of length six. Pairable tuples contribute $15n^3-45n^2+40n$ at $n=64$, below $15n^3$. From any unpaired tuple delete opposite pairs. The nonempty remainder has weight 2, 4 or 6, with a positive multiplicity at each distinct element and at most one side of every antipodal pair. A single surviving element repeated at most six times cannot sum to zero at a prime $p\equiv1\pmod{64}$. Translate exponents so one root is 1. The remaining exponents generate a group of order $m\in\{4,8,16,32,64\}$. Express them at conductor $m$. Some difference is odd, so an odd change of primitive root normalizes a second exponent to 1. This last step preserves the absolute cyclotomic norm; the particular generator in the field can change.

Enumerate ascending distinct places $0,1,e_3,\ldots$ modulo $m$ with distinct residues modulo $m/2$, and all compositions of the total weight into their positive multiplicities. For the resulting polynomial $A$, the congruence $A(x)=0$ at a root of $x^{m/2}=-1$ forces $p$ to divide its integer norm. Write $A(X)=E(X^2)+XO(X^2)$; multiplying by $A(-X)$ gives $E(Y)^2-YO(Y)^2$, and repeat modulo the next negacyclic relation. The reduced coefficient vector is nonzero of degree less than $m/2$, so its norm is nonzero. The negacyclic multiplication matrix has squared row norm at most $5^2+1^2=26$ and dimension at most 32. Hadamard gives absolute norm at most $26^{16}$. All relevant words therefore give finite, nonzero integer tests.

The arbitrary-precision verifier computed **44,223 distinct norms** with **25,573 split prime divisors**. For each factorization it verifies primality of every factor and equality of their product to the norm. For each split divisor it uses the exact normalized pair histogram to compute $E_3$. All other primes have only pairable tuples. For the five- and six-class sectors alone the counts are 40,932 norms and 23,469 split primes. A separate direct triple histogram and anchored-orbit enumerator check representative cases. The full all-prime enumeration is executable arithmetic; the general histogram identity is proved in Lean. [Classification verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_order64_sixth_moment_classification.py) · [Independent direct counts](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sixth_moment_sparse_counterexample.py) · [Lean energy identity](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/NormalizedTripleEnergy.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · Independent audit of all prime fields at subgroup order 64

For every prime $p\equiv1\pmod{64}$ and its subgroup of order 64, the independent audit found exactly **182 primes** violating $pE_3-64^6\le15p\,64^3$. Exactly **32** of them satisfy the three repeated-entry exclusions. Both lists agree element for element with the [arbitrary-precision classification](../../../scripts/probes/probe_order64_sixth_moment_classification.py). The smallest and largest exceptions are 2753 and 926510094425921. The largest exception satisfying the exclusions is 81818497. These are computations at order 64.

A non-pairable sextuple gives a nonempty word after deleting opposite pairs, of weight 2, 4 or 6. A word supported on one class has nonzero sum since $p\ge193$. For two or more surviving classes, translation, passage to the effective conductor and an odd change of generator place its first two occupied exponents at 0 and 1. The remaining antipodal classes, their signs, and all compositions into positive multiplicities give 657,741 normalized words. There are exactly 44,223 distinct integer norms, with maximum $5^{32}+1=23283064365386962890626$.

The independent calculation multiplies all Galois conjugates separately at the auxiliary primes 562949953423489 and 998244353, using the respective primitive 64th roots 381265028180380 and 922799308. CRT reconstructs each positive norm. Indeed with weight at most six on at least two classes, the largest possible squared row norm of the negacyclic multiplication matrix is $5^2+1^2=26$. Hadamard gives the upper bound $26^{16}=43608742899428874059776$, smaller than the modulus product $561961612026610911807617$. Comparison against the separate recursive norm descent matched the full integer norm sets exactly, for both unrestricted and restricted words.

All factor products were checked, and primality of every distinct factor was established by trial division or recursive Lucas certificates. The 26,263 distinct prime factors give 25,573 split candidate primes, the largest 9224829168916031809. A second implementation recomputed the subgroup histogram and the three restrictions with ordinary additive pair sums. It recovered all 182 exceptions, and direct enumeration of the $64^3$ triple sums confirmed each exceptional energy. The full classification is an audited exact computation with a mathematical covering argument; the universal statement has no Lean certificate.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact sixth moment from signed base-three carries

Let $H=-H$ be a multiplicative subgroup of order $n$ in a prime field of characteristic at least seven, with $3\in H$. The ordered zero-sum sextuple count, equivalently the third additive energy $E_3(H)$, satisfies
$$
E_3(H)\ge 15n^3+15n^2-50n.
$$
Consequently $pE_3(H)-n^6>15pn^3$ whenever $p(15n^2-50n)>n^6$.

The pairable contribution is $15n^3-45n^2+40n$. For each $a\in H$ append one antipodal pair to the multiset $\{a,a,a,-3a\}$. Its unique entry of multiplicity at least three recovers $a$, and subtraction of the core recovers the appended pair. The counts of $a$ and $-a$ differ, excluding pairability. Of the $n/2$ antipodal classes, the choices $\{a,-a\}$ and $\{3a,-3a\}$ have 30 and 60 orderings; each of the other $n/2-2$ classes gives 120. A second family is $\{a,a,a,3a,3a,-9a\}$. Its three distinct values have multiplicities $3,2,1$, giving 60 more orders per $a$. This family has no opposite pair and its triply repeated entry recovers $a$. Summing gives the displayed polynomial. The two positive families use the four-term relation $a+a+a-3a=0$, so they fall outside the extra restricted hypotheses in the existing shape classification.

**Exactness at large factors of $3^m+1$.** Suppose $m\ge13$, $3$ has order $2m$ modulo the odd prime $p$, and $p>6\cdot3^{m-3}$. Take $H=\langle3\rangle$, so $3^m=-1$ in the field, and label its antipodal classes cyclically by $0,\dots,m-1$. A sextuple occupies at most six classes. At least two consecutive classes are empty, since otherwise every empty class would have an occupied successor and $m\le12$. Multiplication by a common power of three moves the two empty classes to $m-2,m-1$. The six terms now have signed integer representatives of magnitude at most $3^{m-3}$. Their sum is a multiple of $p$ of magnitude below $p$, hence is zero as an integer.

Cancel all opposite pairs in the integer relation. A nonempty residual of length two cannot sum to zero. In one of length four or six, let $a$ be a term of smallest magnitude. Dividing by $|a|$ and reducing modulo three shows its multiplicity is three or six, since its opposite is absent. Six equal terms cannot sum to zero, so its multiplicity is exactly three. A four-term residual is $\{a,a,a,-3a\}$. In a six-term residual replace the three copies by $3a$. If the new term meets a pre-existing $-3a$, cancelling them leaves two opposite original terms, contradicting the residual condition. The four terms after the carry are thus $\{b,b,b,-3b\}$. The new $3a$ cannot be the singleton $-3b$, as this would give $b=-a$ among the original terms. Hence $b=3a$ and the residual is exactly the second family. Scaling back preserves all three types. This proves equality in the energy bound under the displayed hypotheses.

For $m=32$, $p=(3^{32}+1)/2=926510094425921$, $n=64$, the strict size condition holds, $3^{32}=-1\pmod p$ and $3$ has order 64. The two Lucas witnesses for $p$ and its largest prime divisor of $p-1$, namely $21523361$, are $7$ and $6$. Their predecessor factorizations are $2^6\cdot5\cdot17\cdot41\cdot193\cdot21523361$ and $2^5\cdot5\cdot17\cdot41\cdot193$. The exact energy is **3,990,400**. The centered moment exceeds the unit bound by **53,959,947,830,646,162,304** after multiplication by $p$. This example concerns unit sixth-moment control for prime fields; the named production subgroups have separate arithmetic.

The focused Lean gate passed and a normal olean was compiled. Each audited theorem uses only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. A separate Lean Lucas check verified primality of $926510094425921$ from the two witnesses above using the same standard axioms. The classification by rotating away from two consecutive empty classes is the written argument above.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact coverage of triples from a single order-65536 coset

Write $K=\mu_m\subseteq H=\mu_n$, with $m=65536$ and $n=2^{30}$. Remove triples containing an opposite pair, allowing repeated elements throughout. Of the $\binom{m+2}3$ unordered multisets of three elements, $(m/2)m$ contain an opposite pair: its antipodal class and the remaining element determine the multiset uniquely. Each orbit of clean triples under $K$ has size $m$. Indeed, a nontrivial dyadic stabilizer would contain $-1$ and its support would contain an opposite pair. The scanner must therefore visit $(m^2+2)/6=715827883$ necklaces per field.

Let the weak cyclic gaps be $a,b,c\ge0$, with $a+b+c=m$. The corresponding representative is $\{1,g^a,g^{a+b}\}$ for a generator of $K$. Rotation of the gaps gives a common scaling. The least cyclic rotation has $a\le b,c$, and when $c=a<b$ the other rotation precedes it. Exactly the triples with a gap equal to $m/2$ contain an opposite pair. This includes one zero gap for a double and $(0,0,m)$ for the all-equal triple. Direct enumeration of every unordered multiset independently reproduced the scanner's representatives through order 128.

For each representative $V$ put $t(V)=(\sum V)^n$. Suppose the scanner certifies distinct values on the clean necklaces, all different from zero and one. An $H$-translate preserves this tag. A triple with an opposite pair has tag one because its sum is its uncancelled subgroup entry. Two equal-sum clean triples thus share a representative, and its nonzero sum forces their scaling factors to agree. Two triples each containing an opposite pair have the same uncancelled entry. In both cases negating the second triple produces a pairable sextuple and the unsigned triples share an actual field element. Consequently different $K$-cosets have disjoint triple-sum sets. Every zero sextuple within one coset is pairable, and none has three entries each from two distinct cosets. The conditional counts are $|3K|=46912496205824$ and $|H(3K)|=768614337836220416$. The exact number of ordered equal-sum triple pairs, when each triple is supported inside a single carrier coset, is $(n/m)(15m^3-45m^2+40m)=69172123725872496640$.

The [Lean bridge](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SingleCosetTripleSignatures.lean) proves the actual scan-row coverage including repetitions, generic signature separation for any group of carrier families with a common representative list, and the single-coset implication. The numerical tag checks for the two production fields are separate computations. The criterion covers the one-carrier and three-plus-three occupancies just described.

**Production result at both primes.** The [clustered triple verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_clustered_triples.cpp) processed all **715827883** cyclic gap representatives at each of the two named primes with $n=2^{30}$ and $m=65536$. There were **zero duplicate low limbs, zero signatures equal to zero, and zero signatures equal to one** in each run. Tags are the low limbs of the least nonnegative representatives of the exact GMP results. Distinct tags imply distinct field elements. The order-16 positive control in $\mathbb F_{97}$ had 43 representatives, 37 excess repeated values, zero zeros and four ones; independent enumeration of multisets under all rotations reproduced the control.

Consequently, for either production field, each of the 16384 translates of $K=\mu_{65536}$ in $H=\mu_{2^{30}}$ has exactly 46912496205824 distinct triple sums. These sets are disjoint across translates. Their union therefore has **768614337836220416** distinct field elements. The contribution to $E_3(H)$ from equalities in which each triple lies inside a single such translate is exactly **69172123725872496640**. All those equalities are pairable, and a nonpairable zero sextuple with a 3+3 split across two distinct translates is impossible. Configurations with other occupancies, including those with six different translates, remain to be bounded. [Lean coverage and implication](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SingleCosetTripleSignatures.lean)

Reproduce the finite evaluation with `c++ -O3 -std=c++17 scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_clustered_triples.cpp -lgmpxx -lgmp -o /tmp/sponsor-clustered` (add GMP's include and library directories as needed), then run `/tmp/sponsor-clustered sextic-control 16 2`, `/tmp/sponsor-clustered first 65536 8` and `/tmp/sponsor-clustered second 65536 8`. Each full run uses approximately 5.4 GiB of memory.

## 2026-11-21 · Production sixth-energy signatures and the width of scalar trace recurrences

For the two fixed prime fields, set $n=2^{30}$ and use the global clean unordered triple necklaces in $H=\mu_n$. The three established repeated-entry conditions imply every clean triple sum is nonzero and is outside $H$. A zero $a+b+c=0$ would give $2(a+b)=(-c)+(-c)$ and then $c=0$. A sum $a+b+c=h\in H$ gives $a+b=h-c$, so weak Sidon supplies an opposite pair. At the singleton tag $u=1$, the ordered normalized histogram has exactly $3(n-1)$ entries. All other occupied bins hold clean triple necklaces, weighted 1, 3 or 6 according to multiplicity.

Let $C_{36}$ count unordered pairs of different clean necklaces with the same tag, one with a double and one without repetitions. Let $C_{66}$ count the corresponding pairs with six orderings on each side. Then
$$
 C_{36}=4A_5,\qquad C_{66}=3A_5+10A_6,\qquad C_{36}+2C_{66}=10(A_5+2A_6).
$$
To see this, partition a non-pairable zero sextuple into unordered halves of size three. Negate one half. The resulting two clean necklaces have equal signatures. Conversely, two necklaces of the same signature have a unique relative scaling that aligns their nonzero sums. Interchanging the halves changes the sextuple by the scalar $-1$. Distinct partitions of a fixed sextuple cannot give the same unordered necklace pair: aligned representatives differ by a common scaling, the sextuple's stabilizer is trivial, and reversal was identified. A sextuple with one double admits four partitions keeping the double together and three splitting it. Six distinct classes give ten partitions. Conversely, the triple and two-double exclusions forbid collisions involving a triple repetition or two doubled necklaces. Independent enumeration recovered $(C_{36},C_{66})=(4,133)$ in the order-64 counterexample and $(52,679)$ in the order-128 example.

Now let $m_u$ be the actual normalized *ordered* pair histogram. The sequence $S_j=\sum_u m_u u^j$ in $\mathbb F_p$ has minimum constant-coefficient recurrence degree exactly the number of occupied bins. Every occupied weight is between 1 and $n^2<p$. Applying a polynomial $P$ in the shift multiplies each exponential weight by $P(u)$, and the Vandermonde matrix on distinct bins is nonsingular. Thus an annihilator must vanish at every occupied bin, and the product of their linear factors is an annihilator. Cauchy-Schwarz and the actual energy identity give $n^5\le |\mathrm{supp}(m)|E_3$. The centered bound implies $E_3\le15n^3$, because $n$ divides $E_3$ and $n^6/p<n$. In particular a successful case needs recurrence degree at least **76,861,433,640,456,466**. The new [Lean module](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/NormalizedTraceRecurrenceWidth.lean) verifies the minimum-degree theorem, actual energy inequality, and this production degree bound under the integer Wick ceiling. All six reported axiom sets, including the periodic-coefficient result below, are standard.

The necklace partition argument gives the sharper necessary degree **192,153,583,161,617,075**. The $D=(n^2+2)/6$ clean necklaces lose at most $C_{36}+C_{66}=7A_5+10A_6\le7(A_5+2A_6)$ distinct bins through collisions, since a bin holding $s$ necklaces loses $s-1\le\binom{s}2$. The singleton bin contributes one more. Substitute the required budget $A_5+2A_6\le2^{27}-1$. This sharper figure uses the written partition proof. The width statement concerns ordinary constant-coefficient recurrences for $S_j$; scalar recurrences for other sequences and algorithms using succinct representations are separate questions.

There is also an exact modular coefficient expression. Write $d=(p-1)/n$ and $A(Z)=\sum_{j=0}^d Z^j/(nj)!$ in $\mathbb F_p[Z]$. Then
$$
 E_3\equiv n^6\big(1+[Z^d]A(Z)^6\big)\pmod p,\qquad
 S_j=n^2(nj)![Z^j]A(Z)^3\quad(0\le j\le d).
$$
For the first identity, negate the three entries on the right and use the zero indicator $1-t^{p-1}$. Expansion and summation over the subgroup retain only exponent vectors all divisible by $n$; Wilson's theorem gives $(p-1)!=-1$. The second identity applies the same root filter to $(1+x+y)^{nj}$. The residue determines the *integer* $E_3$, since the first five entries determine the sixth and $n^5<p$ at both named primes. Direct truncation requires degree $2^{128}+192$ or $2^{129}+13$. Initial samples up to $L\le d$ can be batched by evaluating $\prod_{i=1}^n(X+i)$ at $0,n,\ldots,(L-1)n$, multiplying consecutive factorial blocks, and computing $A^3\bmod Z^{L+1}$ by polynomial multiplication. This produces short initial traces, while the degree bound above obstructs complete reconstruction by an ordinary short recurrence. The modular coefficient formulas were checked against literal triple counts at five small prime/group pairs.

Explicit binary powering in the normalized two-variable torus algebra has an independent quadratic intermediate: $(1+X+Y)^{n/2}$ already has $\binom{n/2+2}2=144115188881162241$ nonzero coefficients modulo $(X^n-1,Y^n-1,p)$. All its exponent pairs are distinct and each multinomial coefficient is nonzero since $n/2<p$. This is an obstacle to *materializing* the sparse convolution. An exact subquadratic scalar shortcut for the remaining coefficient, or a bound on the production $C_{36}+2C_{66}$, remains open. [Bostan–Gaudry–Schost on polynomial-coefficient recurrences and factorial blocks](https://doi.org/10.1137/S0097539704443793)

**Polynomial-coefficient recurrences on the finite coefficient window.** Let $D$ be the number of occupied nodes, all nonzero at these two primes. Suppose $\sum_{i=0}^r A_i(j)S_{j+i}=0$ for the first $D(b+1)$ offsets, where the $A_i\in\mathbb F_p[J]$ have degree at most $b<p$ and some $A_i$ is nonzero. Then $r\ge D$. Write the residual as $T_j=\sum_u m_u u^j Q_u(j)$, where $Q_u(J)=\sum_i A_i(J)u^i$. For the shift $E$, applying $E-u$ to $u^jQ(j)$ multiplies the shifted exponential by the finite difference $Q(j+1)-Q(j)$. Thus $\prod_u(E-u)^{b+1}$ kills $T$. This is a monic recurrence of order $D(b+1)$, so the initial zeros propagate to every offset. Choose a residue $a$ at which some $A_i(a)\ne0$, possible since $b<p$. At offsets $j=a+pk$ the coefficients are fixed and $u^{a+pk}=u^a u^k$. The Vandermonde equations for $k=0,\ldots,D-1$ force the nonzero polynomial $\sum_i A_i(a)X^i$ to vanish on all $D$ nodes.

At $n=2^{30}$ there are at most $n^2$ nodes. For any $r,b\le n^2$, $D(b+1)+r\le 2^{120}+2^{61}<2^{128}$. Both coefficient windows $0\le j\le(p-1)/n$ therefore contain enough offsets for this test, even after reserving the last $r$ terms for the shifts. Under the desired Wick bound the same degree floors above apply. The finite-window implication uses the written finite-difference proof. The new theorem `periodic_recurrence_degree` kernel-checks the all-offset implication with *arbitrary* coefficients depending on the residue of $j$ modulo $p$. [Lean theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/NormalizedTraceRecurrenceWidth.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · Six-term relations with two pairs in fourth-root cosets

For the first named production prime, all **2,147,483,643** projective signatures of clean triples having two entries in one coset of the fourth roots of unity are distinct and avoid zero and one. The third entry ranged over the whole order-$2^{30}$ subgroup. The exact GMP run reported `duplicate_tags=0 excess_field_values=0 zeros=0 ones=0`; low 64-bit limbs were already distinct. The second named prime has now completed the same scan with the identical output. Both fields therefore pass. Independent replay with `con-leche --verified` accepted 20,843 declarations covering the numerical-loop reduction, two-pair theorem, rectangle completion and pairing restrictions.

Write $H=\mu_n$, $i=g^{n/4}$. Up to scaling, the two rows are $\{1,1,x\}$ for $x\ne-1$ and $\{1,i,x\}$ for $x\notin\{1,-1,i,-i\}$. A repetition uniquely selects its normalization (unless all three entries coincide, with the same normalization). For a clean triple of three different elements, two directed quarter-turn edges would force opposite entries in the fourth-root coset. Hence each second-row orbit is represented exactly once. Opposite-pair triples form one additional signature and have uncancelled sum in $H$. The focused Lean kernel checked coverage, the implication from the literal two integer exponent loops to the field hypotheses, and the consequent pairing theorem for an actual zero sum. These three generic implications use only the standard axioms. The large numerical signature comparisons are external exact computations. An independent control enumerated the unordered triples over $\mathbb F_{97}$ with $n=16$ and canonicalized their translates: its 27 clean representatives had six signature values with multiplicities $2,7,4,6,5,3$, agreeing with the verifier on 21 excess copies and two unit signatures. [Lean reduction](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/QuarterPairTripleSignatures.lean) · [GMP scan](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_quarter_pair_triples.cpp)

At a passing field, a non-pairable six-term relation can contain at most one disjoint pair from a common fourth-root coset. In the six-distinct-antipodal-class case it uses at least five fourth-root cosets. In the five-class case its four non-repeated entries lie in pairwise different fourth-root cosets. These restrictions follow by splitting two disjoint pairs between the two triples, negating one side and applying the collision theorem. For the family of ordered triples with such a pair, the number of represented sums is $n(2n-4)=2305843004918726656$, the number of ordered triples is $n(12n-32)=13835058020922425344$, and the contribution to ordered sixth energy from comparisons in which **both** triples are in the family is $n(9n^2+27n-152)=11141460384697302935268229120$. Per common scale, one clean representative has weight one, $n-2$ have weight three, $n-4$ have weight six, and the singleton signature has weight $3(n-1)$. Comparisons with triples outside the family remain open.

## 2026-11-21 · A prime-averaged sixth-moment bound at the fifth-power scale

Let $n\ge2$ be a power of two, $H_p=\mu_n(\mathbb F_p)$ for $p\equiv1\pmod n$, and $E_0=15n^3-45n^2+40n$. For **every finite set of such primes**, without any repeated-entry tests,
$$
 \sum_p \big(E_3(H_p)-E_0\big)\log p\ \le\ \tfrac12 n^6\log6.
$$
The [previous FS6 count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_FS6AlmostAllPrimesWickRung.lean) bounded the number of Wick failures beyond $P$ by order $n^5\log n/\log P$. The new bound costs order $n^4/\log P$.

**Proof.** Take every ordered sextuple of exponents modulo $n$, with positive word polynomial $F(X)=\sum_{j=1}^6X^{e_j}$. Negating one half identifies zero words with equalities of two triple sums. For a primitive complex root $\zeta$, the words with $F(\zeta)=0$ are precisely the pairable words, since $\Phi_n=X^{n/2}+1$. They vanish at every split prime. Their number is $E_0$. For any remaining word its norm $N_F=|\prod_{u\in(\mathbb Z/n)^\times}F(\zeta^u)|$ is a positive integer. For a split prime $p$ let $r_p(F)$ count the primitive field roots where $F$ vanishes. Multiplication by $F$ on $\mathbb Z[X]/(\Phi_n)$ has determinant of magnitude $N_F$ and reduces to a diagonal operator in the evaluation basis modulo $p$. It has nullity $r_p(F)$, so Smith normal form gives $p^{r_p(F)}\mid N_F$. Thus $\sum_p r_p(F)\log p\le\log N_F$. At *each* primitive field root, the full exponent set parametrizes $H_p$ bijectively. Summing over the non-pairable words gives exactly $\sum_F r_p(F)=\varphi(n)(E_3(H_p)-E_0)$. This uses multiplicities of split places and allows a word to vanish at several places. [Determinant divisibility](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_G87CoverageDivisibility.lean)

For each complex primitive root consider all $n^6$ word sums $z=F(\zeta^u)$. Their squared magnitudes sum to $6n^6$ by independence of the six coordinates and the zero sum of a complete set of roots. For $z\ne0$, the tangent bound $2\log|z|\le\log6-1+|z|^2/6$ follows from $\log t\le t-1$. Assign a zero word log contribution zero. It satisfies the same bound since $\log6>1$. Summing over words and embeddings gives $\sum_F\log N_F\le\varphi(n)n^6\log6/2$ and cancels the factor $\varphi(n)$ from the modular incidences. Lean checked the literal six-word logarithmic bound for an arbitrary primitive complex root, its exact quadratic mean, and the finite production arithmetic below. The modular-root count and its combination with the mean are a written proof. [Lean certificates](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CyclotomicMeanLogMass.lean)

At a prime failing $E_3(H_p)\le15n^3$, the excess is at least $45n^2-39n$, since scaling the first coordinate freely makes both energies multiples of $n$. Hence the number of failures among **all split primes $p\ge P$** is at most $n^6\log6/[2(45n^2-39n)\log P]$. Setting $n=2^{30}$ and $P=2^{158}$ gives the certified rational upper bound **241669527060695186377734665249132**, using $6^{41}<2^{106}$. An independent exact enumeration over all possible split exceptions at $n=2,4,8,16$ counted $1,2,7,57$ primes with positive excess, respectively. It checked the split-place incidence multiplicities against direct ordered triple histograms at every such prime. [Independent verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_cross_prime_sixth_mass.py)

For growing dyadic $n$, the prime number theorem of Thorner–Zaman (Corollary 3.1, formula (3.2), fixed radical $2$) gives total split-prime logarithmic mass $(1+o(1))X/\varphi(n)$ in $(X,2X]$ for $X=Cn^5$ and fixed $C>0$, and also for $X=n^\beta$, $\beta>5$. Indeed $X/\varphi(n)$ exceeds $(2X)^{7/12+\epsilon}$ for some fixed $\epsilon>0$ in each case. Thus the **upper limiting fraction of Wick failures at $X=Cn^5$ is at most $\log6/(180C)$**. It tends to zero at every fixed $\beta>5$. This supplies a fifth-power-scale family result without assuming GRH. It leaves the sixth-energy values of the two particular production primes unresolved. [Thorner–Zaman paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.10878) · [Do Duc–Leung–Schmidt on norm methods for cyclotomic numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.07314)

## 2026-11-21 · Multiplicative rectangles and a complete order-64 implication

Suppose the four entries $1,r,q,rq$ of a zero-sum sextuple are in $H=\mu_n$ and $r,q\ne-1$. Their sum is $(1+r)(1+q)$. Write $P(x)=(1+x)^n$. A completion by two negative roots exists exactly when $P(r)P(q)=P(s)$ for some $s\in H$ with $s\ne-1$. In one direction normalize the last two roots by their first entry. In the other, the needed first entry is $(1+r)(1+q)/(1+s)$, whose $n$-th power is one. Four of the six entries in a paired relation must already contain an internal opposite pair. In odd characteristic the possible diagonals here are $q=-r$ or $rq=-1$. A completion along either diagonal pairs by the weak Sidon condition. Three pairs having the same ratio force ratio $-1$ by the doubled-sum exclusion and $(1+r)(a+b+c)=0$. The implications for completion, necessary diagonals, and the latter three-pair conclusion passed both the Lean kernel and the independent checker. [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SixthMomentMultiplicativeRectangle.lean)

At **every prime congruent to one modulo 64** the centered unit sixth-moment bound holds if the order-64 subgroup passes all three repeated-entry exclusions, the fourth-root-pair signature test and the nontrivial-rectangle exclusion. This follows by testing every exception from the complete norm classification. Of the 32 exceptions satisfying the three repeated-entry exclusions, 24 also pass the fourth-root-pair test. Exactly one passes the rectangle test, namely 518017, and none pass both. Each rectangle test compared the projective criterion independently with membership of the actual product in the full set of pair sums. The two stronger tests separately remain insufficient. For instance at 518017, the normalized rectangle count is zero, but $A_5=2$, $A_6=6$, the orbit budget is 13, and $E_3=4072960$. At 81818497 the fourth-root-pair test passes and the sixth bound still fails. The all-primes conclusion is a mathematical covering argument with exact external finite checks at order 64. Its quantifier does not extend to larger subgroup orders. [Classification and both tests](../../../scripts/probes/probe_order64_sixth_moment_classification.py)

**An independent rank proof strengthens the finite bound.** On the free module with basis $(\mathbb Z/n)^5$, let $U_i$ shift the $i$th coordinate and $M=I+U_1+\cdots+U_5$. Put $D=n^5$. Over $\mathbb C$ and each split $\mathbb F_p$, tensor products of the one-coordinate Fourier eigenbases diagonalize $M$. The eigenvalues are $1+x_1+\cdots+x_5$ on the full grid of $n$th roots. Normalizing the first of six entries to one shows the two nullities are $E_0/n$ and $E_3(H_p)/n$. Choose a nonzero integer minor $B$ of order $k=D-E_0/n$, the rational rank. Its rank modulo $p$ is at most $D-E_3(H_p)/n$, hence its determinant has at least $(E_3(H_p)-E_0)/n$ factors of $p$ by Smith normal form. Every row of $M$ has six distinct unit entries, so every restricted row of $B$ has squared Euclidean norm at most six. Hadamard gives $|\det B|\le 6^{k/2}$. A single integer minor works for all split primes simultaneously. It follows that
$$
 \sum_p (E_3(H_p)-E_0)\log p\ \le\ (n^6-E_0)\log6/2 .
$$
This argument also applies to any even $n$ when $E_0$ is taken to be its actual complex third energy. At the production $n=2^{30}$ it lowers the certified rational cap on Wick failures above $2^{158}$ to **241669527060695186377734662320845**. The minor construction is a written proof. [Hadamard in Lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/_G86HadamardSupportSix.lean) · [Refined arithmetic certificate](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CyclotomicMeanLogMass.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · Exact rate-half MCA threshold for a production-length prime field

For $n=2^{30}=4h$, $k=n/2$, and any $n$ distinct evaluation points, the number of MCA-bad scalars at $h-1$ errors is at most **268,435,458**. At $h$ errors two polynomial pairs give **536,870,914** distinct bad scalars. Thus at $128$-bit security the first bad error count is **268,435,456** for every field with $268435458\le\lfloor |F|/2^{128}\rfloor\le536870913$. This includes the new smooth-domain instance $p=91343853369979269099964682909737195747164553217=17179869379\cdot2^{122}+1$, whose budget is one above the lower endpoint. The prime $91343853062927602073649116268957764822404956161$ attains the lower endpoint exactly. The earlier integral BCHKS numerator at this predecessor was 357,913,945. The designated primes whose budgets are $n$ and $2n$ have separate threshold questions.

The new upper bound is a written proof for arbitrary received pairs. More than $e+1$ decodable folds force the whole syndrome pencil to have a generic apolar locator $D$ of bidegree $(l,r)$, with $r\le e=h-1$. Its moment functional gives a section $W$ of degree $r-(d-2r+2)l\ge0$ on $D$, where $d=n-k-1$. At every bad scalar $\gamma$, the product of $W$ with the domain vanisher $K$ is zero on the entire locator fiber. ## Arbitrary evaluation domains · multiplicity of failed interpolation checks

For any finite field, any injective evaluation domain of length n, and any received pair, suppose every eligible agreement set has at least t>0 coordinates. If M is the number of affine-line MCA-bad scalars for the dimension-k Reed–Solomon code, then **M·choose(t−1,k) ≤ choose(n,k+1)**. This counts the literal MCA event, including received pairs that already admit some joint agreement. The bound applies to every smooth prime-field domain.

Fix a coordinate x in a witness S on which the second row fails interpolation. Each k-subset T of S minus x determines a failed (k+1)-check. If the row fails on T plus x, use that set. Otherwise choose a failure y of its unique fitted codeword and use T plus y. In the second case two different omitted positions would force the two fitted codewords to agree on x and the other k−1 positions, hence coincide on the whole check. This proves injectivity. Checks produced for distinct bad scalars are disjoint because subtracting two interpolants on the same check would supply a second-row interpolant there. The density factor is sharp for a witness with one erroneous coordinate. At t=k+2 the resulting uniform ceiling is floor(choose(n,k+1)/(k+1)). For fixed positive code rate and a fixed positive error fraction, the quotient can still be exponential in n. The full prime-field proximity gap remains open. Both counting declarations passed focused Lean verification with only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound. [Circuit density](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CircuitDensity.lean)

## Prime-field line MCA at fixed capacity slack

The earlier exact list thresholds for particular codes do not determine the general prime-field MCA radius. The canonical DKT capacity theorem does handle every prescribed domain without a character-sum estimate. For fixed slack g > 0 and n ≥ N(g), its line bound at radius 1 − k/n − g is C(g)n^(d(g)+1)/p. In a prime field an evaluation embedding gives k ≤ n ≤ p, so the characteristic condition follows. The affine-space version has denominator p−1 independently of the number of directions.

[PrimeFieldCapacityGap.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldCapacityGap.lean) now specializes the theorem to every prime-field domain and derives the 128-bit line guarantee at every smaller radius when 2^128 C(g)n^(d(g)+1) ≤ p. Both declarations and the three underlying capacity declarations passed the Lean kernel axiom audit with only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound. This is a sufficient field-size test. It does not identify the first failing radius or supply the tighter exceptional counts needed when p/2^128 is only of order n. [DKT paper](https://eprint.iacr.org/2026/2056)

The rank bound can now be checked without determinant multiplicities. Lean selects a full square minor of the preceding injective catalecticant and a parameter where the minor is regular. After normalizing there, kernels at distinct parameters belong to distinct eigenspaces of one linear map. Their total dimension is at most the matrix size. The verified counting theorem then combines sparse error moments, ordinary polynomial root counts and both orders of incidence counting to give $|S|r'\le |H'|l+r'$. The competing fixed branch now cancels an arbitrary nonzero scalar parameter factor in the polynomial moment equations before applying the code-level bound. ### DEEP transfer from scalar list size to MCA on an arbitrary finite-field domain

For an injective evaluation map of size $n$ into a field of size $q$, suppose a received word has at least $L+1$ Reed–Solomon codewords of dimension $k+1$ at $t>k$ agreements. If $k\binom{L+1}{2}<(q-n)(L+1-B)$, there is a stack with more than $B$ actual MCA-bad challenges for dimension $k$ at the same agreement threshold. The proof averages the deficit in the number of distinct evaluations over all off-domain points. Each pair of distinct source polynomials collides at most $k$ times. At the chosen point $z$, the two rows are $u(x)/(x-z)$ and $1/(x-z)$. The divided difference for each source polynomial gives a folded codeword, and a degree-below-$k$ polynomial cannot match the second row on its witness. This removes the simultaneous-separation assumption from the dimension-one descent. The new generic theorem was kernel checked with only standard axioms. [Lean transfer](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/DeepListMcaTransfer.lean)

## Exact endpoint test over arbitrary prescribed domains

Put D = 2^128 and let q be the field size. For 1 ≤ k < n and q < D², every injective domain has operational line-MCA supremum one **if and only if** D·binom(n,k+1) ≤ q. This covers smooth cosets uniformly. In the opposite case the radius 1−(k+1)/n already fails. The proof decides whether the entire interval is secure. When the binomial test fails it does not identify the first failing step in the interior.

The same equivalence holds for any positive integer security denominator D and at any radius requiring exactly k+1 agreements. Write N = binom(n,k+1) and B = floor(q/D). The existing second-moment argument gives a stack with Nq ≤ M(q+N−1), where M is its number of bad scalars. Since B² < q, this inequality forces M > B whenever N > B. The circuit upper bound handles N ≤ B. Equality of the canonical and campaign MCA errors and the radius-one plateau put both directions on the canonical code. No assumption on the square of N is needed. [BoundarySecurityBudget.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/BoundarySecurityBudget.lean)

The prime-field capacity result above also now handles every positive block length. Below N(g), the exceptional count is at most 2^n by witness-set pinning. At or above N(g), the DKT polynomial bound applies. Both branches and the boundary equivalences were compiled in Lean with only the three standard axioms.

## 2026-11-21 · The logarithmic factor is necessary on dyadic prime-field subgroups

For arbitrarily large powers of two $n$, there is a prime $p$ with $6^n<p\le 6^{2n}$, a subgroup $H\le\mathbb F_p^\times$ of order $n$, and a nonzero frequency $b$ with $|\eta_b|>n/2$. These examples satisfy $n^2\le p$. In particular, $|\eta_b|>\sqrt{n\log_2(p/n)}/5$. A uniform estimate with $o(\log(p/n))$ in place of the logarithm under the square root therefore fails, even when the order is dyadic. The proposed upper estimate with the full logarithm and the MCA thresholds in the interior remain open.

For a domain covered by $d$ signed representatives, partition the $p$ frequencies according to the six equal bins containing their $d$ phases. If $p>6^d$, two frequencies share a signature. At their nonzero difference every phase has real part greater than one half. To synchronize prime and order, take a prime divisor $p$ of $2^{2^j}+1$ for large $j$. Its congruence modulo $2^{j+1}$ forces $p\to\infty$. Let $n$ be the largest power of two with $6^n<p$. The upper bound on the Fermat number gives $n\mid p-1$, and maximality gives $p\le6^{2n}$. The proof uses elementary prime divisors and yields an explicit exponential window as both the order and field grow. Lean 4.35 verified the two-sided window, the nonzero period, and the lower bound at the exact logarithmic scale. [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldPeriodAlignment.lean) · [Duke, Garcia and Lutz, Theorem 6.3, for fixed-order torus density](https://arxiv.org/abs/1212.6825)

The reciprocal parameter chart now works directly over the coordinate polynomial ring. ## Exact arbitrary-domain radius in an integer budget interval

For every field of size q < D², dimension 1 ≤ k < n, and every injective evaluation domain, put N = binom(n,k+1), B = floor(q/D). Assume floor(N/(k+1)) ≤ B < N. Then the complete affine-line MCA security predicate at nonnegative radius δ is exactly (1−δ)n > k+1. Its operational supremum is 1−(k+1)/n, and this boundary radius fails. On the safe side a witness needs k+2 points and at least k+1 distinct detecting circuits. On the failure side the second-moment bound forces at least B+1 bad scalars and the error is constant through radius one. Both the predicate and the supremum were kernel verified. For fixed field and length all smooth cosets have identical canonical MCA errors at every radius, dimension, and generator. Dividing a domain by its coset representative gives the entire subgroup of n-th roots of unity, since it contains n roots and Xⁿ−1 has at most n roots. Changing the coset only changes the polynomial variable by a unit and permutes the coordinates. This removes coset choice and ordering from a worst-case proof. ### An isolated Fourier peak survives the production fourth, sixth and eighth moment tests on symmetric sets

At each of the two designated primes there exists a genuine antipodal subset $D\subset\mathbb F_p^\times$, $|D|=n=2^{30}$, with $\eta_1\ge n/2-4\sqrt{n\ln p}$ and $|\eta_b|\le4\sqrt{n\ln p}$ for every $b\notin\{0,\pm1\}$. Its fourth additive moment is exactly $E_2(D)=3n(n-1)$, and $E_3(D)\le15n^3$, $E_4(D)\le105n^4$. One can additionally require $aD\cap bD=\varnothing$ for $1\le a<b\le2^{20}$ and $D\cap2^jD=\varnothing$ for $1\le j\le160$. Thus even simultaneous control at *every other* nontrivial frequency, the three energy tests, and the tested multiplicative disjointness properties permit a peak of linear size. Full multiplicative closure is essential to distinguish a smooth subgroup from this example.

For the written existence proof, choose $m=n/2$ independent anchors $X_i$ with mass $(1+\cos(2\pi x/p))/p$ at $x$, and take all $\pm X_i$. Orthogonality gives expected period $n/2$ at $\pm1$ and zero at every other nonzero frequency. Each anchor contributes a real summand in $[-2,2]$. Hoeffding's inequality and a union bound give exceptional probability at most $2p^{-3}$ for the claimed simultaneous deviations. Zero anchors or coincidences up to sign cost at most $2n^2/p$. For a labeled signed word of length $2r$ whose formal coefficients of the independent anchors are not all zero, condition on all but one anchor with a nonzero coefficient. The probability its sum vanishes is at most $2/p$, since $2r<p$. There are at most $n^{2r}$ words. For $r=2$, excluding every such relation costs at most $2n^4/p$; the three pairings then give exactly $3n(n-1)$ solutions by inclusion-exclusion. For $r=3,4$, the pairable labeled words consume at most $(2r-1)!!n^r-n^{r-1}$ solutions. The pairing count overcounts at least the $n^{r-1}$ words beginning $(x,-x,x,-x)$ and continuing in opposite pairs. Markov's inequality bounds the chance of exhausting this slack by $2n^{r+1}/p$. For the multiplicative tests, consider each forbidden ratio and ordered pair of signed anchors; the same one-variable bound costs at most $2n^2(2^{40}+160)/p$ in total, after removing zero anchors. No tested double power is $\pm1$ at either prime. Summing the exceptional probabilities gives less than $1/64$. These sets cannot be subgroup cosets, since every multiplier in their stabilizer replicates the peak at another frequency. This probabilistic existence proof has not been formalized in Lean.

### Three almost saturated cores exclude every earlier second-prime witness

At the second production prime set $n=2^{30}=3r+1$, $k=2^{26}$ and $d=k-1$. Suppose the received row pair has **three distinct polynomial head pairs whose joint cores have total size at least $3t-15$**, where $t=n-e$ and $e\le2r-1$. Then the entire row pair has at most $2n$ bad scalars. This includes scalars decoded by other polynomials. In particular, enlarging the previous three-core construction by arbitrarily many further heads cannot produce an earlier violation while those original cores each have size at least $t-5$.

Choose one folding polynomial and bad witness for each scalar. A point $(\gamma,P)$ lies on head $(A,B)$ if $P=A+\gamma B$. A point outside a given head agrees with the fold on at most $d$ positions in its joint core, by polynomial root counting. Write $h$ for the number of sites outside all three cores. If the three head pairs are noncollinear over $F(X)$, their nonzero determinant has degree at most $2d$. A site in $m$ cores charges at least $m-1$ to its vanishing multiplicity. Thus $h\le n-3t+15+2d$. Every point outside the three lines has at most $3d+h<t$ agreements, since $4t>n+5d+15$. The three lines together carry at most $3(e+1)\le 2n-2$ scalars.

If the head pairs are collinear over $F(X)$, pair intersections give $h\le n-3t+15+3d$. The three lines together contribute at most $n+2h$ distinct labels. At any occupied site, specialized collinearity permits at most one genuinely fresh scalar across these lines, and every unoccupied site supplies at most three. Each selected point outside these lines agrees on at least $t-3d$ of the $h$ sites. Three outside points consequently agree together on at least $3(t-3d)-2h>d$ sites. Their interpolation determinant has degree at most $d$, so the outside points all lie on a single fourth line as soon as there are at least two. At the limiting $t=r+2=357913943$, the common-site lower bound is $67108864=d+1$. It increases with $t$.

To violate $2n$, this fourth line would have to hold more than $n-2h\ge671088626$ labels. A line with core size $z\le t-2$ has at most $1+\lfloor e/2\rfloor\le357913941$ labels from disjoint fresh agreements. Hence the fourth core would have size at least $t-1$. The four cores would now have total incidence $I\ge4t-16>n+9d/2$, forcing rational collinearity of all four heads by the earlier four-head determinant bound. Their union has size at least $I-6d>2n/3$, so all four together provide fewer than $2n$ labels. This gives a necessary condition for an earlier violation even when the proposed additional witnesses have millions of fresh agreements.

### Four below-capacity failures at production length, and a failure of reverse transfer

A polynomial map of degree $m$ with exactly $m$ evaluation sites above each base site transports every base MCA bad scalar into the code of dimension $mk$ at the same relative radius. On the base witness $S$, interpolate a row as $p$ of degree below $|S|$. If $|S|>k$, an upstairs polynomial $Q$ of degree below $mk$ agreeing with the pulled-back row on every lift coincides with $p\circ P$ on $m|S|$ distinct sites. The degree bound forces polynomial equality, so $p$ has degree below $k$. For $|S|\le k$ direct interpolation suffices. This proves joint refusal on the lifted witness and requires no loss of rate. The power image of every smooth coset of length $mh$ is again smooth of length $h$ when $h$ is dyadic. [Polynomial fiber transfer](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FullFiberMcaLift.lean) · [Power projection on arbitrary smooth cosets](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothPowerMcaDescent.lean)

In the field-size interval of the preceding length-128 result, **every smooth domain of size $512r$**, including $2^{30}$, violates the 128-bit target at or above the radii **$31/64,95/128,111/128,239/256$** at rates $1/2,1/4,1/8,1/16$, respectively. These are upper bounds on the operational suprema at large lengths. All four are strictly below the corresponding capacities. The proof lifts prescribed-product witnesses on smooth base lengths 128, 256, 256 and 512. The bounds hold for every positive interleaving width. At the three lower rates the actual length-128 power image remains within budget through radius one while arbitrary stacks upstairs violate that same budget. Thus a reverse implication from safety on this quotient to safety upstairs fails, including at the common Hamming-grid points $95/128,111/128,120/128$. The new rate and strict-separation statements are kernel checked with the standard axioms. [All four production bounds and reverse counterexample](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourRateSmoothMcaLift.lean)

### A checked upper bound for every consecutive string of doubled frequencies

For any antipodal $n$-point subset of $\mathbb F_p$ and $b\ne0$, write $A_j=|\eta_{2^jb}|$. If $p\le2^{m+1}$, then for every integer $t\ge0$,
$$
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1} A_j + tn \ \le\ mn + 2\sum_{u=0}^{t-1}\binom{m}{u}(t-u).
$$
At the first designated prime, every chain of 158 frequencies has mean at most $2^{30}-5316069514/158$. At the second, every chain of 159 has mean at most $2^{30}-5314729034/159$. If the domain is closed under doubling, the same bound holds for each individual frequency because the periods in a chain are identical. This condition fails on both designated subgroups. All four declarations, including the finite-field character evaluation, passed the Lean kernel and standard-axiom audit. [Lean upper bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldDoublingChain.lean)

The first $m+1$ binary digits of $bx/p$ identify the field element $x$. Its $j$th digit transition records whether $\cos(2\pi\,2^j bx/p)$ is nonpositive. Choose the preferred transition in column $j$ from the sign of the real period. Every mismatch contributes at least one to $n-A_j$. The initial bit and the set of mismatches recover the word, so at most $2\binom m u$ points have $u$ mismatches. Summing the deficit below $t$ yields the bound. The separate symmetric-set example above gives a large isolated coefficient in spite of small neighbors and simultaneous moment control.

### What the period field discriminant and first two traces determine

Let $H\le\mathbb F_p^\times$ have even order $n$, put $K=\mathbb Q(\zeta_p)^H$, and let $\eta_c=\sum_{x\in cH}\zeta_p^x$ with $c$ ranging over multiplicative cosets. If an algebraic integer $\alpha\in\mathcal O_K$ has $\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha)=-1$ and $\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^2)=p-n$, then $\alpha$ is one of the actual Gaussian periods. Indeed $\zeta_p,\ldots,\zeta_p^{p-1}$ form an integral basis of $\mathbb Z[\zeta_p]$ permuted by $H$. Invariance forces coefficients constant on each coset, hence the $\eta_c$ are an integral basis of $\mathcal O_K$. Each has trace $-1$. Expanding the product and summing over all dilations gives $\operatorname{Tr}(\eta_c\eta_d)=p\,\mathbf1_{c=d}-n$, since $-H=H$. Writing $\alpha=\sum_c z_c\eta_c$ gives $\sum_c z_c=1$ and $p\sum_c z_c^2-n=p-n$. An integral vector with sum and squared length both one is a positive coordinate vector. Thus the first two traces within the specified period field recover the orbit exactly. Estimating its largest real embedding still requires further information. This is a written argument.

The cubic obstruction now checks as a single theorem. On a normalized length-$n$ domain, a moving primitive locator of coordinate degree $r$ and parameter degree $l$ with minimal apolar syndrome through degree $d$ has at most $3(l+1)$ selected sparse fibers if $n<3(d+2-r)+r$ and those fibers use at most $d-r+1$ sites. The formal statement takes generic coprimality; minimal apolarity implies it by the checked companion theorem. Its proof constructs the cubic interpolation polynomial in each of four charts, compares the coordinate degrees, and uses the strictly negative parameter twist to force divisibility of the fixed domain vanisher. Both the cardinality theorem and the coprimality input passed the pinned Lean kernel with standard axioms.
