## Second-prime quadratic bound with local cancellation at every evaluation coordinate

For $n=2^{30}$, $d=2^{26}-1$, and prime characteristic $p>2n$, the quadratic interpolation method gives a safe integer allowance $e=648719018$ at budget $2n$. Thus the second prime has supremum at least $648719019/n$. This advances the earlier quadratic bound by 148 coordinates. The proof below refines the [quadratic discriminant argument](second-prime-quadratic-discriminant.md). It is a written proof with exact numerical comparisons.

Set $t=425022806$ and $h=187904820$. The quadratic dictionary $j\le2,\ i+jd<t,\ j+\ell\le h$ has $3ht-(3h-2)d=n(h+1)+2$ monomials. Write the interpolant as $aY^2+bY+c$. Every selected candidate $P_z$ satisfies the polynomial identity $aP_z^2+bP_z+c=0$ upon specializing $Z=z$.

For $a\ne0$, homogenize in the coordinate variable to degrees $t-1-jd$ for each coefficient of $Y^j$. The binary discriminant $\Delta=b^2-4ac$ has even binary degree $2(t-1-d)$ and parameter degree at most $2h$. Every witness specializes it to a binary square, by homogenizing $P_z$ to degree $d$. If $\Delta\ne0$, remove square factors and vertical factors to write $\Delta=s(Z)V^2H$, with $H$ squarefree and homogeneous in the two coordinate variables, and without any parameter-only factor. Its binary degree $r$ is even. If $r>0$, the two binary first partials have nonzero Sylvester resultant over $\bar F(Z)$, since $p>r$ and $H$ is squarefree there. Except where $s(z)=0$ or $V_z$ vanishes identically, $H_z$ is a binary square. Its linear factors show that its partials have gcd degree at least $r/2$. If $H_z=0$ the entire Sylvester matrix is zero instead. The determinant thus acquires vanishing order at least $r/2$ per such witness; its parameter degree is at most $(2r-2)\deg_Z H$. Counting all three classes gives at most $\deg_Zs+\deg_ZV+(4-4/r)\deg_ZH\le8h=1503238560$ labels.

If $r=0$, use the component equation $2aP_z=\lambda V-b$, with $\lambda^2=s(z)$, as in the earlier proof. This includes $\Delta=0$ by using $V=0$. Discard at most $h$ labels where $a(X,z)\equiv0$. For constant $s$ there are at most two signs, and for nonconstant squarefree $s$ there is one irreducible component. From $2n+1$ putative bad scalars, select a component with exactly $M=979789415$ or $1959578829$ labels respectively. Use $D=h+1$ or $2h+2$ respectively as the root bound for nonidentical relations on that component.

At every fixed domain coordinate $x$ let $j$ be the multiplicity of $X-x$ in $a(X,Z)$ over $\bar F(Z)$. The coefficients of $(X-x)^0,\ldots,(X-x)^{j-1}$ in $\lambda V-b$ vanish identically on the component. Indeed each evaluates to zero at all $M>D$ selected labels, while a nonzero such coefficient has at most $D$ labels, by ordinary root counting or the quadratic norm. Compare coefficients of $(X-x)^j$ in the fiber identity. This gives $2a_x(z)P_z(x)=v_x(z,\lambda)$, where $a_x$ is a **nonzero** polynomial of parameter degree at most $h$, and $v_x$ is the corresponding coefficient in $\lambda V-b$. This comparison remains valid at every selected label, including roots of $a_x$. It recovers the incidence test on all $n$ coordinates. The case $a=0,\ b\ne0$ uses the same argument with $bP_z=-c$, one component and no square root. If $b=0$ also, there are at most $h$ labels.

Let $U$ consist of sites where $2a_x(Z)(u(x)+Zv(x))=v_x(Z,\lambda)$ identically on the component. Outside $U$ at most $D$ labels agree per site; on $U$ at most $h$ selected labels miss each site. Hence $|U|\ge (Mt-Dn)/(M-D)>271089477$. A random pair of distinct selected points shares at least $|U|(1-2h/M)$ agreements on $U$ on average. Choose a pair attaining this bound and let $z$ be its number of shared sites in $U$. These force the joint core of their secant. Every point outside the secant must miss at least $z-d$ of those $z$ sites, and across all labels there are at most $hz$ such misses. Thus $M\le e+1+hz/(z-d)$. For the constant-sign case $z>167109951$ gives $hz/(z-d)<314004237$, whereas $M-e-1=331070396$. For the nonsquare case $z>219099714$ gives $hz/(z-d)<270870860$ and $M-e-1=1310859810$. Both contradict the putative violation. The linear case has at least as many available labels and the smaller nonidentity-site cap.

### Dimension of the common interpolation space improves the moving-cubic floor

At the second production prime and rate $1/16$, the moving-cubic argument and a dimension comparison give the written bound
$$
664797187/1073741824\ \le\ \delta^*\ \le\ 715827882/1073741824.
$$
Set $n=1073741824$, $d=67108863$, $t=408944638$ and $e=n-t$. Suppose $2n+1$ distinct bad scalars have been selected. All polynomials below use coordinate weight one for $X$, weight $d$ for $Y$ and zero for the scalar variable $\Gamma$. Weight below $t$ makes every interpolant vanish identically on each selected polynomial witness when the scalar is specialized.

An interpolant of total $(\Gamma,Y)$-degree at most 9 and $Y$-degree at most 3 exists. Its four rows of coefficients have $10,9,8,7$ choices of scalar exponent, so the dimension surplus is $34t-46d-10n=79691754$. If every positive-$Y$ factor had degree at most two in $Y$, the preceding small-factor bounds would exclude the given witness count: there are at most three affine heads, with $3(e+1)$ labels in total, at most three nonlinear factors with at most 4128 labels each, and at most nine parameter-only labels. These generous bounds sum to less than $2n$. Therefore there is one irreducible cubic-in-$Y$ factor $F$ over $\overline{\mathbb F}_p(X)$, and all other factors of this interpolant are independent of $Y$. At least $2n+1-9$ selected points lie on $F$.

Now take the entire vector space of interpolation polynomials of total $(\Gamma,Y)$-degree at most seven and coordinate weight below $t$. The possible $Y$-degrees are $0,\ldots,6$. There are $35t-77d$ coefficients and at most $8n$ linear conditions, so its dimension is at least **555745287**. Every one of its members vanishes on the same selected points. By plane Bézout over $\overline{\mathbb F}_p(X)$, each nonzero member is divisible by $F$, since at most $7\cdot9$ intersection points would otherwise lie on $F$. Take an integral representative primitive over $\overline{\mathbb F}_p[X]$; Gauss's lemma makes the quotients integral polynomials.

If $F$ had total $(\Gamma,Y)$-degree at least six, every quotient would have total degree at most one in those variables and weight below $t-3d$, since $F$ has weight at least $3d$. The $1,\Gamma,Y$ coefficient slots then give dimension at most **$2(t-3d)+(t-4d)=555745284$**, three below the proved lower bound. Thus $F$ has total degree at most five. The [moving-cubic derivative and inverse-Taylor argument](second-prime-moving-cubic.md) now applies. Its nonzero-derivative cap is at most $21+32d=2147483637$, below the $2147483640$ selected points on $F$. In the identically-zero case either the factor is a single affine head or there are at most $5(n-d)/(t-d)<15$ points there. These contradictions prove goodness at $e=664797186$ errors. Hamming-step constancy gives the lower endpoint above. The decisive dimension comparison is $32t>8n+67d$, whose first integer solution is the stated $t$. This does not require a degree-six cubic derivative bound. The dimension and endpoint comparison here are written mathematics, not a new kernel-verification claim.

At the final predecessor of the second-prime rate-sixteenth bracket, any putative family of $2n+1$ bad scalars must have selected points of affine dimension at least **10,526,872** over the prime field. For a polynomial span of dimension $r$, a Wronskian equation of order $r$ replaces the universal order-$d+1$ derivative in the moving-factor argument. A nonzero differential numerator allows at most $80+136(r-1)$ labels across the quartic/degree-twelve common factors. Identically zero numerators yield polynomial sections over the algebraic parameter field, where the head or small-incidence alternative applies. The previous one-186-point-secant obstruction controls all head contributions. The result is a necessary condition from a written proof. [Wronskian calculation and integer bound](second-prime-quadratic-discriminant.md)

### Sharp incidence bound for four polynomial heads

Let four distinct Reed–Solomon heads be pairs of polynomials of degree at most $d$, and suppose three are noncollinear over the rational function field. Write $C_i$ for sites where the received pair agrees with both coordinates of head $i$. On every injective evaluation domain over any field,
\[
 \sum_i|C_i|+|C_0\cap C_1\cap C_2\cap C_3|\le |C_0\cup C_1\cup C_2\cup C_3|+4d.
\]
One evaluation determinant with columns $1,A,B,Q(A,B)$, for $Q=A^2,AB$ or $B^2$, is nonzero. At a site occupied by two, three or four heads its vanishing order is at least one, two or four, respectively. Its degree is at most $4d$. The earlier four-head bound allowed $9d/2$. The new weighted bound is sharp on every domain: take a degree-$d$ vanisher $P$ on chosen sites and heads $(0,0),(P,0),(0,P),(P,P)$ for the zero received pair.

If a folded polynomial agrees at $t$ sites and $t+\sum_i|C_i|+|\bigcap_i C_i|>n+8d$, it equals one of these four heads at its scalar. An outside polynomial has at most $d$ agreements inside each core, and the determinant controls the complement of their union. This is a uniform geometric constraint on potential MCA violations. Producing sufficiently large heads for an arbitrary bad-scalar family remains necessary to obtain an unconditional threshold this way. The focused canonical build and five axiom audits passed. [Formal determinant, extremizer, and witness cover](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourHeadCoreDeterminant.lean)

### An actual sparse prime field rules out binary-log constant two at mixed order

For $p=\Phi_{36960}(2)$ and $H=\langle2\rangle$ of order $n=36960$, the frequency-one period has real part at least **33750.63425202**, strictly exceeding $2\sqrt{n\log_2(p/n)}$. The 7681-bit prime satisfies $n^2\le p$. An exact rational cosine bound certified the separation, and an independently replayed 304-step primality certificate returned `PRIME`. This constrains the constant for the sparse **all-subgroup-order** statement. The order contains $3,5,7,11$, so it falls outside ABF Definition 2.12, which requires a power of two. A general binary-block inequality shows how sufficiently large prime factors of suitable cyclotomic values would rule out every constant at mixed orders. Their required size remains unproved. [Finite and conditional proofs](sparse-cyclotomic-prime-period.md) · [Exact interval check](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sparse_cyclotomic_period.py)

### Worst-center list security can pass while production-length MCA fails beyond Johnson

At rate one eighth on **every** length-$2^{30}$ domain in the prime field $p=459\cdot2^{150}+1$, the two closed tests give different 128-bit verdicts at **every integer error count from 641,728,513 to 694,116,763**, inclusive. At the last point $t=379625061$ and $t^2<n(k-1)$. The uniform punctured list cap is 1,660,990,722 at every interleaving width. A common locator of degree 32,566,678 and three private blocks of 347,058,382 sites give at least 2,082,350,292 bad line-MCA scalars. The budget is 1,925,185,536. An earlier choice of the three blocks already gives 1,925,185,542 bad scalars at the left endpoint. The kernel checks a Lucas certificate with witness 5, the existence of a smooth dyadic domain in this field, and both probability comparisons. The last-point separation also holds on every domain for every prime with budget from 1,660,990,722 through 2,082,350,291. This rules out inferring same-radius MCA security from worst-center list security even strictly beyond Johnson at production length. The reverse implication supplied by the same-domain list-to-MCA reduction is consistent with these bounds. [Production proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProductionBeyondJohnsonListMca.lean) · [List-to-MCA reduction](same-domain-list-to-mca.md)

At length 64, rate one eighth, the exact **first failing error counts are 54 for line MCA and 56 for every positive-width worst-center list**, on every smooth evaluation domain of either originally nominated prime. These two cutoffs hold throughout the integer budget interval $612012989\le\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor\le2366768981$. At 55 errors the exact MCA numerator is 27,540,584,512 in both nominated primes, while every interleaved list has at most 491,796,152 words. More generally at $k+1$ agreements, if $q\ge\binom n{k+1}^2$, the worst MCA numerator attains $\binom n{k+1}$ and is at least $(n-k)$ times the packing upper bound $\lfloor\binom nk/(k+1)\rfloor$ for any list. [Exact thresholds and general ratio](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/BoundaryListMcaSeparation.lean)

## Exact half-budget thresholds on arbitrary prime-field domains

Write $B=\lfloor p/Q\rfloor$ and $j=\lfloor B/2\rfloor$. For every injective length-$n$ Reed–Solomon domain in a prime field, the operational MCA radius is exactly $j/n$ if $n\ge4096$, $k\ge2$, $8k\le n$, $n-k\le2j$, $64j\le31n$ and $Q\ge1$. At $n=2^{30}$ and $k=n/8$, this gives $\lfloor B/2\rfloor/n$ for every budget from $939524096$ through $1040187393$, inclusive. The predecessor is safe for all received pairs, and a two-head construction exceeds the budget at $j$. [Finite and asymptotic theorems](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldTwoLinePhase.lean)

For sequences with $n\to\infty$, $k/n\to\rho>0$, $p/n\to\lambda$ and fixed $Q\ge2$, put $a=\lambda/(2Q)>0$. The same finite formula holds eventually, and the radius tends to $a$, whenever $\rho+a<1$, $1-\rho<2a$, $\rho<(1-a)^3$ and $1+3\rho<3(1-a)$. At rate $1/8$ this includes $7/8<\lambda/Q<1$. At rate $1/16$ it includes $15/16<\lambda/Q<2(1-2^{-4/3})$. In these ranges the threshold exceeds half the relative minimum distance in the limit. A third-moment count locates a large line inside each sufficiently large family, and bounds on intersections of common agreement sets exclude a third such line. [All-pair bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/GeneralTwoLineSafety.lean) · [Limiting inequalities](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/GeneralTwoLineArithmetic.lean)

Over **every finite field** of order $q$, whenever $k\ge2$, $n\le k+2e$ and $k+e+1\le n$, a received pair on any prescribed domain has at least $\min(q,2(e+1))$ bad scalars. This removes the field-size condition from the two-head failure test. In particular the worst bad probability equals one when the construction fills the field. [Two-head witness and budget ceiling](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/UniversalTwoHeadBudget.lean) · [Overlapping private sites](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/OptimalOverlapPacking.lean)

On smooth prime-field domains the same counting argument gives further exact phases. Fix $r\ge1$, put $a=\lambda/(Qr)$, and let $E=\binom{r+1}{2}$. Under $0<a<1-\rho$, $r(1-\rho-a)<1-\rho$, $\rho<(1-a)^3$, $1+\binom{r+1}{2}\rho<(r+1)(1-a)$ and $r E(E-1)<\lambda$, the first failing index eventually equals $\lfloor B/r\rfloor$. At an error sequence with limit $e/n\to a$ the corresponding exact worst bad probability is eventually $r(e+1)/p$. The upper bound applies on arbitrary domains. The matching construction here uses smoothness. These strict inequalities leave rates and budgets outside this classification. [General family count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/GeneralManyLineSafety.lean) · [Exact smooth phases](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothManyLinePhase.lean)

At $n=2^{30}$ and integer 128-bit budget $2^{31}$, all dimensions $3\le k\le39768172$ have the same exact cutoff $715827882/n$ on every injective domain over every eligible finite field. The adjacent worst-case bad counts are $2147483646$ and $2147483649$. [Finite three-line classification](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LowDimensionThreeLinePhase.lean)

For any injective evaluation map $x:I\hookrightarrow F$ and label set $V\subset F$, some nonzero $t$ makes every sum $x_i+t v$ distinct as a pair $(i,v)$ after discarding $h$ sites, whenever $n(n-1)|V|(|V|-1)\le (|F|-1)h$ and $h\le n$. Each ordered collision forbids a unique dilation, so averaging controls the number of sites needed for a common locator. [Separation lemma](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/TranslatedSiteSeparation.lean)

Three companion results constrain other approaches to the full problem.

- A dyadic subgroup of order $1024$ in a 237-bit prime field has a nonprincipal additive period larger than $2.05\sqrt{1024\log_2(p/1024)}$. This excludes the stated constant within the sponsor's field-size window. The universal-constant question remains open. A separate norm inequality constrains the use of dyadic cyclotomic norms to construct an unbounded sequence of ratios. [Period certificate](dyadic-prime-norm-periods.md) · [Norm inequality](dyadic-principal-norm-gap.md)
- At the production rate-$1/8$ Johnson step, a list exceeding the two named budgets forces respectively four or six words whose pairwise differences split completely on the domain. Six words with fully split differences can coexist in a single received list on smooth domains of length $16d$. The six-word example concerns full agreement sets. A five-word example retains every pair intersection after cropping all supports to the target agreement size. [Counting bounds, constructions and hypotheses](saturated-list-pencils.md)
- For $n=128s$, $k=32s$, characteristic at least $148s$, and agreement threshold at least $74s$, every received pair has at most $6656s-54$ bad scalars on any injective domain. The proof charges components of a multiplicity interpolant against its total degree in the ordinate. [Uniform probability bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/JetSurfaceSafety.lean) · [Component count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FlatSurfacePopulation.lean)

## Beyond the half-distance plateau in fields of linear size

Let $m$ be the number of active heads, let $e$ be the integer error count, and put $t=n-e$. When $2\le k<t\le n$ and $m(t-k+1)\le n-k+2$, every prescribed domain over a field of order $q$ fails the MCA budget $1/Q$ if $Q>1$ and $(Q-1)m(e+1)\ge q$. This statement places no restriction on $q$ or its characteristic. Counting collisions over all projective directions gives at least $\lceil M(q+1)/(q+M)\rceil$ distinct projected labels from $M=m(e+1)$ keys. The stated integer inequality makes this count strictly larger than $\lfloor q/Q\rfloor$. [Kernel-verified radius bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldProjectionBudget.lean)

On smooth domains the secant construction retains **all** $m(e+1)$ labels when $m(E+E(E-1)n)<q$, with $E=\binom{m+1}{2}$. The additional head coefficient belongs to sites outside all assigned blocks. For prescribed prime-field sequences with $n\to\infty$, $k/n\to\rho>0$ and $p/n\to\lambda$, any fixed fraction $0\le\alpha<1-\rho$ satisfying
$$
m(1-\rho-\alpha)<1-\rho,\qquad m\alpha>\lambda/Q,\qquad \lambda>mE(E-1)
$$
eventually bounds the actual operational radius from above. This covers budgets beyond the proved plateau and supplies only the failure side there. [Exact finite count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothManyHeadMass.lean) · [Limiting ceiling](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothLinearFieldEnvelope.lean)

The dense spectral comparison now gives the sharp limsup $(d-1)/d$ in units of $\sqrt p$ for each fixed subgroup index $d>2$, by Mit'kin's theorem. These groups eventually exceed the sparse bound $|H|^2\le p$. [Derivation and paper](dense-prime-power-periods.md#sharp-limsup-at-every-fixed-index)

### Averaged projection and all fresh sites on an arbitrary finite-field domain

For $M>0$ distinct points in $\mathbb F_q^2$, one linear projection has at least
\[
 R(M,q)=\max\!\left(M-\left\lfloor\binom M2/(q+1)\right\rfloor,
                       \left\lceil M(q+1)/(q+M)\right\rceil\right)
\]
distinct image values. Each pair of unequal points collides in a single projective direction. The loss of distinct values is at most the number of colliding unordered pairs. Averaging over the $q+1$ directions gives the first term, and the finite fingerprint second moment gives the second. Taking a projection with the larger image attains both guarantees for one pair of received words. [Projection and literal MCA mass](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/AveragedPencilProjection.lean)

At agreement $k<t\le n$, for $k\ge2$ and $m\ge1$ satisfying $m(t-k+1)\le n-k+2$, every injective evaluation domain over every finite field has worst-case line-MCA numerator at least $R(m(n-t+1),q)$. Use a common locator $H$ of degree $k-2$ and $t-k+1$ private coordinates per head. At an evaluation point $x$ with owner coefficient $a$, a foreign head with coefficient $b$ has key $(-x,-(a+b))$ from the first-row pencil $Hax,Ha^2$ and second row $Ha$. The key recovers the site, its owner, and the foreign head in every characteristic. Give each unused site a coefficient distinct from all head coefficients, so that site supplies a key for each head. At tolerance $2^{-128}$ and $q\le2^{256}$, the layout certifies failure as soon as $m(n-t+1)>\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor$, even if $\binom{m(n-t+1)}2>q$. This gives a domain-uniform failure bound. Exact proximity radii still require agreement-side upper bounds on the bad count. [Finite-field construction and 128-bit failure test](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ManyHeadLocatorMass.lean)

### Exceptional-site construction verified over every finite field

For an arbitrary injective domain of size $n$ over $\mathbb F_q$, take $1\le m\le k<t\le n$ and $m(t-k)\le n-k$. At agreement $t$ one received pair has at least $R(m(n-t+1),q)$ bad scalars, with $R$ the projective projection bound above. Each degree-$k-1$ head omits a different site from one block of $k$ coordinates and owns $t-k$ private coordinates. Every other position, including that head’s exceptional site, gives a distinct two-component key. For an ordinary site $x$ with owner coefficient $c$, the key of head $a$ is $(-x,-(a+c)x+ac)$. Choosing a fixed private site as the coefficient for extra positions ensures $x\ne c$ even when the domain fills the entire field. The key thus recovers both site and head. At $q\le2^{256}$ and 128-bit tolerance, the single inequality $m(n-t+1)>\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor$ certifies actual MCA failure under the displayed layout conditions. [Arbitrary-field proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ExceptionalHeadLocatorMass.lean)

At the second designated prime and $n=2^{30}$, the kernel now verifies operational upper bounds of **429496730**, **603979776**, and **704643072** errors at rates $1/2,1/4,1/8$, respectively. Each improves the prior formal ceiling by one Hamming step and matches the existing written attack. The associated denominators are all $2^{30}$. The theorem certifies failure at these steps. Safety of their predecessors remains open. Both canonical focused builds passed; all six public axiom audits use only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. [Three numerical thresholds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SecondPrimeExceptionalHeadCeilings.lean)

### Actual dyadic prime-field period excludes the binary-log coefficient 2.1

At $p=4283494302159617$, the power-of-two subgroup generated by $g=3800919073425889$ has order 256. At $b=4275064871947385$ the real part of its nonprincipal period exceeds **223**, hence exceeds $(21/10)\sqrt{256\log_2(p/256)}$. This meets the sponsor's power-of-two domain definition and the sparse workbench condition $256^2\le p$. The kernel verifies primality, the subgroup, the nonzero frequency, the centered-residue sum, the trigonometric bound and the final strict logarithmic inequality. The field is 52 bits, and the result excludes this explicit constant in the general dyadic estimate. It does not settle the existence of a larger constant or the coding-theoretic threshold. [Kernel-checked example and reusable residue inequality](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PlasticDyadicPeriod.lean)

### Scalar list size at the known first-prime failures

At the first named prime's production length, the worst-center interleaved list has exactly **two** members at its known first MCA failure of one-half radius, at each of rates one eighth and one sixteenth. On every even-length finite-field domain with positive dimension and width, $3(k-1)<n/2$ suffices for this exact list size. Zero and one fit a ball with their values split evenly. Three words would require $3n/2\le n+3(k-1)$. At the two higher rates, integer coordinate multiplicities bound worst-center lists by **118** and **4**, respectively, at the known first-prime MCA attack counts 313,174,699 and 480,946,859. These last two counts are upper bounds on the first MCA failure. [All four list bounds and low-rate separation](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/HalfRadiusListMcaSeparation.lean)

## Many-head ceilings for smooth domains in fields of linear size

Write $n$ for block length, $k$ for dimension, $q$ for field size and $m=d+1$ for the number of heads. Choose $h,r$ with $h+mr\le n$ and $h+2\le k\le h+r$. Put $E=\binom m2$. If $d(E+E(E-1)n)<q$, every smooth domain has a received pair with at least $mdr$ distinct bad challenges at agreement $h+r+1$. For fixed $m$, this field-size condition grows linearly with $n$. At a prime $p$ and soundness target $1/Q$, take $r=\lfloor(n-k+2)/m\rfloor$. If $2\le k\le n$, $r\ge2$ and $mdr>\lfloor p/Q\rfloor$, the same field-size condition gives $\delta^*\le(n-k+1-r)/n$. Both bounds count the actual line-MCA bad event. [Mass and radius proofs](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothManyHeadMass.lean)

The site ratios of a multiplicative coset take only $n$ values. Choose distinct $b_j$ and set $a_j=t^j$. Each pair of heads has secant slope $(a_i-a_j)/(b_i-b_j)$. Distinct unordered pairs give polynomials in $t$ with distinct two-element supports. Root counting chooses one $t$ for which all edge slopes are nonzero and their multiples by domain points are distinct across all edges and sites. On the $h$ common sites the locator vanishes. On private sites the second received row identifies the owner, and agreement on a foreign head's core plus one such site certifies a distinct bad scalar. The construction provides the failure side. A matching uniform bound on bad scalars remains open beyond the half-distance plateau. [Slope separation](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CosetHeadSlopes.lean)

## The half-distance plateau for fields of linear size

For fixed security denominator \(Q\), let \(n\to\infty\), \(p/n\to\lambda\) and \(k/n\to\rho\in(0,1)\). For **every** choice of injective prime-field evaluation domains, including smooth domains whenever supplied, the operational scalar line-MCA radius has the limit
\[
 \min\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{Q},\frac{1-\rho}{2}\right)
 \qquad\text{if}\qquad \frac{\lambda}{Q}\le 1-\rho .
\]
Below half the limiting redundancy, the earlier exact finite formula applies eventually. From half through full redundancy the limit stays at \((1-\rho)/2\), including both endpoints and sequences crossing either endpoint at finite lengths. Beyond that interval this theorem leaves the limiting radius open. [Earlier strict regime](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldBudgetLimit.lean) and [plateau theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldHalfDistancePlateau.lean).

The lower bound uses the apolar predecessor at a fixed number of Hamming steps below \(\min(\lfloor p/Q\rfloor,\lfloor(n-k)/2\rfloor)\). For the upper bound, at any integer \(e\) with \(2(e+1)\le n\le k+2e\) and \(k+e+1\le n\), one received pair has at least \(2(e+1)\) distinct bad challenges over any finite field. Choose two disjoint private sets of \(e+1\) sites. The locator \(H\) of their common complement supplies the heads \((0,0)\) and \((XH,H)\). Each private site \(x\) gives the bad challenge \(-x\); the opposite core pins both codewords. Applying this at \(e=\max(\lceil(n-k)/2\rceil,\lfloor\lfloor p/Q\rfloor/2\rfloor)\) yields the upper limit. [Finite lower approximation](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CriticalLinearBudget.lean), [two-head witness](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/TwoHeadLinearBudget.lean) and [earlier two-head construction](three-rates-two-head-window.md).

## Exact line-MCA radius when the field size grows linearly

Let \(n\) be the block length, \(k\ge1\) the dimension, \(Q\ge1\) the security denominator, and \(B=\lfloor q/Q\rfloor\). For every finite field of size \(q\) and every injective evaluation domain, the operational line-MCA radius at error \(1/Q\) is exactly \(B/n\) if
\[
 B\ge1,\qquad k+2B\le n,\qquad n<B(n-k+3-2B).
\]
At the predecessor \(B-1\) the apolar count bounds every received pair by \(B\) bad scalars. At \(B\) errors a spike gives \(B+1\) distinct bad scalars. Thus the code fails at the supremum. These statements apply to each individual finite instance meeting the displayed inequalities. [Finite theorem and mass bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldBudgetRadius.lean).

In particular, take any prime fields and any prescribed domains along which \(n\to\infty\), \(p/n\to\lambda\), and \(k/n\to\rho\), with positive dimension eventually. For fixed \(Q\), if \(\rho+2\lambda/Q<1\), all sufficiently large members have first bad count exactly \(\lfloor p/Q\rfloor\), and their operational radii tend to \(\lambda/Q\). This includes \(Q=2^{128}\). It complements the earlier superpolynomial-field classification. The hypotheses classify supplied sequences and do not assert the existence of dyadic smooth domains at every prescribed \(\lambda\). [Eventually exact radii and their limit](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/LinearFieldBudgetLimit.lean).

## Low additive energy can coexist with the four known MCA failures

At \(p=65\cdot2^{239}+1\) and \(n=2^{30}\) there is a single evaluation domain \(G\) with order-four additive energy \(E_4(G)\le24n^4\) on which MCA at the four respective radii \(31/64,95/128,111/128,239/256\) has error above the allowed \(2^{-128}\). Here \(E_r\) counts ordered pairs of length-\(r\) tuples with equal sums, allowing repeated sites. The domain is a reciprocal image of a smooth domain. Scaling both received rows preserves the worst-case MCA error exactly. This exhibits a limitation of using bounded-order additive energy as a sufficient statistic on arbitrary evaluation domains. It leaves open what the multiplicative structure of a smooth domain adds. [Exact transport and simultaneous failure](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalEnergyFailure.lean).

For any prescribed \(n\)-site domain in a prime field, \(r<p\), a single reciprocal chart removes all non-permutation collisions of length \(r\) whenever \(p>n+2r n^{2r}\). A multiplicity difference appears as a nonzero simple-pole coefficient and its cleared numerator has at most \(2r\) roots. Consequently \(E_r\le r!n^r\) in this chart. At order two it gives the exact minimum \(E_2=2n^2-n\). [Tuples with repetitions](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalTupleEnergy.lean) and [energy theorems](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalChartMoments.lean).

Averaging over the \(p-n\) allowed poles also works when \(n<p\) and complete separation is too costly. For each \(r<p\), at least one chart satisfies
\[
 E_r\le r!n^r+\left\lfloor\frac{2r n^{2r}}{p-n}\right\rfloor.
\]
The same chart bounds the \(2r\)-th Fourier moment for every primitive additive character. At length \(2^{30}\), the bounds are \(E_5\le121n^5\) for \(p\ge2^{158}\) and \(E_8\le40321n^8\) for \(p\ge2^{245}\). The chart can depend on \(r\). These aggregate estimates do not supply the required worst-frequency estimate. The reciprocal-interval results of Bourgain and Garaev study a different, fixed-domain setting. [Lean averaging proof and finite bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalAverageEnergy.lean) and [Bourgain and Garaev](https://arxiv.org/abs/1211.4184).

## Reciprocal coordinates preserve the bad challenges

For every \(n\)-point domain in \(\mathbb F_q\), a single pole \(t\) outside the domain makes the sums of every two distinct \(r\)-element subsets different in the transformed coordinates \(y_i=(t-x_i)^{-1}\), provided \(q>n+2r\binom nr^2\). For distinct supports the difference of their logarithmic derivatives has a genuine simple pole, so its cleared numerator is nonzero and has at most \(2r\) roots. This proves simultaneous separation by counting the excluded poles. At \(n=2^{30}\) the condition holds for pairs when \(q\ge 2^{158}\) and for four-element subsets when \(q\ge2^{245}\). [Separation and field-size calculations](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalSubsetSeparation.lean).

At every degree cap \(D\) the same chart multiplies the received rows by \(y_i^D\) and preserves the exact MCA event for every challenge and radius. Homogeneous polynomial substitution transports codewords in both directions and preserves agreement on each support. This lets proofs use the separated coordinates on an arbitrary input domain. The separation theorem concerns distinct-site subsets. The transformed domain need not be a multiplicative subgroup, and a bound for all MCA witnesses is still needed. [Event equivalence and joint existence](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ReciprocalChartMCA.lean).

## Exact limiting radii and counts for families of decoding witnesses

At fixed 128-bit security and any interior limiting rate \(\rho\), every convergent subsequence of operational MCA radii over superpolynomial-size prime fields has limit in \(\{1-\rho,1\}\). At the critical exponent \(\log(p)/n\to H(\rho)\) both limits occur for any prescribed lengths and dimensions tending to that rate. There is also a single sequence of smooth dyadic domains at a rate \(\rho\in[1/512,1/16)\) on which changing the dimension by two produces both limits. The latter construction selects its limiting rate. [Classification](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldLimitSet.lean) and [same-domain construction](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothCriticalEntropy.lean).

For a finite set of primitive nonlinear directions with the same affine head, the bound on bad scalars from the preceding theorem uses \(\sum_j(s_j+1)\) in place of \(s+1\). The additive cost \(n-A+1\) occurs only once. At the four designated agreement counts and \(n=2^{30}\), the bound allows 9,493,333, 8,755,370, 4,845,568 and 2,182,656 quadratic directions, respectively, within a budget of \(n\) bad scalars. For a single primitive direction of challenge degree at most one, the proven upper counts are 658,505,729, 1,128,267,777, 1,518,338,047 and 1,971,322,879. Each is below \(2n\). An upper bound on every received pair still requires control of witnesses outside these families. [Finite unions](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProjectiveDirectionUnion.lean) and [affine directions](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/AffinePrimitiveDirection.lean).

For dyadic subgroup order \(n>1\) and odd \(r\), let \(R_p\) count the \((r-1)\)-tuples of \(n\)-th roots of unity in \(\mathbb F_p\) with sum \(-1\). Every finite set \(P\) of primes splitting those roots satisfies \((\prod_{p\in P}p^{R_p})^2\le r^{n^{r-1}}\). A modular determinant supplies the divisibility and Hadamard's inequality supplies the upper bound. It controls the aggregate over primes. A pointwise estimate at the designated prime still requires additional information. [Kernel proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/OddReturnDeterminant.lean) and [relation to odd moments](dense-prime-power-periods.md).

## Nonlinear projective directions, smooth capacity families and the critical exponent

For any field and injective evaluation domain of \(n\) sites, fix two codewords \(q_0,q_1\) of degree at most \(D\). Let \(U(X,Z)\) have site degree at most \(D\). As polynomials in the challenge \(Z\), its coefficients have gcd \(1\), have degrees at most \(s\), and include a coefficient of degree at least two. For every pair of received words and \(A>D\), the number of distinct nonjoint bad challenges admitting a witness of the form \(q_0+Zq_1+a_ZU(X,Z)\) on at least \(A\) sites is at most
\[
n-A+1+\left\lfloor\frac{n(n-1)(s+1)}{(A-D)^2}\right\rfloor.
\]
Indeed, proportional residuals on more than \(D\) sites force coefficient-wise divisibility by interpolation. Primitivity then forces all the challenge coefficients to be affine, contrary to the degree hypothesis. Double-counting the remaining ordered pairs gives the bound. This applies to every domain and received pair. To bound the whole MCA event by this method one still needs to cover all its witnesses. [Incidence count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProjectiveDirectionIncidence.lean) and [primitive-direction theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProjectiveDirectionRigidity.lean).

There exists a sequence of smooth dyadic domains of unbounded length in prime fields on which the line-MCA radius tends to \(1-\rho\) simultaneously for the rates \(1/2,1/4,1/8,1/16\) at fixed 128-bit security. The construction uses prime divisors of Fermat numbers and gives the required two-adic divisibility. [Smooth domain construction and four limits](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothFermatCapacity.lean).

At the critical exponent \(\log(p)/n\to H(\rho)\), for each interior limiting rate, there are prime sequences with limiting operational radii \(1-\rho\) and \(1\), respectively. This holds for every embedding into the respective fields. The construction chooses Bertrand primes at two constant multiples of the same binomial coefficient. It does not prescribe roots-of-unity domains in those fields. [Finite transition and critical limits](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldCriticalEntropy.lean).

Finite signed moment identities express Fermat point-count discrepancies in terms of the actual coset periods. Fixed-index prime examples from a horizontal equidistribution theorem invalidate a version of the hoped-for estimate that omits the sparsity condition. The workbench retains \(n^2\le p\), where these examples leave the question open. The note separates the Lean identities, the external equidistribution theorem, and the sparse average over primes. [Lean identities](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FermatPowerMoment.lean) and [derivation and primary sources](dense-prime-power-periods.md).

## Capacity and field growth at fixed 128-bit security

Let the block length tend to infinity, let the prime field orders grow faster than every fixed power of the length, and take arbitrary evaluation domains in those fields. If eventually $p\le 2^{\min(k+1,n-k-1)}$, the difference between $1-k/n$ and the operational line-MCA supremum tends to zero. The result holds when the interleaving width is any positive integer at each length. The stronger finite upper test allows $p\le(2^{128}-1)\binom{n}{k+1}$. [Finite sandwich and limits](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldCapacityWindow.lean)

For $k/n\to\rho\in(0,1)$ and $\log(p)/n\to c>0$, write $H(\rho)=-\rho\log\rho-(1-\rho)\log(1-\rho)$. If $c<H(\rho)$, the operational radius tends to $1-\rho$. If $c>H(\rho)$, the operational radius equals one for all sufficiently large lengths. These conclusions concern sequences of actual codes on every prescribed domain. The critical exponent and the exact finite thresholds between the known bounds remain open. [Entropy phase theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldEntropyPhase.lean)

A direct count controls labels whose rich polynomial witness satisfies $(X+z)P'=DP$. Over a field of characteristic zero or greater than the degree cap, a nonzero solution is a scalar multiple of $(X+z)^D$. Two agreement coordinates eliminate the multiplier and produce a nonzero scalar polynomial of degree at most $D+1$. This gives conditional thin-field bounds on actual bad challenges belonging to that differential equation. [Polynomial solution and MCA count](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ShiftedPowerCurveCount.lean)

For the translated polynomial powers in the companion theorem, the four respective label counts are at most 882,394,513, 956,021,025, 1,033,073,255 and 1,048,369,882 at the four previously certified radii. Each lies below the thinner designated budget of $2^{30}$. The target remains to control witnesses outside these families. [Translated polynomial pencils](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PowerPencilCurveCount.lean)

## Prime-field radii, interpolation charges and four simultaneous witnesses

At a positive multiple $n=128b$, every injection into a prime field of order $p\ge 2^{165}n^2$ meets the 128-bit affine-line MCA bound at the radii in the table. Each lies strictly beyond the finite Johnson radius. At the same radii, every prime field that admits the domain has the listed bound on the worst-case list size for every finite interleaving width. Packing the column into a rational function extends the scalar interpolation bound to all widths. [Four-rate bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ScaledFirstOrderSecurity.lean) · [Dao, Kominers and Thaler](https://eprint.iacr.org/2026/2056)

| Rate | Proven good line radius | Interleaved list-size bound | Proven failing line radius at $p=65\cdot 2^{239}+1$, $n=2^{30}$ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| $1/2$ | $39/128$ | $173373n$ | $31/64$ |
| $1/4$ | $67/128$ | $113975n$ | $95/128$ |
| $1/8$ | $86/128$ | $58872n$ | $111/128$ |
| $1/16$ | $99/128$ | $52349n$ | $239/256$ |

The line endpoints in each row apply to the same smooth code at the displayed prime and length, for every positive interleaving width. All four failure witnesses arise by pulling received rows back through power maps. The prime admits such a domain. The same tests hold on all smooth domains throughout the field-budget interval in the theorem. The operational supremum lies between the endpoints. The table leaves its value inside that interval open. [Simultaneous bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProductionMcaIntervals.lean) · [Power-map witnesses and domain existence](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourRateSmoothMcaLift.lean)

For every length and nontrivial dimension $1\le k<n$, put $N=\binom n{k+1}$. In *every* finite field and every prescribed domain, if $\lfloor N/(k+1)\rfloor\le\lfloor q/D\rfloor$ and $q\le(D-1)N$, the first failing Hamming count at tolerance $1/D$ is exactly $n-k-1$. The threshold for real radii is $1-(k+1)/n$. The upper bound on bad scalars uses private interpolation circuits. The lower bound uses the second moment of collisions among the $N$ circuits. This field-size interval has no fixed bit cap. [Exact radius predicate and supremum](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CircuitBudgetRadius.lean)

The shifted first-order slot test with zero derivative cap passes for some multiplicity, degree and height exactly when $A^2>nD$, where $A$ is the agreement count and $D$ is the maximum message degree. With positive cap and total degree one, the exact line boundary is $3A\ge2n+D$. At the half-rate radius $39/128$, every passing choice of slots at length $2^{30}$ incurs a hybrid exceptional-count charge greater than $2^{58}$. The alternative optimized squarefree bound also exceeds $2n$ at the four displayed radii, even with Taylor exponent zero and the profile hypotheses stated in the theorem. The two designated thin-budget fields allow $n$ and $2n$ bad challenges. These estimates bound the certificate charges. The actual exceptional counts at these radii remain open. [Zero-cap and unrestricted-charge theorems](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FirstOrderZeroCapJohnson.lean) · [Degree-one boundary](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FirstOrderDegreeOneBoundary.lean) · [Squarefree bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SquarefreeRetentionFloor.lean) · [Taylor pole example](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/RegularTaylorPole.lean)

At dimension $2h$, let four disjoint $h$-point blocks be fibers of the same monic degree-$h$ polynomial. Choose a subset $T_i$ of each block on which all four mixed words must agree. With the four fiber values as scalar labels, the prescribed MCA witnesses exist exactly when $\sum_i |T_i|<h$. This covers four interlaced cosets in a cyclic length-$4h$ domain. The general error supports in the prize can have other shapes. [Construction and necessity](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourFiberThreshold.lean) · [Cross-ratio and spectral restrictions](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourSupportSpectrum.lean) · [Polynomial restrictions on consecutive supports](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SyndromeMotion.lean)

For a dyadic subgroup $H$ of size $n$, the fourth-energy equality $E_2(H)=3n(n-1)$ implies, in characteristic greater than three, that $H$ contains no zero-sum triple. The deduction from the equality and the resulting cubic Fourier moment are formal. The fourth-energy checks for the two named primes use the separate arithmetic certificates. A related incidence bound controls affine images of $H$ across disjoint shift cosets. On a projection choosing one site in each of the order-16 blocks, the two named fields force at least 3,132,099 disjoint signed relations of weight at most 14. The uniform upper bound on individual nontrivial periods remains open. [Zero triples](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/DyadicSidonTriple.lean) · [Affine intersections](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/AffineMultiplierOverlap.lean) · [Projected short relations](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProjectedShortRelations.lean)

The prime-field comparison of lists and proximity uses different finite inequalities from the asymptotic capacity result. Reference copies for this work were downloaded to the shared research cache. [Brakensiek, Chen, Putterman, Zhang and Zheng](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08005) · [Montgomery, Vaughan and Wooley on power Gauss sums](https://www.math.purdue.edu/~twooley/publ/1995%20GSUM.pdf) · [Cochrane, Hart, Pinner and Spencer on Waring's number](https://www.math.ksu.edu/~cvs/cochrane_hart_pinner_spencer-waring_subgroup.pdf)

## 2026-11-21 · From the coding prizes to classical Paley discrepancy

Two signed interpolation determinants give the discrepancy at each prescribed pair of sets. If $W_A(b)=\sum_{u\in A}\chi(b+u)$, their orders at $b$ are $\lfloor (W_A(b)+1)^2/4\rfloor$ and $\lfloor (W_A(b)-1)^2/4\rfloor$. Their difference is $W_A(b)$. Summing those differences over $B$ recovers the signed discrepancy without changing coordinates. Lean also proves the equivalence between a power saving on arbitrary rectangles and a uniform bound on the largest parity-corrected root weights of the central determinant. The uniform root-weight estimate remains open. [Signed formula and proof](paley-shift-multiplicities.md#two-determinants-recover-the-sign) · [Lean rectangle identity](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleySignedFactorization.lean) · [Lean root-weight criterion](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyAbsoluteSlice.lean)

For a multiplicative subgroup $H$ and prescribed $A\subseteq\mathbb F_q^\times$ with at most $K$ points in each $H$-coset, Lean proves a power saving below square-root size. If $|A|,|H|\ge q^{1/4+\epsilon}$ and $q^\epsilon\ge4C(q)K$, the relative quadratic discrepancy of $A\times H$ is at most $q^{-\epsilon/4}$. Here $C(q)=11$ for odd $q\ge3251$ and $17$ for the remaining odd field orders. In particular one may prescribe the representative in each occupied coset of a partial transversal. At square-root size a written extension covers each fixed character order $k\ge2$ on arbitrary rectangles, giving $\sqrt{2/3}+o(1)$ at order three. [Subgroup theorem and conditions](paley-subgroup-transversals.md) · [Fixed-order bounds](paley-fixed-order-rectangles.md)


For arbitrary prescribed $A,B\subseteq\mathbb F_p$ with $|A|,|B|=(1+o(1))\sqrt p$, the quadratic discrepancy is at most $(\sqrt3/2+o(1))|A||B|$. The proof counts shifts with a given number of opposite signs using the determinant of normalized derivatives of a Reed–Solomon interpolation polynomial. For $p=65537$ and two 256-element sets, the finite bound is 56,719 out of 65,536, including the number of zero entries on the left. The argument gives a constant saving at this scale. A power saving for every prescribed pair of polynomial-size sets remains open. [Finite inequality and proof](paley-defective-rectangle.md) · [Lean bound for every prescribed rectangle](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyPrescribedRectangle.lean)

For a single supplied set $A$, a second interpolation determinant has root multiplicity exactly $\lfloor (\sum_{u\in A}\chi(u+b))^2/4\rfloor$ at every shift $b$. A Vandermonde rank gives the minority-sign count at the same shift. Lean verifies the full factorization and the exact order at each root by using a second moment to account for the whole degree. A direct field calculation checked 1,765 full factorizations and 21,889 ranks. A uniform tail bound on the largest root multiplicities would imply the classical quadratic Paley conjecture. [Proof and equivalent tail estimate](paley-shift-multiplicities.md) · [End-to-end Lean factorization](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyShiftFactorization.lean) · [Verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_paley_shift_multiplicities.py)

Lean also proves a reduction to one set of one fixed size. For $2\le m\le p/4$, put $D_m=\max_{|T|=m}|\sum_{x,y\in T}\chi(x+y)|$. The relative discrepancy of any prescribed rectangle with both sizes at least $m$ is at most $6D_m/m^2+8/m$. The converse follows by taking $A=T$ and $B=-T$. [Written derivation](paley-single-size-test.md) · [Lean graph bound and converse](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleySingleSizeQuadratic.lean)


The prize rank formulas give identical data on each of three indexed order-16 cosets, yet one pair has every classical Paley edge and another has exactly half the edges. Their common coding thresholds are explicit at all four rates. At logarithmic subgroup orders a written application of Weil’s bound realizes every possible signed coset discrepancy. Inversion gives examples preserving support ranks even when both sets are marked inside one domain. In the positive direction, a written second-moment estimate gives the conjectured power saving for almost every simultaneous inverse chart on any two supplied polynomial-size sets, for every nontrivial multiplicative character. Lean also proves the exact translation mean square for every nontrivial character and fixed dilation, in terms of additive energy. The bound at an arbitrary prescribed placement remains open. [Proofs and finite certificates](paley-support-ranks.md) · [Lean translation theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyTranslationEnergy.lean)

For every finite field of odd order \(q\ge3251\) and nonempty \(A,B\), Lean bounds the fraction of common inverse charts of relative quadratic discrepancy at least \(\eta>0\) by \(\eta^{-2}(2/(|A||B|)+8/\sqrt q)\), puncturing at the pole. On the full smooth domain at \(p=65537\), an exact \(18\times18\) example has signed discrepancy 324 before inversion and zero afterward. [Kernel proof of the chart bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyProjectiveMoment.lean) · [Exact rectangle and derivation](paley-support-ranks.md)

The joint coding theorem covers the order-128 subgroup of the prime field of order
$p=65\cdot2^{239}+1$. At rates $1/2,1/4,1/8,1/16$ its first bad list error counts are
$63,97,113,121$. For MCA the half-rate crossing is $62$ errors and the other three rates
stay within budget through radius one. The proof counts agreement subsets, constructs
prescribed-product witnesses, and transfers scalar lists to MCA under explicit field-size
conditions. The length-$2^{30}$ four-rate MCA result has a separate field-budget window.
[Joint theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourRateSmoothMca128.lean) ·
[List proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourRateList128.lean) ·
[Production-length window](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SharpApolarMCA.lean)

**An exact coding reduction.** For an odd prime, write $S(A,B)=\sum_{b\in B,a\in A}\chi(a+b)$
for the quadratic character. On coordinates $a\in A$, use the affine Reed–Solomon word
$a\mapsto a+b$. For $s=\pm1$ let $h_s(b)$ count coordinates where the symbol has character
$s$. The signed excess is exactly $2h_s(b)-|A|+\mathbf1_{-b\in A}$. If for each sign at
most $L$ shifts have excess greater than a nonnegative integer $t$, then
$|S(A,B)|\le t|B|+L|A|$ for every $B$. Conversely, if $|S(A,B)|\le t|B|$ for every
$|B|\ge m>0$, each exceptional list has size below $m$. This identifies the missing estimate
in coding language. Every coordinate accepts the same half-alphabet of squares, or of
nonsquares. A single-symbol center has just $|A|$ agreements across *all* shifts.
The affine pair $X,1$ has zero MCA-bad scalars in every dimension at least two. Direct
encoding of the Legendre values also meets a degree obstruction. On at most $(p-1)/2$
coordinates their off-domain translates have full affine hull over the prime field.
[Shift-list equivalences and MCA proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyShiftListRecovery.lean) ·
[Affine-hull theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyTranslateInterpolation.lean)

In particular the classical quadratic-character conjecture is equivalent to the
following list bound. For every $0<\epsilon<1$ there is $\gamma>0$ such that,
for all sufficiently large primes and every $|A|>p^\epsilon$, each sign has
at most $p^{\epsilon/2}$ shifts with signed excess greater than
$|A|p^{-\gamma}$. For the forward direction apply the conjecture at exponent
$\epsilon/2$, taking $B$ to be the entire list of shifts of one sign if it
exceeds that size. In the reverse direction, for every $|B|>p^\epsilon$
the relative discrepancy is bounded by $p^{-\gamma}+p^{-\epsilon/2}$.
For sufficiently large $p$ this gives the conjectured power saving with
exponent $\tfrac12\min(\gamma,\epsilon/2)$. Rounding the excess threshold
down to an integer preserves the two lists.

**Why the usual list-decoding interpolant stops at this grid.** Write $m=|A|$,
$d=(p-1)/2$ and $L_A(X)=\prod_{a\in A}(X-a)$. Assume $1\le m\le d$ and
let $Q$ be the nonzero squares. In coordinates $(X,T)=(X,Y-X)$ the allowed
points above $a$ are $(a,t)$ with $t\in Q-a$. If $\deg_T H<d$, grid
vanishing is equivalent to $L_A\mid H$. Indeed, each specialization $H(a,T)$
has $d$ distinct roots, so every coefficient vanishes on $A$. Lean proves
this equivalence for any field and any common list. At any order $s$, Lean
also proves that $(X-a)^s\mid H(X,t)$ at every allowed $(a,t)$ if and only
if $L_A^s\mid H$, under the same parameter-degree condition. This follows by
applying the Vandermonde argument to the first $s$ Hasse derivative evaluations.
[Sheared interpolation theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyListInterpolation.lean)

For larger degrees the following written argument treats multiplicity $s\ge1$,
where multiplicity means
membership in the $s$th power of the ideal of the point. For a nonzero
interpolant $H$, extract the largest common power $L_A^e$. If $e<s$, the
quotient has a nonzero slice at some $a\in A$. At each of the $d$ allowed
points of that slice its vanishing order is at least $s-e$, since locally
$L_A$ is $X-a$ times a unit. Thus $\deg_T H\ge(s-e)d$ and $\deg_X H\ge em$.
In the original coordinates the same argument gives total degree at least
$em+(s-e)d\ge sm$. When $e\ge s$, the factor $L_A^s$ already has degree $sm$.
The strict degree test for a word with at most $m$ agreements cannot force
its polynomial identity by this total-degree argument.

Even allowing $\deg_T H=d$ gives $\deg_X H\ge sm-1$ in sheared coordinates.
The only case left to check is $e=s-1$. The quotient on each nonzero row
is then a scalar multiple of $(T+a)^d-1$. If there are $z$ nonzero rows,
factor out the $m-z$ remaining row factors $X-a$. The surviving $z$ row
polynomials are linearly independent. Their coefficients of powers $T^d$
through $T^{d-z+1}$ form a Vandermonde matrix up to nonzero row and column
scalars, since $z\le d<p$. A polynomial of $X$-degree $k$ has row span of
dimension at most $k+1$. Restoring the extracted factors gives
$(s-1)m+(m-z)+(z-1)=sm-1$. Equality is attained by interpolating the
polynomials $(T+a)^d-1$ in $a$ and multiplying by $L_A^{s-1}$.
With that degree the strict root count guarantees an identity at perfect
agreement. Paley discrepancy requires control of agreement just above half.

**Classical Paley results.** At the second designated production prime the order-$2^{30}$
generator $g$ is a nonsquare. Simultaneous scaling by $g$ makes $S(A,B)=0$ for every pair
of unions of its cosets. For two geometric intervals in arbitrary such cosets,
$|S(A,B)|\le |A|+|B|$. Negating $B$ converts the sum convention above to graph differences.
At the first prime, the complete subgroup has quadratic row sum $-30929$ at one, and
all its elements are squares. Thus its ordered difference sum is $-33209760874496$.
The second-prime zero and interval bound are Lean theorems. The first-prime value is
an exact GMP evaluation. The scan pairs $g^i$ with $g^{-i}$, treats the two self-inverse
elements separately, and checks each shard endpoint by modular powering. Direct evaluation
of every ordered pair at the two small controls reproduced every dyadic subtotal.
[Cosets and intervals](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyCosetCancellation.lean) ·
[Production character scan](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_paley_row.cpp)

For arbitrary sets in a finite field of order $q$, put $a=|A|$ and $b=|B|$.
The kernel-checked second moment yields $q|S|^2\le ab(q-a)(q-b)$ for every nontrivial
multiplicative character. For the quadratic character, the repository's unconditional Stepanov
fourth moment gives $r^2|S|^4\le17qa^3b^4$ whenever $1\le r\le b$ and $r^4\le q$.
Averaging over the $r$-subsets of $B$ yields this bound with no upper limit on $b$.
Thus $17q^{1+4\delta}\le ar^2$ suffices for a relative saving $q^{-\delta}$.
For arbitrary balanced sets of size $p^\epsilon$ below the square-root threshold, the
uniform saving remains open. [Lean estimates](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyBilinearDiscrepancy.lean) ·
[Kim–Yip–Yoo, Conjecture 2.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2309.09124v3#S2.SS4)

**Every pair of sets under almost every relative affine placement.** Fix arbitrary
$A,B$ in a finite field of size $q$ and a nontrivial multiplicative character
$\chi$. Put $a=|A|$, $b=|B|$, and $S(A,B)=\sum_{x\in A,y\in B}\chi(x+y)$.
There is an exact identity
$$
\sum_{u\ne0,\,v}|S(A,uB+v)|^2=ab(q-a)(q-b).
$$
To see it, $W(y)=\sum_{x\in A}\chi(x+y)$ has mean zero and
$\sum_y|W(y)|^2=a(q-a)$ by character orthogonality. Under a uniformly
random invertible affine map, each point of $B$ has a uniform image and
each pair of distinct points has a uniform ordered pair of distinct images.
Expansion of the square gives the identity, with all $q(q-1)$ maps counted.
For $a,b>q^\epsilon$ and $0<\delta<\epsilon$, the fraction of placements
with $|S(A,uB+v)|\ge q^{-\delta}ab$ is below $q^{-2(\epsilon-\delta)}$.
This includes arbitrary sparse shapes. The prescribed map $u=1,v=0$ can
belong to the exceptional set. Lean checked the identity and the exceptional
count for all nonnegative thresholds. Independent kernel replay with
`con-leche --verified` accepted the exports of both affine theorems (14,466
declarations) and both common-list interpolation theorems (13,638 declarations).
[Affine second moment](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyAffineSecondMoment.lean)

**Two graphs on the joint prize instance.** In the classical Paley graph, the subgraph
induced on the 128 code coordinates is 67-regular and its ordered signed sum is 896.
Lean checked Euler's criterion at every power of the domain generator. A separate
integer computation gives clique number 10, independence number 8 and signed matrix
norm below 30 on these coordinates. The signed matrix $K$ is circulant and symmetric.
Nine cyclic convolution squarings give $\operatorname{tr}(K^{512})<30^{512}$.
Every eigenvalue therefore has absolute value below 30. In particular all subsets
$U,V$ of the domain satisfy $|S(U,-V)|\le30\sqrt{|U||V|}$. Clique enumeration fixes
one vertex by transitivity, then recursively intersects ordered candidate sets with the
neighborhood of each chosen vertex. For the generalized graph with connection set
the same subgroup, Lean instead finds a nonprincipal additive period of magnitude
greater than $64$. The base-two logarithmic upper bound with constant one holds here
because $\log_2(p/128)>128$. The uniform prime-field logarithmic estimate remains open.
[Kernel-checked results](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrizeInstancePaleySpectrum.lean) ·
[Exact induced-graph calculation](../../../scripts/probes/probe_paley_joint_prize_domain.py)

**Arbitrary subsets of one production coset.** At either named prime let $H$ have order
$n=2^{30}$. For all $A,B$ in a common affine copy $t+cH$, $c\ne0$, the exact-arithmetic
certificate gives
$$
\left|\sum_{a\in A,b\in B}\chi(a-b)\right|\le K\sqrt{|A||B|},\qquad K_1=449676,\quad K_2=448831.
$$
In particular, for sets of size at least $2^{20}$ the relative discrepancy is below
$0.429$. This covers arbitrary choices of the two subsets *within* the specified copy.

To reproduce the certificate, compile the spectrum probe with C++17 and GMP, then
run it with arguments `first 128 4` and `second 128 4`. For a generator $g$, the signed matrix on $H$
has entries $\chi(g)^i v_{j-i}$ with $v_l=\chi(1-g^l)$. Multiplying the rows by signs
preserves singular values, giving the magnitudes of the discrete Fourier transform of
$v$. Split the exponents into 128 residue classes. At each frequency modulo $2^{23}$
sum the certified magnitudes across the classes before maximizing. The row phases in
the full transform have modulus one. Each radix-two transform uses fixed-point integer
arithmetic, with GMP square-root enclosures for its roots of unity and error below one
per row. The largest upper bounds occur at residues 66,773 and 3,625,762, respectively. The row sums
$-30929$ and $-1441$ at one agree with the independent character scans. Both certified
operator norms are below $14\sqrt n$. Applying the matrix bound to
indicator vectors yields the displayed inequality. Translation cancels in $a-b$,
and scaling contributes the common sign $\chi(c)$. These finite numerical inequalities
were computed outside Lean. [Certified transform](../../../scripts/probes/probe_sponsor_paley_spectrum.cpp)

**Fourier energy of a weighted code domain.** Let $D=t+cH$, write
$S_{xy}=\chi(x-y)$ on $D$, and for complex weights $z$ put
$\widehat z(r)=\sum_{x\in D}z_x e^{2\pi irx/p}$. For nonzero squares $R$ and
nonsquares $N$, let $E_U=\sum_{r\in U}|\widehat z(r)|^2$. Parseval gives
$E_R+E_N=p\|z\|_2^2-|\sum z_x|^2$. The quadratic Gauss sum at either prime gives
$E_R-E_N=\sqrt p\,z^*Sz$. Thus the Fourier matrix with rows from $R$ scaled by
$\sqrt{2/p}$ and the zero row scaled by $1/\sqrt p$ has Gram matrix $I+S/\sqrt p$
on $D$. The two certificates give distortion below $2^{-60}$ on every such support.
For $z_x=e^{-2\pi it'x/p}$ on $H$, this bounds the signed quadratic partition of
the energies of *all* translated additive periods. Bounding the largest individual
period remains open. This deduction uses the classical quadratic Gauss sum evaluation
and the computed operator norm. [Paley Fourier matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.02907)

A fractional linear map sends the signed matrix on its non-poles to the original
matrix up to a common sign and row and column signs. The same two operator bounds
therefore hold on subsets of a common projective image of $H$ in the finite field.
[Character identity](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PaleyCosetCancellation.lean)

There is a limit to covering arbitrary sparse pairs by these images. The ordered
cross ratios of four distinct elements of $H$ occupy fewer than $n^3$ values,
since multiplying through by the inverse of the first entry fixes it to one.
Given $t$ selected finite points whose quadruples avoid these values, at most
$t+t(t-1)(t-2)n^3$ next points are forbidden. For each ordered triple and
cross ratio the fourth point is unique on the projective line. The set of
forbidden cross ratios already includes every ordering of an $H$ quadruple.
At either prime this bound is below $p$ for $t<2^{22}$. Consequently there
exists a set of $2^{22}$ points meeting every projective image in at most three.
Split it into two equal parts to obtain a sparse pair on which these local matrix
bounds alone give no cancellation. This counting argument concerns the coverage
of the proof method and makes no claim about the sign of that pair's discrepancy.

## Exact four-rate thresholds at budget two and length sixteen

Let $D$ be any smooth domain of length 16 over a finite field of order $q$
with $\lfloor q/2^{128}\rfloor=2$. The following are the first failing closed-ball
error counts. At every positive interleaving width, the security predicate holds
exactly at real radii strictly below the indicated count divided by 16.

| Rate | First MCA failure | First list failure |
|---|---:|---:|
| $1/2$ | 2 | 5 |
| $1/4$ | 2 | 8 |
| $1/8$ | 2 | 10 |
| $1/16$ | 2 | 11 |

For MCA the same first failure holds for every injective length-$n$ domain
and dimension $k$ with $k+3\le n$ at budget two. For lists, take three distinct words
at agreement $t$. Two words contribute at most $n+k-1$ agreements with the
center in total. Three contribute at most $n+3(k-1)$, since every agreement at
a site after the first uses an agreeing pair. At the first three rates, three
explicit polynomials attain the next grid point. Their pairwise agreements occur
on three fibers of the power map of degree $4,2,1$, respectively, together with
common locator roots. Three constants suffice at the last rate. Lean verifies the
two answer functions against the full operational review contract for every
such input field, ordering of the domain, eligible dimension and positive width.
[Joint theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/BudgetTwoSixteenReview.lean) ·
[Uniform witnesses](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SixteenCosetListLower.lean) ·
[MCA theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/BudgetTwoMcaBoundary.lean)

On the order-16 subgroup of the prime field
$p=680564733906249808969588989158290882561$, the largest absolute nonprincipal
additive period belongs to exactly one multiplicative coset. Its least positive
frequency is $340274770462278292654815367542743271468$, its sign is negative,
and its magnitude lies in
$[15.999999991006943333,\;15.999999991006943336]$.
The degree is 16. On the same code Lean proves a positive period above $16-10^{-8}$
at a different frequency, together with both coding thresholds.
[Joint formal statement](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/BudgetTwoSixteenReview.lean)

The global maximum has an exact-arithmetic certificate outside Lean.
With $g$ of order 16, eight antipodal pairs determine each period. Signed
residues for a positive candidate, and the odd residues $2(bg^i\bmod p)-p$
for a negative candidate, belong to an integer lattice of index $p^7$.
Eight signed rotations of a short vector give a basis with determinant
of absolute value $p^7$. The cosine chord bound limits the squared norm
of any lift capable of winning. Exact inverse-basis bounds and parity leave
560 positive and 416 negative candidate frequencies after the norm test.
Rational Taylor bounds separate the two winning cosets from all competitors.
Run the standard-library Python probe with optimization enabled to reproduce
the full enumeration and the interval.
[Extremal-period certificate](../../../scripts/probes/probe_budget_two_sixteen_period.py)

There is also a uniform statement as the prime varies. If a nonempty domain
$D\subseteq\mathbb F_p$ is covered by $d$ signed representatives, Lean produces
a nonzero frequency with $|D|-\operatorname{Re}\eta_b<8\pi^2|D|p^{-2/d}$.
Partition the phases of the representatives into $K$ equal intervals each.
For $K^d<p$, two frequencies have the same interval in every coordinate.
At their difference, $1-\cos\theta\le\theta^2/2$ gives a deficit strictly
below $2\pi^2|D|/K^2$. For a subgroup $H$ of fixed dyadic order $n=2d$,
one representative of each antipodal pair suffices. Together with the
dyadic determinant bound earlier in this ledger, this gives
$$
\frac{8n}{d}(2p)^{-2/d}\ \le\
n-\max_{b\ne0}|\eta_b(H)|\ <\ 8\pi^2 n\,p^{-2/d}.
$$
In particular the deficit has order $p^{-1/4}$ for order 16 as $p$ ranges
over primes congruent to one modulo 16. The proof of the right inequality
is kernel-checked. The left uses the written cyclotomic determinant argument.
The sparse prime-field upper bound with growing subgroup order remains open.
[Signed-cover bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldPeriodAlignment.lean) ·
[Dyadic determinant check](../../../scripts/probes/probe_paley_dyadic_period_ceiling.py)

For fixed dyadic $n=2d$, this also bounds the number of candidate vectors in
the exact search independently of $p$. In the phase lattice of the first $d$
powers of a generator, every nonzero vector $v$ and its signed rotations form
a matrix of nonzero determinant divisible by $p^{d-1}$. Nonvanishing follows
from irreducibility of $X^d+1$ over $\mathbb Q$, and divisibility follows
because the columns span at most one dimension modulo $p$. Hadamard's
inequality gives $\|v\|_2\ge p^{1-1/d}$. For a maximizing frequency take
centered lifts with denominator $m=p$ for its positive sign or odd lifts
with $m=2p$ for the negative sign. The chord bound gives
$16\|v\|_2^2/m^2\le n-|\eta_b|$, so $\|v\|_2<2\pi\sqrt d\,p^{1-1/d}$.
Disjoint balls of radius $\tfrac12 p^{1-1/d}$ around these lattice points
fit in a ball with that radius added to the search radius. Their volume ratio
bounds the count by $(1+4\pi\sqrt d)^d$. The order-16 certificate above
computes a much smaller search region from its explicit basis.

## Production list bounds at the Johnson threshold and one step beyond

For $n=2^{30}$ the following closed balls have list size at most $n$ on
**every** injective evaluation domain, for every finite interleaving width.
At rates $1/2,1/4,1/8,1/16$ the certified error counts are respectively
$314491699,536870913,694116762,805306370$. These are safe at both named
prime fields' 128-bit budgets. Choose exactly $t=n-e$ agreement sites for
each word. Distinct resulting sets meet in at most $k-1$ sites. When
$t^2>n(k-1)$ their centered indicator vectors have strictly negative pairwise
inner products, giving at most $n$ vectors in their $(n-1)$-dimensional
span. [Four-rate Lean theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/InterleavedListJohnsonRank.lean)

At rate $1/8$ the **second** named prime is safe one more step, through
$694116763$ errors. Here $t=379625061$ and $t^2<n(k-1)$, while
$t^2>(n-1)(k-1)$. For each coordinate, the agreement sets omitting it have
at most $n-1$ members by the previous argument on the other coordinates.
Each set is counted $n-t$ times. Thus the list has at most
$\lfloor n(n-1)/(n-t)\rfloor=1660990722$ members, below the exact
budget $2147483648$. This argument bounds every center at every width.
An over-budget center at the subsequent error count is still unknown.
[Puncturing proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/InterleavedListJohnsonPuncture.lean)

An independent quadratic estimate at this same grid point gives $1931589824$.
Write $V=t(n-t)$ and $U=n(k-1)-t^2$. In the centered Gram matrix
$G_{ij}=n|S_i\cap S_j|-t^2$, the diagonal is $V$ and every off-diagonal
entry is in $[-t^2,U]$. Summing $(G_{ij}-U)(G_{ij}+t^2)$ over all entries
and using $\sum G_{ij}\ge0$ and $n\sum G_{ij}^2\ge L^2V^2$ gives
$L(V^2-nUt^2)\le n(V-U)(V+t^2)$ when $U\le t^2$. Lean checks the
inequality over arbitrary finite set families and its specialization to the
canonical list size at the second prime.
[Quadratic inequality](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/AgreementQuadratic.lean) ·
[Production specialization](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/InterleavedListJohnsonQuadratic.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · The optimized-power apolar bound and the exact lower-rate error curves

The reciprocal interpolation proof now applies to every positive integral power $m$. If $d=n-k-1$, $2e\le n-k$ and $n<m(d+2-e)+e$, then every genuine Reed–Solomon bad-scalar family on every injective length-$n$ evaluation domain satisfies
$$
M_e\le\max\left(e+1,\ m\left(1+\left\lfloor\frac{e}{d+2-2e}\right\rfloor\right)\right).
$$
The optimized power is $1+\lfloor (n-e)/(d+2-e)\rfloor$. At the unique-decoding endpoint $n=k+2e$, $e>0$, this gives $M_e\le(e+1)\lfloor(n+1)/(e+1)\rfloor$. The proof uses the actual received words. It treats odd and even powers separately in the reciprocal-coordinate interpolation, then descends in the parameter chart. The uniform cubic predecessor result has separately passed independent replay over 39,394 declarations. [Optimized power and endpoint](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarPowerBound.lean) · [Arbitrary-power bad-event theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SharpApolarMCA.lean) · [Reciprocal-chart argument](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarCubicObstruction.lean)

For every prime-field multiplicative subgroup of length $n=k+2a$ with $k\ge2$ and $n\le3a+1$, the scalar MCA numerator equals **$e+1$ at every $0\le e<a$** and **$2a+2$ at $e=a$**. All the upper bounds also hold for arbitrary injective evaluation domains over finite fields. At length $2^{30}$ the exact endpoint numerators are **805306370, 939524098, and 1006632962** at rates $1/4$, $1/8$, and $1/16$, respectively. Thus the full scalar error curves up through $3/8$, $7/16$, and $15/32$ are determined on all three lower-rate production subgroups. The existing rate-sixteenth staircase already established its special case. These statements concern the full numerator and allow comparison with any integer field budget. All six new Lean declarations used only the three standard axioms. [Full curves](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarPowerBound.lean)
