### Short congruences after projection onto order-sixteen blocks

At either production prime, choose one arbitrary element of every multiplicative coset of the order-sixteen subgroup inside the order-$2^{30}$ domain. Every such choice contains at least **3,132,099 pairwise disjoint supports** of balanced signed relations of weight at most fourteen. In each relation the positive and negative coefficients are all units, and no two entries come from the same block. Consequently the relations survive a projection keeping just one coordinate per block. The assertion holds for every choice of those coordinates.

Every set of $23,259,482$ anchors has more seven-subsets than there are elements of the larger field. Take two distinct seven-subsets with equal sum, remove their common points and delete the resulting support of at most fourteen anchors. Starting with $2^{26}$ anchors, the same pigeonhole argument still applies before each of the stated number of deletions. Lean checked this packing for arbitrary finite abelian groups and the literal numerical threshold. Each disjoint relation can independently be used with either sign or omitted. Thus for $0<z\le1$ the sum of $z^{\|v\|_1}$ over signed congruence vectors on these coordinates is at least $(1+2z^{14})^{3132099}$. This rules out exact factorization into the individual blocks. It gives a lower bound on uncentered weighted energy and leaves its difference from the zero-frequency contribution open. [Kernel-checked packing](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ProjectedShortRelations.lean)

### The polynomial prime-field range and the Montgomery–Vaughan–Wooley conjecture

For $n\mid p-1$ set $k=(p-1)/n$. The complete $k$th-power sum in Montgomery, Vaughan and Wooley (1995) is exactly $S(p,b)=1+k\eta_b(H)$, since every element of $H$ has $k$ preimages among the units. Their explicit conjecture in the introduction gives $|S|\le (1+o(1))\sqrt{2kp\log(kp)}$ as both $k$ and $p/k$ tend to infinity. Thus it would imply the desired big-O period scale whenever $n\le p^{1-\varepsilon}$ for fixed $\varepsilon>0$, since $p/k=n+1/k$ and $\log(kp)=O_\varepsilon(\log k)$. In the production range it identifies the proposed cancellation bound with a dyadic-image restriction, up to constants and the logarithm base, of a classical power Gauss sum conjecture. The unconditional Theorem 1 of that paper assumes $2k\nmid p-1$, whereas every even-order $H$ here has $2k\mid p-1$.

Cochrane, Hart, Pinner and Spencer's Theorem 2.1 instead bounds the error in additive representation counts using the largest period as an input. Their coverage results concern the existence of representations and do not supply that upper bound. The generalized birthday relations above concern the uncentered local representation counts. [Montgomery–Vaughan–Wooley, pp. 1–2](https://www.math.purdue.edu/~twooley/publ/1995%20GSUM.pdf) · [Cochrane–Hart–Pinner–Spencer, Theorem 2.1](https://www.math.ksu.edu/~cvs/cochrane_hart_pinner_spencer-waring_subgroup.pdf)

### The exact four-fiber pattern threshold, including all four literal MCA events

Let $H$ be any monic degree-$h$ polynomial with four complete $h$-point fibers over distinct $\alpha_0,\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3$. In each fiber $i$ prescribe an **arbitrary** set of $r_i$ sites at which all four decoders should agree. Require decoder $i$ to agree everywhere outside fiber $i$ and to miss exactly the other $h-r_i$ sites of its own fiber. At code dimension $2h$, with scalar labels $\alpha_i$, this simultaneous decoding pattern with genuine MCA failure for all four scalars exists **if and only if** $\sum_i r_i<h$. The upper bound applies to every tuple of distinct scalar labels and also forces the cross-ratio equality above. In the balanced case, the smallest simultaneous error count is $h-\lfloor(h-1)/4\rfloor$. Taking $H=X^h$ gives this result for the four interlaced index classes in any smooth coset of size $4h$.

For sufficiency, take $M$ to be the nodal polynomial of the prescribed common sites and put $v=MH^2$, $u=M(H^3-(\sum_i\alpha_i)H^2)$. At scalar $\alpha_i$, subtracting $M\prod_{j\ne i}(H-\alpha_j)$ leaves a decoder of degree less than $2h$ with exactly the specified agreements. Each witness includes at least $3h$ sites. Since $2h\le\deg v<3h$, no codeword agrees with $v$ on that witness, which proves the literal MCA event. Conversely, the two normalized check polynomials factor through proportional degree-$<h$ quotients. A nonzero quotient vanishes at every prescribed common site. If all but one decoding word were globally exact, two entire codewords would already span the received pair and preclude MCA failure. This exact result concerns the four prescribed fiber supports. It gives no bound on arbitrary witness patterns at the general prize threshold. All three endpoint declarations passed Lean with only the standard axioms. [Construction, necessity and threshold](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/FourFiberThreshold.lean)

### The dyadic fourth-energy floor forces the signed third moment

Let $H=-H$ be a multiplicative subgroup of dyadic order $n$ in characteristic greater than three. Its exact fourth-energy floor $E_2=3n(n-1)$ forces **zero ordered zero-sum triples**. Thus the signed cubic Fourier moment over all nonzero frequencies is exactly $-n^3$ for every primitive additive character. Both designated prime fields satisfy the fourth-energy hypothesis by the independent exhaustive modular computation recorded above.

To see this, put $R=\{x\in H:1-x\in H\}$. The floor gives $|R|\le2$. The map $f(x)=(1-x)^{-1}$ preserves $R$ and has $f^3(x)=x$. A fixed point satisfies $(-x)^3=1$. Since $-x\in H$ and the exponent is coprime to three, this gives $x=-1$, which would force $3=0$. Every nonempty orbit therefore has three elements, so $R$ is empty. The earlier G105 identity gives $Z_3=n|R|=0$, and additive-character orthogonality with the zero frequency removed yields the signed moment. The implication and the Fourier identity both passed the pinned Lean kernel. The calculation supplies the zero-triple premise in the depth-six decomposition; its primitive quadruple strata still require separate bounds. [Kernel proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/DyadicSidonTriple.lean)

### Squarefree retention and a genuine regular Taylor pole

The retained squarefree certificate exceeds both named production budgets at all four beyond-Johnson test radii even if its Taylor exponent is replaced by zero. Writing B for the total jet cap, its ordinary and regular components respectively cost at least B(n−A) and B(n−A)(n−D)/(A−D), independently of both retention thresholds and of the height. Every successful beyond-Johnson shifted profile with positive derivative cap needs B≥2. At the four test radii the resulting total lower bounds are respectively greater than 2.16n, 4.51n, 7.13n, and 10.38n. For the actual four certificates the regular component alone exceeds respectively 46n, 114n, 193n, and 349n. At a generally sufficient Taylor exponent its lower bound includes the factor 3D−2 in place of B. These are lower bounds on the stated numerical controller. [Squarefree profile and exact numerical comparisons](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SquarefreeRetentionFloor.lean)

A degree-D polynomial solution can itself require a denominator of unbounded order. Over F(T), take the equation (X+T)P′=DP with solution P=(X+T)^D/T^(D−1). At the coordinate origin the initial value and slope are T and D, and the separant is T. The actual rational Taylor coordinate of degree D admits a numerator in F[T] over the denominator T^τ exactly when τ≥D−1. This holds for every field of characteristic zero or greater than D, including both named primes. The example concerns a fixed-center chart with polynomial parameter coordinates. [Kernel-checked differential equation and Taylor-chart lower bound](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/RegularTaylorPole.lean)

The rational-fiber construction is now proved in Lean. At $n=k+2e$, use the monic degree-$(e+1)$ pencil $P-cB$, domain vanisher $V$, and $W=(V/B)\bmod P$. The rows $xW/P,-W/P$ have a literal bad event for each label $t$ whose other $e$ fiber roots lie in the domain. The denominator $P$ avoids the domain and is coprime to $B$. Thus three disjoint $(h+1)$-subsets with collinear monic vanishers on a smooth domain would suffice for the sharp endpoint; an arbitrary rational denominator is permitted. The companion no-joint proof uses a linear polynomial modulo $P$ and applies to the same agreement set as the fold. [Proof and subgroup criterion](rate-half-apolar-endpoint.md#rational-functions-give-a-broader-construction) · [Kernel-checked identities and event](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/RationalFiberEndpoint.lean)

The unique-decoding endpoint upper count is now **proved optimal over arbitrary evaluation domains** whenever $e+1$ does not divide $n+1$, for every $n=k+2e$ with positive $k,e$. On every prime $p>n$ splitting $e+1$, one domain and one received pair attain $(e+1)\lfloor(n+1)/(e+1)\rfloor$. The error probability on that same domain is exactly the count divided by $p$, and these examples exist over arbitrarily large primes. Each of the three claims passed Lean on the standard axioms. [Optimality theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarPowerOptimal.lean)

When $e+1$ **does** divide $n+1$, deleting one site of a union of full power fibers produces **$n-e+1$** genuinely bad labels on every such splitting prime. The deleted site supplies an additional scalar because only the other sites of its fiber are required in the domain. The deleted-site theorem and all preceding full-fiber results passed Lean with the standard axioms. The matching upper is now kernel-checked using the complete span of the locator coefficients, weak coordinate-degree interpolation, and cleared parameter descent. [Deleted-site construction](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PowerFiberEndpoint.lean) · [Divisible endpoint proof](apolar-divisible-endpoint.md)

An irreducible terminal-height quartic whose generic Galois group preserves a pairing of its four roots carries fewer than **4,000,000+150h** bad parameters ($h\le34$). The block labels give a quadratic resolvent of parameter degree at most $4h$. The square-specialization bound either limits its parameters or splits the blocks over a parameter-only quadratic extension. Applying the same Sylvester argument to the two block discriminants uses coefficient-vector heights on this parameter curve. Each genuinely split block then has coefficient pole degree at most $4h$ after one additional quadratic extension; a million witnesses on a component would force a generic polynomial secant. This leaves the primitive quartic Galois groups $A_4,S_4$ for this argument. [Complete reduction](second-prime-quadratic-discriminant.md)

### Operational line-MCA capacity law for arbitrary prime-field evaluation domains

Fix the actual $2^{-128}$ security test, and let $\delta^*$ be the supremum of its good radii in $[0,1]$. For every prime $p$, $0<k<n$, every embedding of $n$ points in $\mathbb F_p$, and every $g>0$, Lean proves the finite sandwich
$$1-k/n-g\ \le\ \delta^*\ \le\ 1-(k+1)/n$$
whenever $n\ge N_g$, $2^{128}C_g n^{d_g+1}\le p$, and $p\le(2^{128}-1)\binom{n}{k+1}$. Here $N_g,C_g,d_g$ are the repository's uniform capacity-theorem quantities. The upper bound on its own needs only the binomial field-size condition. Its proof combines actual failure at radius 1 with the equality of the MCA error throughout the last $k+1$ agreements. [Finite bounds](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldCapacityWindow.lean)

For any sequence of such embeddings with $n\to\infty$, suppose $p\ge n^d$ eventually for each fixed natural $d$ and $p\le 2^{\min(k+1,n-k-1)}$ eventually. Then $(1-k/n)-\delta^*\to0$. The upper field-size assumption can also be replaced by $p\le(2^{128}-1)\binom{n}{k+1}$ eventually. This yields $\delta^*\to1-\rho$ whenever $k/n\to\rho$. The power-of-two bound follows from the central binomial recurrence and symmetry. The same capacity-gap convergence holds with any sequence of positive interleaving widths. These are actual Lean `Tendsto` statements for the operational supremum. All eight endpoint audits use only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. The law does not determine exact finite radii outside the previously resolved cases. [Limit theorems](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldCapacityWindow.lean)

## Three degree-$(h+1)$ rational fibers on the smooth domain

Let $H=\mu_{4h}$, $d=h+1$, and $m\mid h$ with $m\ge2$. If two full degree-$d$ fibers each comprise $h/m$ full $\mu_m$ cosets and one extra site, every third fiber has at most $2h/m\le h$ sites. On each remaining coset the ratio of the two vanishers is a nonzero constant times $(X-a)/(X-b)$, where $a,b$ are the two distinct extra sites. That function takes each value at most once per coset. Lean checks the injection, the count of remaining cosets, and the strict comparison with $d$. Conversely, any two disjoint $d$-element subsets $A,B$ are full fibers of $F_A/(F_A+F_B)$ at 0 and 1, where $F_A,F_B$ are their monic vanishers. Three full fibers for an unrestricted rational pencil on the designated smooth subgroup remain open. [Coset obstruction](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothRationalFiberObstruction.lean)

### Fixed domain zeros at the first half-rate successor

The five-label whole-stack bound at $e=2^{28}+1$ now extends, by a written shortening proof, to a fixed degree-$h$ locator with fewer than $h$ distinct roots on the evaluation domain, including repeated roots. A nonzero residual after division by the domain-root polynomial forces all $h+1$ errors outside those roots. Shortening restores a pole-free rational presentation and leaves room for the single possible exceptional scalar. The case of $h$ distinct domain roots and the moving-locator cases remain open. [Fixed-domain-root reduction](apolar-successor-domain-roots.md)

The extra span and weak-coordinate steps in the divisible-case upper argument now pass the pinned Lean kernel. For an affine rectangular polynomial matrix, a minimal primitive kernel vector of degree $l$ has coordinate entries spanning all polynomials of degree at most $l$. If a linear relation on its parameter coefficients existed, scalar multiplication by its reversed relation would make the degree-$l$ coefficient vanish. The terms of smaller degree form another kernel vector because the matrix is affine. Minimality forces that vector to vanish, and primitivity at zero then kills the relation. For the actual normalized syndrome, the identity $qW_f=fW_q$ for apolar $q$ together with generic coprimality implies $f\mid q$, giving the necessary minimality. The proof applies to nonreduced locators. These statements and the weak coordinate-degree interpolation passed with only the standard three axioms. [Affine-kernel lemma](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarAffineKernelSpan.lean) · [Actual locator span](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarCoefficientSpan.lean) · [Coordinate-boundary interpolation](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarBorderCoordinates.lean)

## 2026-11-21 · No four rate-half codewords within nine errors on the 32nd roots of unity

**Result (characteristic zero, computer-assisted).** Over any field of characteristic zero, the code $\mathrm{RS}[32,16]$ evaluated on $\mu_{32}$ has at most **three** codewords in every Hamming ball of radius $9=n/4+1$. The octagon $\mathrm{RS}[8,4]$ on $\mu_8$ has four at radius $3=n/4+1$, and the earlier exhaustive check found none at radius 5 on $\mu_{16}$ over the budget-three prime. So the octagon star behind the $7/24$ list and MCA ceilings has no analogue on 16 or 32 fibers. The statement is about characteristic zero. A configuration modulo one large prime that does not lift is not excluded by this proof, and nothing here is a statement at length $2^{30}$.

**Reduction to split pencils.** Let $n=4b$, $k=2b$ and let four codewords have error sets $E_i$ of size at most $b+1$ against one word. Minimum distance $2b+1$ gives $|E_i\cap E_j|\le1$, and a difference $P_i-P_j$ with $|E_i\cup E_j|=2b+1$ is a constant times $K/L_{E_i\cup E_j}$, where $K=X^n-1$ and $L_S$ is the monic vanisher of $S$. Counting sites by their number of erring heads leaves exactly these overlap graphs on the four heads: the star (one head with $b$ errors, the other three pairwise meeting), a graph containing a triangle whose three overlaps are distinct sites, the pattern with one site where three heads err plus two further overlaps, and the 4-cycle. Two sites with three erring heads would give some pair two common errors.

* A triangle $i,j,l$ with three distinct overlap sites gives $c_{ij}L_{Q_l}+c_{jl}L_{Q_i}+c_{li}L_{Q_j}=0$ with all $c\ne0$, where $Q_i$ is $E_i$ without its two overlap sites. These are three monic, pairwise coprime polynomials of degree $b-1$ with all roots in $\mu_n$ and linearly dependent. Call this a **three-split pencil** of degree $b-1$. The star, every pattern with five or six overlaps, the triangle-plus-edge pattern, and the three-error-site patterns all contain such a triangle.
* The 4-cycle $0,1,2,3$ with private sets $Q_i$ ($b-1$ points) and overlaps $s_{01},s_{12},s_{23},s_{30}$ gives, modulo $L_{Q_0}$, that $L_{Q_3\cup s_{23}}/L_{Q_1\cup s_{12}}$ is constant on $Q_0$, and the same at every vertex.

**Finite-field census for the $\mu_{32}$ exclusion.** After normalizing the distinguished support by rotations and reflection and ordering the other two supports, the split-prime verifier finds among the filtered supports (i) no degree-seven three-split pencil candidate, (ii) 23 degree-eight candidates, all with antipodally symmetric outer supports, and (iii) 33 candidates $(Q_0,A,C)$ with a seven-point $Q_0$ and disjoint eight-point sets $A,C$ whose vanisher ratio is constant on $Q_0$. Both $A$ and $C$ are antipodally symmetric in all 33 candidates. A characteristic-zero configuration must survive these tests. The counts 23 and 33 enumerate normalized finite-field survivors. They do not count characteristic-zero lifts or quotient by the stabilizer of the distinguished support. Item (i) removes every pattern containing a triangle. In the 4-cycle, (iii) makes both $Q_1\cup\{s_{12}\}$ and $Q_1\cup\{s_{01}\}$ antipodally symmetric. $Q_1$ has seven points, so both completions must add the unique antipode of its unpaired point, forcing $s_{12}=s_{01}$. This contradiction proves the exclusion.

**How the census is made rigorous.** At a root $\beta$ of $B$ in a pencil $A-\lambda B=(1-\lambda)C$ with complement $D$ of the three root sets, $L_C(\beta)^2(1-\lambda)L_D(\beta)L_B'(\beta)\beta=n$. Hence $L_D(\beta)L_B'(\beta)\beta$ lies in one square class of $\mathbb Q(\zeta_{32})$ for all $\beta\in B$. All factors are units away from 2, so the quadratic characters modulo 16 primes $q\equiv1\pmod{32}$ must agree. This is a necessary condition on the pair $(B,D)$ alone and costs one XOR per subset: $1.2\cdot10^{11}$ pairs at degree 8 leave 3736 candidates, $2.3\cdot10^{11}$ at degree 7 leave 748, and the 4-cycle analogue leaves 4077. Each candidate is then decided exactly modulo the 61-bit split prime 1332653270109928577 by enumerating its half splits. A characteristic-zero configuration survives both steps. For the star alone there is a faster test: the three equal locator ratios multiply to a known product, so that product must be a cube, and cubic characters modulo 14 primes $q\equiv1\pmod{96}$ reject all 165288 classes of $T$ in a quarter of a second. Filter-free brute force at lengths 8 and 16 reproduces the filtered counts 19, 23, 7 and 37.

**What a star would have bought.** In the common-direction MCA construction (heads $(XHf_i(X^s),Hf_i(X^s))$, one label $-x$ per ordinary site, three labels per hole in an overlap fiber), a star on $4b$ fibers with $r\approx s/3$ locator roots and $q\approx s/6$ holes per overlap fiber balances all four heads at $(b+\tfrac12)s$ errors, i.e. first-prime ceiling $\approx(\tfrac14+\tfrac1{8b})n$. At $b=2$ this is the known $5/16$. At $b=8$ it would have been $17/64$, below the current $7/24$. That route is closed for structural (characteristic-zero) base configurations on 16 and 32 fibers. A 32-fiber four-head quotient construction therefore needs a base radius of at least 10 for some head.

**Follow-up.** In characteristic zero, the exclusion of degree-$n/4-1$ pencils for dyadic $n\ge64$ and of the 4-cycle for $n\ge32$ now has a written proof; see the entry "At most three rate-half codewords at radius $n/4+1$ on every dyadic smooth domain in characteristic zero" below. It remains open whether every degree-$n/4$ three-split pencil is an $X^2$-pullback. The finite checks above do not settle the first-prime rate-half MCA threshold or the Paley bound.

Reproduce: [star cube filter](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu32_star_cube_filter.cpp) · [pencil filter](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu32_three_split_pencils.cpp) · [4-cycle filter](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu32_four_cycle_pencils.cpp) · [exact verifier with commands and expected counts](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu32_split_pencil_verify.py) · [brute-force control](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu16_split_pencil_bruteforce.py). No Lean formalization yet.

### Exact boundary radius in an unbounded field-size interval

For every evaluation domain in a finite field of size $q$, write $N=\binom n{k+1}$ and suppose $1\le k<n$. If $\lfloor N/(k+1)\rfloor\le\lfloor q/D\rfloor$ and $q\le(D-1)N$, the $1/D$ line-MCA test passes exactly when $(1-\delta)n>k+1$. The first failing Hamming count is $n-k-1$. This follows by combining the detecting-circuit upper bound at $k+2$ agreements with the second-moment lower bound at $k+1$. There is no fixed upper bit-length requirement. Both the real-radius predicate and the exact operational supremum passed Lean on the published source. [Wide field interval](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/CircuitBudgetRadius.lean)

### The singular Taylor example admits a direct thin-field MCA count

Fix any injective evaluation domain, any received pair, and any degree-at-most-D polynomials q₀,q₁. Count the scalar labels z having an MCA witness on at least A sites whose decoding polynomial P satisfies (X+z)R′=DR for R=P−q₀−zq₁. In characteristic zero or greater than D the count is at most n−A+1+⌊n(n−1)(D+1)/((A−1)(A−2))⌋, for A≥3 and D≥2. Translation by −z reduces the differential equation to the Euler equation, so R is a scalar multiple of (X+z)^D. For a nonzero multiplier, two agreement sites away from −z eliminate that multiplier and constrain z by a nonzero polynomial of degree at most D+1. Their two distinct linear factors are coprime. A zero multiplier costs at most n−A+1 labels because, after fixing one witness, each remaining label needs a unique nonjoint site outside its support.

At n=2³⁰ and the four established radii, the respective caps are 1437633310, 1743989581, 1968032121, and 2137858210, each below the second named prime's budget 2147483648. The bound applies to witnesses on each fixed translated curve for every received pair, including on arbitrary smooth domains. The verified local MCA event bound applies to the full pair when every bad witness satisfies its equation. Proving that arbitrary bad witnesses fall into a controlled collection of curves remains open. [Actual MCA count and two-site incidence proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ShiftedPowerCurveCount.lean)

### Irreducible quartic eliminated at the last second-prime predecessor

At $n=2^{30}$, $d=2^{26}-1$ and $t=357913943$, a selected family of $2n+1$ rich labels cannot lie on a degree-four-in-$Y$ irreducible factor of the degree-34 interpolant, even after discarding 34 outliers. The entire degree-119 interpolation space bounds its weighted degree by $302954072$. The numerator of the second implicit derivative in the scalar variable bounds the sum of local multiplicities at the received lines by $1412316443$. Its identically-zero case would split the component into four affine lines over algebraic constants, each carrying at most one selected point. On the remaining possibility, intrinsic interpolation with multiplicity 64 has $40640596161682872$ more variables than scalar constraints. The resulting auxiliary of $Y$-degree at most three would furnish at least $2002579700$ roots of a nonzero scalar resultant of degree at most $268435546$. This excludes the primitive $A_4,S_4$ cases along with all other irreducible quartics. Together with the earlier low-degree-factor argument it leaves a cubic-in-$Y$ component as the necessary factor at this predecessor. The argument is written mathematics; the accompanying executable verifies the discrete estimates. [Proof](second-prime-quartic-intrinsic.md) · [Exact arithmetic](../../../scripts/probes/probe_second_prime_intrinsic_quartic.py)

### The binary entropy boundary for exponential-size fields

Suppose $n\to\infty$, $k/n\to\rho\in(0,1)$, and $(\log p)/n\to c>0$, using natural logarithms. Every sequence of arbitrary prime-field evaluation domains obeys these two conclusions for the operational $2^{-128}$ line-MCA test.

* For $c<H(\rho)$ the operational radii converge to $1-\rho$.
* For $c>H(\rho)$ the operational radius is **exactly one eventually**.

Here $H(x)=-x\log x-(1-x)\log(1-x)$. The proof squeezes $n^{-1}\log\binom{n}{k+1}$ between the binary entropy of $(k+1)/n$ and that value minus $n^{-1}\log(n+1)$. On the first side, positive exponential field growth supplies every fixed polynomial field-size lower bound in the capacity theorem, while the binomial supplies the requisite bad shell. On the second side, the detecting-circuit cap already meets the security budget at radius one. The case $c=H(\rho)$ requires finer field-size information and these conclusions leave it open. All five endpoint audits have only the standard axioms. [Entropy bounds and operational phase theorems](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/PrimeFieldEntropyPhase.lean)

### Exact unrestricted extremum at every unique-decoding endpoint

For all $k,e>0$ and $n=k+2e$, the sharp scalar numerator over arbitrary evaluation domains is now proved in Lean in **both** divisibility cases. It equals $n-e+1$ when $e+1$ divides $n+1$, and $(e+1)\lfloor(n+1)/(e+1)\rfloor$ otherwise. The bound applies to every pair on every finite-field domain. For each prime $p>n$ with $e+1\mid p-1$, one domain and one pair attain the numerator, and the same domain has exact worst-case probability equal to that count divided by $p$. Attaining primes are arbitrarily large. Four unconditional public theorems passed the pinned Lean kernel, the forbidden-token gate and the output gate with only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. The divisible case gains $e$ labels over the previous upper bound. The arbitrary-domain attaining constructions do not establish attainment on a prescribed smooth subgroup. [Complete upper and lower theorem](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/ApolarEndpointExact.lean) · [Divisible-case proof](apolar-divisible-endpoint.md)

### Intrinsic interpolation excludes the quartic and improves the field-size range

At the second designated prime and rate $1/16$, a written proof gives a first bad count of **at least 677602243**. It counts constraints on the surface containing the witnesses. Local intersection with the reverse second derivative limits the multiplicities of the received lines, and a truncated-jet interpolation on that surface gives the contradiction. The exact safe-count calculation and multiplicity sum passed the pinned Lean kernel. At the final predecessor of the three-core attack, a separate proof excludes **every irreducible factor of value degree four**, including the primitive quartic cases. A noncyclic cubic remains possible. The same method bounds the numerator by $2N$ at $5N/8$ errors on **every injective domain** over every prime $p>2N$, for all $N\ge2^{30}$ divisible by 16 and dimension $N/16$. Thus a field of size $p\ge2^{129}N$ suffices for 128-bit line MCA security at closed radius $5/8$ for that rate. This is a uniform written theorem. The exact second-prime threshold, the other prize rates, and a matching attack for the general theorem remain separate questions. [Uniform proof](second-prime-intrinsic-interpolation.md)

### Three full quintic fibers on a prescribed smooth subgroup

At length 16, a subgroup contains three disjoint fibers of size five of one degree-five rational map exactly in characteristics **3, 17 and 31**. Exhaustive cyclotomic integer arithmetic covers every characteristic through 15,981 Galois orbits. With the omitted site fixed, the three characteristics have respectively two, one and one unordered fiber partitions. Over the prime field of order 17 an explicit received pair has exactly 15 bad scalars at dimension eight and four errors. Direct interpolation on every witness set verified that count, which attains the universal upper bound on this particular smooth subgroup. The calculation classifies the rational construction at length 16. ### Exact endpoint on every prescribed multiplicative coset when the fiber size divides its length

Let $n=k+2e$ with $k,e>0$ and $e+1$ dividing $n$. For **every** prescribed multiplicative coset $D=b\mu_n$ of size $n$ in a finite field of order $q$, one received pair has bad-scalar set exactly $D$ at $e$ errors. Hence the worst-case probability is $n/q$. Each $(e+1)$-st power fiber through a site stays inside this particular coset. The same quotient pair therefore supplies all $n$ labels, and the unrestricted endpoint theorem caps every pair at $n$. For example, at length $2^{30}$ take $e=2^{28}-1$ and dimension $2^{29}+2$; the result holds on each of the two named subgroups at this dimension. [Lean proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothPowerEndpoint.lean)

## A projective list-to-MCA bound on the same Reed–Solomon code

For every injective domain of size $n$ over $\mathbb F_q$, let $1\le k<t\le n$. A list of $L$ distinct degree-$<k$ polynomials at agreement $t$ gives a single received pair with at least $\lceil L(q+1)/(q+1+(L-1)(k-1))\rceil$ bad folding scalars on the same domain, at the same dimension and Hamming step. A spike realizes evaluation at a site of the domain, the DEEP quotient realizes any external evaluation, and a degree-$k$ monomial realizes evaluation at infinity. A pair of polynomials coincides at most $k-1$ times over these $q+1$ evaluations. Cauchy–Schwarz counts the resulting labels. Gao, Yang, Xu and Kan's Reed–Solomon reduction allows changing a site. [Primary paper, Corollary 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10572) · [Proof and comparison](same-domain-list-to-mca.md)

With $B=\lfloor q/Q\rfloor$ and $B^2(k-1)<q+1$, MCA error at most $1/Q$ certifies a scalar closed list of at most $B$ at that grid point. At $Q=2^{128}$ this applies for $q\le2^{216}$ and $k\le2^{40}$, including the two originally nominated primes. Earlier off-domain estimates have enough room on those two fields as well. The new theorem also applies to a full-field evaluation domain and supplies a lower count at every list size. The transfer and its budget corollaries passed Lean with only the standard axioms. An independent con-leche replay accepted 26,177 declarations. [Quantitative theorem and tolerance corollaries](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SameDomainListMca.lean)

The integer certificate checks the one-scalar gap in the last resultant comparison. Lean checks that a nonzero polynomial relation of coordinate degree below the characteristic forces a differential constant to be algebraic over the coefficient field. ### The second rate-sixteenth threshold and 134217726 field budgets

At the second designated prime, length $2^{30}$ and rate $1/16$, the written proof gives **715827882** as the first bad error count for MCA at $2^{-128}$. The largest good grid count is **715827881**. The good real radii end strictly below $715827882/1073741824$. The same proof works on every injective domain of this length in a prime field with integer budget $2013265923\le B\le2147483648$. The first bad count is $\lfloor B/3\rfloor$ throughout this interval, at every positive interleaving width.

At least $1342177249$ selected points would lie on any cubic factor left by the quartic interpolation. When the two coordinate derivatives have small common intersection length, their degree-40 products impose few enough conditions to construct an auxiliary equation of value degree two. When that length is large, the reverse second derivative bounds the multiplicities at received lines tightly enough for a second construction of such an equation. In both cases the resultant has fewer scalar roots than the construction requires. For lower-degree factors, a noncollinear triple of large affine heads covers all selected points; otherwise their collinearity bounds the number of distinct fresh labels. For the matching attack, three affine heads share a locator on their common core. Their private-site scalars can be separated by choosing one coefficient outside at most $3n^2$ field elements.

Lean verifies the two polar dimension estimates, the budget window arithmetic, the initial degree-four interpolation and the named-prime attack. The strongest **end-to-end Lean bracket** is still $[647627102,715827882]$. The geometric good-side proof and all-domain three-head construction are written mathematics. An executable checker reconstructs the rows and checks the bad-scalar witnesses on four small instances. ## Exact MCA probability on the sixteen nonzero residues modulo seventeen

For the rate-half Reed–Solomon code on the prescribed subgroup $\mu_{16}=\mathbb F_{17}^{\times}$ at radius $4/16$, the worst-case MCA probability is exactly $15/17$. The explicit rows $xW(x)/P(x)$ and $-W(x)/P(x)$ have bad scalars $2,\ldots,16$. For $P=X^5+4X^4+14X^3+11X^2+5X+11$, $B=16X^4+2X^3+16X^2+10$, and $W=12X^4+12X^3+3X^2+10X+15$, the fibers $P/B=1,2,3$ on the domain are respectively $\{10,11,12,14,16\}$, $\{3,5,9,13,15\}$ and $\{2,4,6,7,8\}$. The kernel checks the fiber and Bézout identities, the shared row certificate $P\mid WB-(X^{16}-1)$ and the resulting literal bad events. The apolar upper bound gives equality for the worst-case probability. Both public conclusions use only the standard Lean axioms. [Formal proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/SmoothQuinticSeventeen.lean)

### A minimum degree for every component of a full production fiber correspondence

Let $F$ be an odd-degree rational pencil with three full multiplicative-subgroup fibers at $d=2^{28}-1$ or $2^{28}+1$ on either named field. Every irreducible off-diagonal component of $F(x)=F(y)$ must have degree at least **9,085,508 in each coordinate**. Thus every nontrivial right factor has degree at least 9,085,509 and every proper outer factor has degree at most 29. In the four-cycle case $d=2^{28}$, at least two adjacent vertices obey the same nontoric bound, with at most one possible degree-one reflection graph on each. Each degree-$r$ correspondence receives at least $(d+2t)r$ protected pairs from two complete fibers and $t\ge d/2$ protected third-fiber sites. The proof applies Corvaja–Zannier's two-function bound with integer parameters 8 and 2 to the normalized image under $(x,y)\mapsto(x^n,y^n)$. Large indecomposable maps remain possible. [Full proof](production-fiber-correspondence.md) · [Integer certificate](../../../scripts/probes/probe_production_ratio_lifting.py)

These restrictions also exclude solvable geometric monodromy for both odd-degree pencils and for the two nonsymmetric four-cycle vertex maps. A decomposable degree-$2^{28}+1$ pencil must have an inner map of prime degree 15,790,321 with alternating or symmetric geometric monodromy. [Group argument](production-fiber-correspondence.md#solvable-monodromy-is-excluded)

### Simultaneous ratio-lifting counterexamples at the named production primes

At $n=2^{30}$ and $h=n/4$, disjoint supports of exact degree $h-1$ or $h+1$ can have constant vanisher ratio on the entire subgroup $\mu_{262144}$ in either named field while the ratio takes distinct values at the same complex roots. With reduced degree at most $267,185,456$, a collision exists on any five million antipode-closed test sites containing three of each exponent parity. The proof combines projective evaluation counting, a finite-field polynomial estimate for forbidden intersections, and the characteristic-zero parity constraint. A full odd-degree pencil would also require at least $d+2$ translated color mismatches across its three fibers at every nonidentity shift. Explicit trace colorings satisfy all these shift inequalities simultaneously. The existence of a full prescribed $d$-site third fiber remains open. [Proof, including the complex parity argument](production-ratio-lifting.md) · [Integer checks and finite examples](../../../scripts/probes/probe_production_ratio_lifting.py)

Both hold at every positive interleaving width. ### Syndrome-flat profiles and the exceptional characteristic of the smooth octagon

For every linear code the dimensions and inclusions of intersections of its parity-check column spans determine the complete distribution of list sizes at each radius and interleaving width. A binomial inversion counts errors and a finite-flat recursion counts centers. For Reed–Solomon codes, Vandermonde locator normals give the required ranks directly from the prescribed domain. This is practical at small redundancy. On cyclic cosets the resulting syndrome construction gives an exact first-failure table for every length at redundancy three. At any even minimum distance $2e$ with $e\mid n$, the largest list at $e$ errors equals $n/e$.

At length eight and rate one half, eight words at three errors would force eight triples on eight sites with one missing partner per site. A functional on binary cubics reduces their labels to common-sum or common-product triples. Counting through each site forces all eight labels into one fiber of tripling. This rules out eight except in characteristic three, where the eight affine lines of $\mathbb F_9$ avoiding zero attain it. The common-product construction attains seven in the other admissible characteristics. On smooth domains the corresponding first bad counts for budgets from zero through $q$ appear in the proof. Lean verifies the abelian-group incidence argument. The rank probe independently checks tiny histograms, the characteristic-three incidence, and the Goldilocks octagon at widths one and two. Above characteristic $400^4$, the three-error spectrum is $\{0,1,2,3,4,5,7\}$, with exactly eight maximizing syndrome lines. ### Degree-dropping power fibers and the 263-label quadratic bound

At rate $1/16$, length $2^{30}$ and the second named prime, every irreducible quadratic divisor of the terminal interpolant carries at most **263** selected bad scalars. This sharpens the checked 330 bound. For a constant or zero fiber, use $(u,0)$. Thus the bound includes all fibers whose coordinate degree drops. If $H$ has nonzero derivative resultant and $h$ is its parameter degree, a nonempty set of square fibers has size strictly below $4h$, and a nonempty set of cube fibers has size strictly below $3h$. For the terminal discriminant the parameter degree is at most 66. A scalar where its square factor specializes identically to zero is charged to the degree of that factor. ## 2026-11-21 · At most three rate-half codewords at radius $n/4+1$ on every dyadic smooth domain in characteristic zero

**Theorem (written proof; characteristic zero only).** Let $n=2^k\ge32$ and let $F$ have characteristic zero and contain $\mu_n$. Every Hamming ball of radius $n/4+1$ contains at most **three** codewords of the rate-half Reed–Solomon code on $\mu_n$. Three is attained by the geometric-sum center. The statement includes $n=2^{30}$. For $n\ge64$ the proof uses no computation; $n=32$ uses the census in the entry on the 32nd roots of unity above. [Full proof](dyadic-radius-successor-list-three.md)

**This is not a statement about the prize prime fields.** Characteristic zero enters through the multiplicative independence of the $n/4$ numbers $1-\zeta^r$ ($r$ odd, $r<n/2$). Modulo $p$ their relation lattice has index at most $p-1$, so by Minkowski it has nonzero vectors with entries of size about $p^{4/n}$, which is below 2 at $n=2^{30}$, $p\approx2^{160}$. The argument uses one ratio equality at a time and therefore cannot be reduced modulo a production prime. For fixed $n$ it holds for all primes outside a finite, non-explicit set.

**Method.** A three-split pencil is three monic, pairwise coprime, linearly dependent polynomials of degree $m$ with all roots in $\mu_n$; equivalently a degree-$m$ rational function with three full fibers in $\mu_n$.

1. *Unit lattice.* If $L_\alpha/L_\gamma$ is constant on $\beta\subseteq\mu_n$, write each $x-s$ in the free group on the classes $1-\zeta^{\pm i}$, $i$ odd. Subtracting the coordinates $i$ and $i+n/2$ leaves, for $f^-(y)=(\mathbf1_\alpha-\mathbf1_\gamma)(y)-(\mathbf1_\alpha-\mathbf1_\gamma)(y+n/2)$, the equation $f^-(x+i)+f^-(x-i)=c_i$ for all $x\in\beta$ and odd $i$. On $\mu_{16}$ the full lattice condition has exactly the true pencils as solutions (7 in degree 3, 23 in degree 4, by brute force).
2. *Parity lemma.* If $\beta$ has three points in one parity class, then $f^-$ vanishes on the other class, and $\alpha,\gamma$ are antipodally symmetric there. The Fourier step is kernel-checked: `ProximityGap.DyadicParity.dyadic_eq_zero_of_three_centers` in [DyadicParityLemma.lean](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/DyadicParityLemma.lean) proves that an antiperiodic function on $\mathbb Z/2^{e+1}$ whose symmetrisations about three distinct centres coincide is zero, with only `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`. The cosine coefficients $2|\hat F(\xi)|\cos(2\pi\xi x/n+\theta_\xi)$ would have to agree at three distinct arguments for each odd $\xi$. Checked on every verified pencil of degree 3, 4, 6, 8 on $\mu_{32}$ (1756 instances, no failure).
3. *Theorem A.* For odd $m\ge5$ with $3(m-2)>n/2$ there is **no** three-split pencil of degree $m$ on $\mu_n$ in characteristic zero. Odd root sets cannot be symmetric, so each sits in one parity class up to two points; a parity count forces all three into the same class, which is too small.
4. *4-cycle.* The only overlap pattern of four close codewords without a triangle is excluded for $n\ge32$ by the parity lemma and a case analysis on which class holds the four overlap sites.

**Relation to "Three degree-$(h+1)$ rational fibers on the smooth domain" above.** That entry leaves open three full fibers of an unrestricted degree-$(h+1)$ pencil on $\mu_{4h}$. Theorem A with $m=h+1$ (odd, and $3(h-1)>2h$) shows there are none in characteristic zero for every dyadic $h\ge4$. This agrees with the length-16 classification (characteristics 3, 17 and 31 only) and with the census: no degree-5 pencil on $\mu_{16}$, none of degree 5, 7, 9 or 10 on $\mu_{32}$. It says nothing at the named primes.

**Census of three-split pencils on $\mu_{32}$**, verified survivors by degree 2 through 10, with the first root set normalized by rotation and reflection: 2401, 101, 449, 0, 7, 0, 23, 0, 0. Survivors that are not invariant under $x\mapsto-x$ occur only in degrees 2, 3, 4 (2296, 101, 164). The degree-6 and degree-8 lists are exactly the $X\mapsto X^2$ pullbacks of the 7 and 23 on $\mu_{16}$. Degrees 9 and 10 have no candidates even before exact verification.

**What a prime-field version would need.** All $n/4-1$ equalities $L_A(x)/L_C(x)=\text{const}$ must be used jointly, for example through coefficient or power-sum identities with small integer coefficients, to which the existing cyclotomic-norm lifts at $p\ge2^{128}$ apply. The pencil relation is linear in the three coefficient vectors but carries an unknown field scalar $\lambda$; that is the present obstruction to such a lift.

Background on linear relations among roots of unity of prime-power order (all generated by antipodal pairs when the order is a power of two): [Lam and Leung, arXiv:math/9511209](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/9511209), local copy `research/lam-leung-vanishing-sums-9511209.pdf`. The multiplicative independence used here is the separate classical statement in Washington's book.

Reproduce from `scripts/probes`: `python3 -O probe_dyadic_pencil_parity_lemma.py lattice`, `... lemma 32 FILE`, `... regulator`. Census commands are in [the verifier](../../../scripts/probes/probe_mu32_split_pencil_verify.py). Lean covers the parity lemma only; the unit-lattice input, Theorem A and the 4-cycle analysis are written proofs.

## 2026-11-21 · Exact budget-three rate-half list threshold at length 32 over a 130-bit prime field

**Result (prime field, computer-assisted, no Lean).** Let $p=(3\cdot2^{48}+69)2^{80}+1=1020847100762898806271677231708359360513$, whose $2^{-128}$ list budget is $\lfloor p/2^{128}\rfloor=3$. For the rate-half Reed–Solomon code on $\mu_{32}\subset\mathbb F_p$, every Hamming ball of radius **9** contains at most three codewords, and an explicit ball of radius **10** contains four. So the last good error count is 9, the first bad one is 10, and the good real radii are exactly $\delta<10/32$. The scalar-to-interleaved projection for budgets with $\binom{B+1}{2}<q$ (entry "Scalar list budgets and a rate-half exact slice") carries this to every positive interleaving width. This extends the earlier length-16 budget-three threshold at the same prime. It is a fixed-length instance at length 32, not a production-length statement.

**Why the characteristic-zero proof was not enough, and what replaced it.** The dyadic theorem above this entry is about characteristic zero. Its lift to a prime needs that prime to exceed a norm of size about $2^{262}$ already at length 32, and at production length a single-ideal divisibility can never suffice: one would need vanishing at about $n/4$ conjugate roots. So this entry is a direct exhaustive search modulo $p$. The overlap-pattern reduction (triangle gives a degree-7 three-split pencil; 4-cycle gives $L_A/L_C$ constant on a 7-set $Q_0$) is characteristic-free.

**Search.** The 2-Sylow subgroup of $\mathbb F_p^\times$ has order $2^{80}$. Let $\ell(x)\in\mathbb Z/2^{80}$ be the discrete logarithm of $x^{(p-1)/2^{80}}$. It is additive, so $L_A(x)/L_C(x)$ constant on $T$ implies $\sum_{a\in A}d_j(a)=\sum_{c\in C}d_j(c)$ with $d_j(r)=\ell(t_j-r)-\ell(t_0-r)$, for every $t_j\in T$. This turns the search into sorting sums of 80-bit integers. A table self-test confirms additivity and exactness on 200 random products.

* Degree-7 pencils: for all 52819 normalized 7-sets $T$ and all pairs of disjoint 7-subsets of the complement, **no** pair passes. Even the one-role condition has no solution, so there is no three-split pencil of degree 7 on $\mu_{32}$ over $\mathbb F_p$.
* 4-cycle: for all 52819 normalized $Q_0$ and all pairs of disjoint 8-subsets, exactly **33** pairs pass the fingerprints, all 33 hold exactly in $\mathbb F_p$, and in all 33 both sets are invariant under $x\mapsto-x$. They are the same 33 as in the characteristic-zero census. As there, $Q_1\cup\{s_{12}\}$ and $Q_1\cup\{s_{01}\}$ would both be antipodally symmetric with $|Q_1|=7$, forcing $s_{12}=s_{01}$.
* Radius 10: the octagon lift with $s=4$ (heads $V(X)f_i(X^4)$, $V$ vanishing at one point over each of the exponents 2, 3, 5) gives four distinct polynomials of degree at most 15 with error counts $8,10,10,10$ against one word, checked by direct evaluation modulo $p$.

At length 16 the same program returns 7, 23 and 37, the characteristic-zero counts. Normalization uses rotation and inversion, which preserve constancy of a ratio of equal-degree vanishers.

Reproduce (from `scripts/probes`, about 20 minutes on 10 cores):
`python3 probe_mu32_modp_fingerprint_table.py 1020847100762898806271677231708359360513 32 tab.txt`; `c++ -O3 -std=c++17 probe_mu32_modp_ratio_search.cpp -o modp`; `./modp tab.txt 7 7 10` (expect `hits=0`); `./modp tab.txt 7 8 10 > cyc.txt` (expect `hits=33`); `python3 probe_mu32_modp_ratio_verify.py 1020847100762898806271677231708359360513 32 cycle cyc.txt` (33 exact, all symmetric); `python3 probe_mu32_radius_ten_four_list.py 1020847100762898806271677231708359360513` (error counts `[8, 10, 10, 10]`, four distinct codewords).

**Scope.** One prime, length 32, rate one half, list budget three. The method is exhaustive in the length and does not scale to length 64. The production-length list and MCA thresholds at the named primes and the Paley bound remain open.

### First-prime half rate one error beyond the unique-decoding boundary

At length $n=2^{30}$ and rate one half, every injective domain and every positive interleaving width has list size at most four through **286331153 errors**. If four distinct polynomial heads each have at least 787410671 joint agreements, every selected bad-scalar decoder in that range is on one of them. The proof rules out five agreement sets using their integer incidence multiplicities. At 268435457 errors four such determined heads bound the entire bad-scalar count by $n+9$. A pairwise core overlap cap of $k-2$ reduces this to $n+3$. Lean also shows that the current weighted Johnson exception formula exceeds the first-prime budget for every admissible choice of its parameters.

A written refinement shows that an over-budget four-head family here would have primitive direction of degree exactly one, exactly one uncovered position and all four core sizes equal to 805306366. The remaining three multiplicity profiles pass every individual integer overlap bound and force a nonconstant polynomial cross-ratio of degree at most two. Excluding or realizing them on the actual subgroup and bounding families without four moderately rich heads remain open. The certified first-prime half-rate MCA interval stays $[268435457,313174699]$ in first-failure error counts. ### A proper quartic carries at most 268,435,546 rich scalar labels at the final predecessor

Lean proves the full proper-intersection branch at $n=2^{30}$, $d=2^{26}-1$ and $t=357913943$. The input is a trivariate polynomial $F$ of scalar/value total degree at most 34, with value degree four and coordinate/value weight at most 302954072. Over $k(Z)[X]$ its coefficients in the value variable must be primitive. Over $k(Z)(X)$ it must be irreducible and must fail to divide its actual scalar Hessian. On any $n$ distinct coordinates with any two received rows, **at most 268435546 different scalars** can select a degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial lying on $F$ and agreeing with the received combination on at least $t$ sites. This gives a strict contradiction for a quartic carrying the $2n+1-34$ labels forced by a putative all-quartic factorization.

The proof uses the established bound $R=\sum r_x\le1412316443$ for the actual received-site multiplicities. At multiplicity $\mu=64$, the truncated plane jet has $2080$ monomials. Multiplication by $F$ on the monomials of degree below $64-r_x$ has a triangular minor whose determinant is a power of the first nonzero coefficient of $F$ at the site. Give each independent column its own scalar-polynomial multiplier of degree at most $2^{26}-34$. The resulting identities hold at every scalar, including scalars where that minor specializes to zero. The original auxiliary window has **6121893077231948920 coefficients**. Even the coarse upper bound $64R(2^{26}+1-34)+34\cdot2080 n$ for the net number of conditions is only **6065925886497513792**. Thus a nonzero auxiliary of value degree at most three and total scalar/value degree at most $2^{26}$ exists. Substitution along each selected polynomial gives a root of multiplicity at least 64 at every agreement. The coordinate degree is below $64t$, so every such section vanishes on the auxiliary.

The nonzero value resultant can be padded to degrees four and three. After swapping the two coefficient-indeterminates in the polynomial ring, its scalar degree is at most $3\cdot34+4\cdot2^{26}-4\cdot3=268435546$. Every selected scalar kills the whole resultant, including when a specialized degree drops. The kernel checks both the auxiliary construction and the label cap with only the three standard axioms. The theorem treats the proper quartic branch. Applying it to the full MCA bad event also needs the original interpolant's factor hypotheses and the other factor branches. ### Exact first five coset moments still allow a near-linear coordinate

At $n=2^{30}$ and every $p=nk+1$ with $k\ge2^{128}$, there is a list of exactly $k$ real numbers of absolute value at most $n$ whose first five power sums are
$-1,\ p-n,\ -n^2,\ 3p(n-1)-n^3,\ -n^4$.
These simultaneously match the actual coset identities when the third and fifth returns vanish and the fourth energy is minimal. The list contains $1/2700-n$. Thus those scalar constraints alone cannot imply the logarithmic period bound in the 159-bit band. The entries in this constructed list are real numbers with unit multiplicity when expanded. The construction does not realize them as conjugates in the cyclotomic period field. The proof specifies 348,480 entries by integer multiplicities, matches the remaining even power sums with two groups of opposite pairs, and pads to the exact index. Four distinct degree-$d$ polynomial heads on $n>2d$ sites can match the three roles prescribed by a four-valued rational function of height $r$ on at most $r+d$ sites. The function may have unequal fibers and must have no poles on the domain. The proof uses two degree-$r+d$ polynomial identities and the root bound for a head difference. At the first-prime length, a four-head over-budget instance in the three surviving occupancy profiles would have at least $n-8$ triple sites. Every four-valued classifier of height at most $n/4$, including the partition into four subgroup cosets, would get at least **268435450** omitted heads wrong. ### The first half-rate successor at the first prime is equivalent to a single punctured four-word list

Using the written four-head extraction, over-budget MCA at $268435457$ errors is equivalent to a four-word scalar list at the same error count on $H\setminus\{1\}$ in dimension $2^{29}-1$. Every such list yields at least $n+2$ actual bad scalars by an explicit two-row construction. Only three error patterns are possible, and each contains a triangle with three distinct overlaps. Dividing the triangle differences by their common vanisher forces **three full fibers of degree $2^{28}-1$**. The four-cycle alternative from the unpunctured list problem is unnecessary here. The named-field existence of this punctured list remains open. [Proof and endpoint hypotheses](first-prime-punctured-four-list.md)

Equivalently, one rational function $A/B$ must meet four affine functions on full fibers of $h+1$ distinct punctured-domain roots each. The locator minor argument proves this criterion in both directions, with $\deg A\le h+1$ and $\deg B\le h$. The explicit $\mathbb F_{17}$ example passes an exact polynomial and agreement check. ### The pencil scalar is cyclotomic: explicit small congruences behind every three-fiber pencil

This corrects one sentence of "At most three rate-half codewords at radius $n/4+1$ ..." above, which named the unknown field scalar as the obstruction to a prime-field lift. The scalar is not unknown. Let $A=uB+vC$ be monic of degree $d$ with root sets $\alpha,\beta,\gamma\subseteq\mu_n$ and put $\varepsilon_S=\prod_{s\in S}s\in\mu_n$. Comparing leading and constant coefficients gives $u+v=1$ and $\varepsilon_\alpha=u\varepsilon_\beta+v\varepsilon_\gamma$, so unless the three products coincide,
$$u=\frac{\varepsilon_\alpha-\varepsilon_\gamma}{\varepsilon_\beta-\varepsilon_\gamma}.$$
Every coefficient identity then becomes a congruence modulo $\mathfrak p$ for an explicit element of $\mathbb Z[\zeta_n]$. The two outermost ones are linear in the indicator vectors, with $p_{\pm1}(S)=\sum_{s\in S}s^{\pm1}$:
$$(\varepsilon_\beta-\varepsilon_\gamma)\big(p_1(\alpha)-p_1(\gamma)\big)=(\varepsilon_\alpha-\varepsilon_\gamma)\big(p_1(\beta)-p_1(\gamma)\big),$$
$$(\varepsilon_\beta-\varepsilon_\gamma)\big(\varepsilon_\alpha p_{-1}(\alpha)-\varepsilon_\gamma p_{-1}(\gamma)\big)=(\varepsilon_\alpha-\varepsilon_\gamma)\big(\varepsilon_\beta p_{-1}(\beta)-\varepsilon_\gamma p_{-1}(\gamma)\big).$$
Each side is a sum of at most $4d$ roots of unity with signs. Both identities were checked numerically on three true pencils on $\mu_{16}$. The same holds at each vertex of a 4-cycle, with $u$ replaced by the ratio of the two constants in (1.1) of the proof note.

**What this does and does not give.** A production pencil over a named prime forces two signed sums of at most $n$ roots of unity in $\mu_n$ to vanish modulo $p$, and $d-3$ further sums of rapidly growing length. The obstruction to lifting is therefore exactly the single-ideal one: each sum lies in one prime ideal above $p$, and its norm is bounded only by about $(4d)^{n/2}$, far above $p$. The cyclotomic-norm lift used elsewhere needs the same integer polynomial to vanish at about $n/4$ powers $g^r$; a pencil at $g$ gives no pencil at $g^r$. A joint argument would have to bound the ideal generated by several of these sums, and no such bound is known here. This is a necessary-condition filter for searches and a precise statement of the remaining gap. It proves nothing at production length.

### Which primes carry three-fiber pencils that do not exist in characteristic zero: complete data at length 16

For every prime $p\equiv1\pmod{16}$ below 30000 (399 primes) the exact number of solutions on $\mu_{16}\subset\mathbb F_p$ was computed for four problems: three-split pencils of degree 3, 4 and 5 (first root set normalized by rotation and reflection), and the 4-cycle vertex condition ($L_A/L_C$ constant on a 3-set, $|A|=|C|=4$). The characteristic-zero counts are 7, 23, 0, 37. The counts differ from these exactly at the 16 primes

17, 97, 113, 193, 241, 257, 337, 353, 401, 449, 577, 673, 769, 977, 1201, 2593,

and at every one of the other 383 primes, including all 372 primes between 2593 and 29921, the counts equal the characteristic-zero ones. Degree-5 pencils occur only at $p=17$, where $\mu_{16}$ is all of $\mathbb F_p^\times$; this matches the classification "characteristics 3, 17 and 31" in the entry on three full quintic fibers (3 and 31 are not $\equiv1\pmod{16}$). The largest surplus above $p=17$ is at 97: counts 18, 27, 0, 154.

Method: for these small primes the full discrete logarithm modulo $p-1$ is an exact additive key, so equality of vanisher ratios is decided by integer sums with no verification step. The program is the search used for the 130-bit prime with the modulus changed.

**Reading.** At length 16 a non-liftable solution needs a prime below about $n^{2.9}$. A count of configurations against $p^{-(d-1)}$ predicts that such primes stay below a bound that does not grow with $n$ once the degree is about $n/4$; a length-32 run for degree 7 is in progress. This is evidence that the production statement (no degree-$(2^{28}\pm1)$ pencil over the named primes) is true. It is not a proof, and no effective bound on the exceptional primes is known beyond the norm bound of size $\exp(O(n\log n))$.

Reproduce: `python3 scripts/probes/probe_small_prime_dlog_table.py P 16 t.txt`, then `probe_small_prime_pencil_census.cpp` compiled with `c++ -O3 -std=c++17` and run as `./a.out t.txt 3 3 2`, `4 4 2`, `5 5 2`, `3 4 2`; the last number printed is the count.

On the full order-sixteen subgroup of the field of seventeen elements, the dimension-eight code has exact worst-case MCA probability **1 at five errors**, following its previously checked value **15/17 at four errors**. Four explicit degree-six heads agree with a punctured word on ten of fifteen sites each. The common direction $(1,X-1)$ puts its pole at the unused coordinate. Over separably closed differential coefficients, take any primitive value cubic with nonzero value-partial and scalar-Hessian resultants. 3\sum_{x\in D}r_x+2\deg\gcd(A,B)\le 2|D|+4\deg(\operatorname{lc}_Y F)+\deg\operatorname{Res}_{3,7}(F,H).

### Raw ordinary polars in one coordinate chart

An irreducible cubic in the original coordinate, scalar and ordinate variables carrying at least 1,342,177,249 polynomial sections now gives a single integral chart for all but at most 161 labels. This removes the separate leading-coefficient calculation at coordinate infinity. All three modules passed the pinned kernel with only the standard axioms. ### Removing the value content preserves every rich section

For any field, any injective evaluation domain and any nonzero first-order interpolant of weight strictly below the number $t$ of agreements, every original rich section lies on its value-primitive part. Writing the interpolant as $Q=VP$, with content $V(X,Z)$, choose a nonzero coefficient $g(X)$ of $V$. At each agreeing site either $g$ vanishes or $P$ vanishes on the received line. The specialization $g(X)P(X,z,q_z(X))$ has at least $t$ roots and degree below $t$, since removing $V$ saves at least $\deg g$ in weight. This eliminates the parameter exceptions for the original second-prime quartic and the degree-ten interpolant. At rate $1/16$ all 112,920,886 budgets **1220000050** through **1332920935** have their first failure between $\lfloor B/2\rfloor-24$ and $\lfloor B/2\rfloor$, on every injective length-$2^{30}$ prime-field domain at every positive width. ### One coordinate multiplier preserves every bad scalar as dimension rises

Let the two dimensions satisfy $1\le k\le K$, and let the minimum support size be $t>K$. On the same injective evaluation domain over any finite field, the worst line-MCA probability in dimension $k$ is at most the one in dimension $K$. For a single dimension step use the $q+1$ multipliers $1$ and $X+c$. On each bad witness choose a row that does not fit in dimension $k$. If two multipliers carried that row into dimension $k+1$ on the witness, both $v$ and $Xv$ would fit. Their interpolants $P,Q$ satisfy $XP=Q$ on at least $k+2$ sites, hence identically, forcing $v$ into dimension $k$. There is at most one forbidden multiplier per bad scalar and at most $q$ scalars, so a single multiplier works for the whole received pair. Iteration gives the dimension comparison. [Lean transport proof](../../../ArkLib/Research/ProximityGap/Frontier/DimensionRaiseMCA.lean) · [Background on componentwise products](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.0022)

For $n=2^{30}$, $1565873494\le B=\lfloor p/2^{128}\rfloor\le2147483648$, let $e=\max(671088640,\lfloor B/3\rfloor)$. At every dimension $2\le K\le2^{26}$ satisfying $3e+2K\ge2n$, the first bad count is $e$ on every injective domain. Every positive interleaving width has precisely the safe real radii $0\le\delta<e/n$. Three exceptional heads give the endpoint attack in dimensions at least three. The degree-one construction handles dimension two. At the second named prime this covers all **67,108,863** dimensions from 2 through 67,108,864, including the gap above the separate low-dimension three-line theorem. The general classification and the literal named theorem passed the Lean kernel with its three standard axioms.
